# Project 1: Ludlul bēl nēmeqi ("I will praise the lord of wisdom"), Tablets I–V

Wisdom corpus, project 1 (`../corpus.md`). Read `../../translation-guidelines.md`, `../corpus.md`, `../renderings.md`, `../translator-brief.md`, then this file.

## Scope
The whole poem as eBL edits it: **534 line labels** – I 120, II 120, III 121 (1–110, `a+1`, `a+2`, `b+1`, 113–120), IV 53 (`1`, `a+1`–`a+30`, `b+1`, `c+1`–`c+17`, `d+1`–`d+10`; most lines are fragments), V 120. Five annotated files, one per tablet, drafted by sequential translators: I, II, III, IV + V (one run), each with its own `translation--intro-<T>.rtl.md`.

## Base edition
- **Rank 1:** eBL `L/2/2`, *Righteous Sufferer (Ludlul bēl nēmeqi)*, ed. Aino Hätinen, rev. Z. J. Földi, A. C. Heinrich, E. Jiménez, T. D. N. Mitto: `../../sources/eBL/Ludlul-SB-<T>/reading-edition.md` (N = normalization, T = Foster's translation, L = lemmas, notes, parallels, the ATF of every manuscript); `glossary.md` for the stems.
- **Rank 3–4:** Foster (the eBL T line); **Lenzi 2023** (`../../sources/Lenzi-2023--Suffering-in-Babylon--OBO-300/full-text.txt`: ch. 2 text and translation from l. 3443; ch. 3 notes from l. 4793; ch. 6 the body and disease vocabulary; ch. 5 the omens; ch. 8 the prayer language; ch. 9 the ancient commentary; ch. 11 II 71–85) – read **after** your own reading of each line. The ancient commentary K 3291: `../../sources/CCP/1.3--P394923--Ludlul-commentary/` and eBL's `NinNAQuo2` lines.
- **Line numbering:** eBL's labels are canonical. Lenzi follows Oshima 2014; for I, II, V they mostly agree with eBL, for III and IV they differ (eBL notes such as "Previous line numbers as …" give the older numbers). Every translator of III and IV adds a concordance table (eBL ↔ Lenzi/Oshima ↔ Lambert BWL where known) to their section.

## Why this text (guidelines §1, both purposes)
- **Bible:** Job (the sufferer's god-forsakenness, social death, body, the unknowable divine will, restoration); Psalms of lament and thanksgiving (Ps 22: "my god … far … does not answer", the encircling enemies, the bones; Ps 30: anger for a moment, life in his favour, "you raised me from the pit"; Ps 38, 41 (the sickbed), 88 (the grave, the friends removed), 31:12–14 (scorn of neighbours, `דִּבַּת רַבִּים`), 102); Isa 53 (despised, `וִידוּעַ חֹלִי`, "we esteemed him stricken"); Lam 3; Qohelet (II 39–47: the reversals of fortune); Deut 28 (the diseases), 18:10–11 (the diviners), 32:39; 1 Sam 2:6–8.
- **The ancient mind:** personal religion (the personal god and goddess, *šēdu* and *lamassu* who leave a man), the Kassite court and the courtier's fall, omens and dreams, the failure and the vindication of the exorcist, the demon list and the body, the incantation-prayer form of the whole poem (Lenzi ch. 8), the procession through Esagil's gates and its ritual acts (V), the sufferer as a historical man (Šubši-mešrê-Šakkan, under Nazi-Maruttaš, 1307–1282 BCE).

## Files
`annotated-I.rtl.md` … `annotated-V.rtl.md`; `translation--intro-<T>.rtl.md` (title + Hebrew introduction per tablet, written by its translator); `translation--head.rtl.md` (the poem's general introduction, coordinator); `translation-<T>.rtl.md` and `translation.rtl.md` (generated: `python3 -I ../wisdom_tools.py build`).

## Open decisions for the user
Each tablet section below lists its own (with the alternatives); the coordinator's selection of the ones that matter most:
1. *šiptu* "incantation" = `קֶסֶם` (III; `קסם` is the Bible's word for divination, Deut 18:10) – or `חֶבֶר` / `לַחַשׁ` (*tû* already has `לחש`). Coordinator's recommendation: change to `חֶבֶר` (Deut 18:11 `וְחֹבֵר חָבֶר`, Ps 58:6), which is an incantation word; tagged [equivalent].
2. *šūlu* in II 53 / III 71: the demon `שׁוּלוּ` (as now, so that the reversal is visible) or eBL's lemma "cough" in III 71.
3. *muštālu* = `מָתוּן` (title line); *aḫulap* = `רַב!`; *parṣu* = `פולחן` (I).
4. *wabālu* = `הביא` / `הוביל`, `נשא` only for *našû* (III): a deviation from the Gilgameš corpus (`הוביל` = *warû* there).
5. *maṣḫatu* = `קמח־מַצְחַתּוּ` (II), avoiding `מנחה` (Gen 4); *daddaru* = `לענה` (II 88), which creates the Lam 3:15 contact by itself; `יתרון` (II 26), Qohelet's word.
6. *aḫu* "arm" and *idu* "arm" both `זרוע` (I).
7. The gate method of V 41–53; `הֵן!` as the refrain particle (V 31–33); *ṭātu* = `שׁוֹחַד` (V 56).
8. Line-numbering: eBL's; concordances with Lenzi/Oshima/Lambert in the Scope of III, IV, V.

## Progress log
- 2026-10-19: base texts fetched (eBL), project set up by the coordinator.
- 2026-10-19: Tablets I, II, III, IV+V drafted by four sequential translators (runs 1–4), three review passes each; renderings merged after each run (`../renderings.md` §10.1–§10.4); coordinator change III a+1 `ובשורשו` → `ובבסיסו` (`שורש` kept for *šuršu*, V 97). `python3 -I ../wisdom_tools.py verify I II III IV V --strict`: 534 entries, 0 problems (2 justified repeated-line differences, III 35/38 ≠ 55); `check_bible_quotes.py`: 0. **Status: complete (first draft).**

(Each translator appends `## Tablet <T>` below, with the subsections of `../translator-brief.md` §7.)

## Tablet I

### Scope
Ludlul I, all **120 eBL lines** (1–120), in `annotated-I.rtl.md`; intro `translation--intro-I.rtl.md`; generated `translation-I.rtl.md`. The tablet is almost wholly preserved: only 27 (one illegible word) and 29–30 (*ap[kallu]*, *i[na ilī]*) have restorations. 41 manuscripts (Nineveh, Aššur, Kalḫu, Ḫuzirina, Sippar, Babylon, Kiš; many school tablets), the ancient commentary K 3291 (NinNACom1, explains I 24, 26, 47, 48, 61, 69, 71, 78, 86, 87, 93, 105, 106), quotations (NinNAQuo1 colophon of Assurbanipal; NinNAQuo2 the commentary's title; NinNAQuo3 a catalogue; BabLBQuo1–2; UrkLBQuo1, an Uruk *šumma izbu* commentary quoting I 76–77 "as said in Ludlul"). eBL numbering = Lenzi/Oshima for this tablet. Sections (our headings): 1–4 proem; 5–12, 13–28, 29–36 the hymn; 37–40 the speaker's vow; 41–54 gods depart, omens; 55–72 king and courtiers; 73–83 the courtier humbled; 84–104 social death and loss of property and offices; 105–120 lament and hope.

### Sources consulted
eBL `reading-edition.md` (N, T = Foster, L, notes, ATF) and `glossary.md` (stems: *karāṭu* N "to be merciful", *ênu*, *ṣabāru* G/Gt, *nagāru* D, *maṭāṭu*, *kâšu*/*kiāšu*, *salātu*, *duppuru*, *šuqammumu*, *mūtawû*); Lenzi 2023 ch. 2 (translation), ch. 3 (all Tablet I notes), ch. 5 (5.2.2, the revelations of I 41–54), ch. 8 (8.2.1.1–2, the hymn and self-presentation); CCP 1.3 (K 3291 obv. 1′–23′); the renderings of this corpus, Gilgameš (§1–§21), and spot checks in the creation corpus (Enūma eliš V 67: *pelludû* = `חוקות`, *parṣu* = `פולחנים`) and the law corpus (LH prologue *muštālum* = `המתון`). Every Bible quotation printed from `תנך-מנוקד/` and checked.

### Decisions
- **Title line I 1 (= I 3 with Marduk):** `אֲהַלֵּל אֶת אֲדוֹן הַחָכְמָה, אֵל מָתוּן,` (since the 2026-10-23 harmonization `אֲפָאֵר אֶת אֲדוֹן הַחָכְמָה, אֵל מָתוּן,`: *dalālu* = `פֵּאֵר`) / `מַרְדּוּךְ, אדון החכמה, אֵל מתון,`. *ila muštāla* = `אֵל מָתוּן` (vocalized `אֵל`): *muštālu* (Gt of *šâlu*) "who deliberates with himself, judicious, considerate"; `מתון` ("הֱווּ מְתוּנִים בַּדִּין") is exactly that, and it is already the law corpus's rendering of the same epithet in the LH prologue (*muštālum* = `המתון`). Rejected: `השוקל` (weighing; also *šaqālu*), `המתחשב` (modern), `השואל` (reserved for *šāʾilu*), `הנמלך` (*malāku*).
- **Refrains:** I 2 = 4 `זועם בלילה, מתפייס ביום:` (fixed); I 9 / 11 `אשר כובד ידיו – לא יישאו השמים;` / `מרדוך, אשר כובד ידיו – לא יישאו השמים;`; I 10 / 12 `כפו רכה – סועד את המת.` / `רכה כפו – סועד את המת.` (the Akkadian word order flips: *rittuššu rabbat* / *rabbat rittašu*); I 29 / 31 `האדון ה[חכם] – כל אשר בלב האלים בוחן,` / `מרדוך החכם – כל אשר בלב האלים בוחן,`. I 30 and 32 differ in the Akkadian (*alaktašu ul īde* / *ul ilammad ṭēnšu*).
- **Anger lemmas (one Hebrew word each):** *ezēzu* `זעם` (stat. `זועם`); *uzzu* `זעם`; *uggatu* `חמה` (5, 37); *libbātu* `עברה`; *rūbu* `חרון`; *šabāsu* `כעס`, *šabbasû* `כועס`; *nakbatu* `כובד` (cognate root of *kabātu*); *ennettu* `עונש` and *enēnu* II "punish" `ענש` (41); *akṣu* `חמור` (vocalized); *gaṣṣu* `אכזר`; *nekelmû* `הזעיף פנים` (15, 82); *nadāru* N `השתולל`, *nanduru* `משתולל`; *nalbubu* `זועף`. (*kamālu*, *agāgu*, *šamru* do not occur in I.)
- **Mercy words:** *rēmēnû* `רחום`; *gamālu* `חמל`, *gāmilu* `חומל`; *karāṭu* N "be merciful" `נִכְמָר` (18); *enēnu* I "grant favour" `חנן` [cognate] (38; the pun with *enēnu* II "punish" 41 is noted); *kâšu*/*kiāšu* D "help, rescue" `סעד` (10, 12, 97; eBL's lemma for 10/12 is *kâšu* "delay", its translation "rescue" – we follow Lenzi/Moran and say so); *bulluṭu* `החיה`; *nêšu* Š `קיים (חיים)` (34, kept apart from *bulluṭu*); *pašāḫu* `נרגע` / Š `הרגיע`; *salāmu* D `השלים את`.
- ***damqu* family:** *damiqtu* as a noun = `חסד` (93 `לאומר את חסדי`, 119 `יישר חסדי`); *damqu* adjective = `נאה` (40 `מחשבתו הנאה`, vocalized); *šēd dumqi* `רוח־המגן של חסד` (45). `טוב` only for *ṭābu* (6, 88), *ṭiābu* D `היטיב` (117), *ṭābtu* `טובות` (118).
- **Personal religion:** *ilu* `אֵל` / `אֵלִי` (43) / `אֵלוֹ` (16); *ištaru* `אֵלָה` / `אֵלָתִי` (44); *ilu u ištaru* `האל והאלה` (28); *šēdu* `רוח־מגן`, *lamassu* `אלת־מגן` (15, 45, 46, 64, 97); I 43 *nadû* = `השליך` (not *ezēbu*; tagged against Ps 22:2 and Ps 71:9); *šadâšu īli* `עלה אל הרו`; *naparkû* `חדל` (44; also II 15); *bêšu* `זז`; ***aḫītu*** `הלאה` (44; also II 105 *itaddâ aḫīta*); *salātu* `ניתק` (45); *parādu* `נבהל` and *pirittu* `בהלה` (one root); *idu* "side" in *ša idīya* / *ālik idi* = `לצד` (45, 98; *idu* "arm" stays `זרוע`).
- **Self and dignity (47–48):** *bāštu* `הדר` (as Gilg. XI 258, 267 `שמלת הדרו`); *dūtu* `און` (vocalized `אוֹנִי`); *wamālu* Dt `הועם` (Lam 4:1); *simtu* (a person's quality) `צביון` (the construct *simat* "befitting" stays `הראוי ל־`); *tarānu* `מחסה`; *eṭēru* N `נחלץ` (the renderings' `חלץ`, here "was pulled off"); *parāsu* `חתך` (48 "cut off"; 52 "decided", as Dan 9:24 `נֶחְתַּךְ`).
- **Divination:** *ittu* `אות`; *têrtu* omen `הוראה` (51), office `משרה` (60); *dalāḫu* `דלוח` [cognate Ezek 32:2]; *nuppuḫu* `מנופח`; *bārû* `הבוחן`, *šāʾilu* `השואל`; *alaktu* `הליכה`; *egerrû* `קול־המקרה`; *šuttu* `חלום`.
- **Court and courtiers:** *nanzāzu* `העומדים לפניו`; *taslītu* `רכילות` (kept apart from *ṭapultu* `דיבה`); *nadānu* Štn `נדברים`; *nulliātu* `נבלות`; *tuššu* `עלילה`; *napraku* `מחסום` (69, 118); *qīptu* `פקודה` (61, ↔ Ps 109:8); *tamāḫu* `תפש` (the commentary glosses it *ṣabātu*, which keeps `אחז`); *kaṣāru* + *riksu* `קשרו קשר` (65; *libbu ikkaṣir* 56 `לבו נקשר`); *illatu* `חבורה`; **the numerical sequence 59–64** `האחד … השני … השלישי … הרביעי … החמישי … החמישים … השישי והשביעי` and *sebet* `שבעה` (65); *nabalkutu* Š `הֵסֵב` (63); *appatu* `מתג`; *ešēʾu* `בלם`; *mutellu* `נעלה`; *šagimmatu* `שאגה`; *šapû* `עבה`; *kabāru* `כביר` [cognate]; *agašgû* `זאטוט`; *etellu* `נגיד`; *rēšu* "servant" (78) `משרת` / "head" (94) `ראש`; *ṣabāru* G "blink" `מצמץ` (81), Gt "speak" `מלל` (71).
- **Social death:** *kimtu* `משפחה`; *aḫu* `אח`, *aḫû* `זר` (84, 104); *ibru* `רֵעַ`, *tappû* `חבר`, *kinattu* `בן־גיל`, *rūʾu* `עמית`; *gallû* `גַּלּוּ`; *nagāru* D `הלשין`; *karāṣu* D `קרץ` [cognate; Dan 3:8]; *ardu* `עבד`, *amtu* `אמה`; *mūdû* (acquaintance) `מכיר`; *ḫaštu* `שחת`; *mūtawû* `השָּׂח`; *rēṣu* `עוזר`; ***aḫulap*** `רַב!` (vocalized; ↔ 2 Sam 24:16 `רַב עַתָּה`; binds III 35, 38, 55); *ālik idi* `הולך לצד`; *ṣiddu u birtu* `אספסוף וריקים`; *zâzu* D `חילק`; *mimmû* `כל אשר לי`; *maṭāṭu* `מוטט` [cognate]; *sakīku* `טין`; *qerbetu* `נאות`; *alālu* `הֵידָד`; *šuqammumu* `הֵדַם`; ***parṣu*** `פולחן` (pl. `פולחנים`) and ***pelludû*** `חוקות` – the creation corpus's pair (Enūma eliš V 67), and the Theodicy 135 pair; *leqû* Š `הביאו אחר לקחת` (103).
- **Body and lament (105–118):** *anāḫu* Št `היאנחות`; *gerrānu* `מספד` (2026-10-23 harmonization: `תַּאֲנִיָּה`); *qitayyulu* `תמהון` (the commentary's *qūlu*; ↔ Deut 28:28); *idirtu* `צרה`; *warḫu* `ירח` (106, 120); *kīma summi adammum* `כיונה קוננתי` (fixed); *zammāru* `זמר` [cognate]; *qubbû* `קינה`; *ṣarāpu* II Š `הצריח`, *ṣarāpu* I `צרב`; *ḫamšiš* `חמישית`; *usukku* `לסת`; *ṣalāmu* `שחר`; *adirtu* `קדרות`; *arāqu* D `הוריק` [cognate]; *unūtu* `כלים` (*unât libbi* `כלי לבי`); *gitallutu* `פלצות`; *râbu* `רעד`; *enû* Dt `התחלף`; *nablu* `שלהבת`; *ḫamāṭu* III Št `התלהט`; *teslītu* `תחנון`, *supû* `תחינה`; *ṣāltu* `ריב`, *puḫpuḫḫû* `קטטה`; *ašṭu* `קשה`; *nāpalû* `מענה`; *tuša* `כאילו` (83) / `אוּלַי` (119, vocalized).
- **Other:** *rušumtu* `רפש` (`טיט` reserved); *ḫissatu* `מחשבה`; *tabālu* `סילק`; *mānītu* `רוח־קלה` (*šāru* alone `רוח`); *ūmu* II `סערה`; *genû* `נגח`; *edēdu* `התחדד`; *ṣimdu* "bandage" `תחבושת` (Job 5:18, Hos 6:1); *namtaru* `נַמְתָּר`; *utukku* `אוּטוּכּוּ`; *tû* `לחש`; *mâdu* ŠD `הרבה`; *ênu* `עוין` (14, `?`); *karašû* `אסון`; *maqtu* `הנופל`; *tebû* Š `הקים` (14 and 60 – the pun is noted); *ešertu* `*מקדש*` (24, uncertain; Foster "justice", Lenzi "offering"); *eʾiltu* `חוב`; *paṭāru* `פטר`; *daʾīmu* `*רומח*`; *šūpîš* `בגלוי`.
- Parenthesized words of the normalization ((u), (ša), (ana), (ina)) are not translated (13, 20, 85, 97, 100, 104), following the Gilgameš convention.

### Open decisions for the user
1. *ila muštāla* = `אֵל מָתוּן` (as LH *muštālum* `המתון`); alternatives `השוקל`, `המתחשב`.
2. *parṣu* = `פולחן` instead of the renderings' `פֶּלַח (note)`: Hebrew `פֶּלַח` reads "slice"; `פולחן` is the creation corpus's word (Enūma eliš V 67) and Gilgameš OB Schøyen₂ 18's. Coordinator to confirm and correct §2.
3. *adirtu* (I 111) = `קדרות` ("gloominess", eBL gloss; Gilgameš *adāru* I `התקדר`), against renderings §2, which pairs *adāru* / *adirtu* with `חרד` / `חרדה` (fear, *adāru* II). Proposal: split by lemma.
4. *aḫu* II "arm" (I 76) = `זרועות`, the same Hebrew as *idu* "arm" (Gilgameš; Ludlul II 77, 97). No good second word was found (`אגף`, `אמה`, `כנף` all mislead); alternatively render *idu* "arm" in II as `זרוע` and *aḫu* by a paraphrase. Coordinator's choice.
5. *kabāru* = `כביר` [cognate], although Gilgameš uses `כביר` for *gašru* (which does not occur in this corpus). Alternative `איתן`.
6. *ênu* (I 14) = `עוֹיְנוֹ?` (cognate, 1 Sam 18:9), a negative-sounding verb in a merciful line; alternative: follow Lenzi's *inūšu* "then" (`אז`).
7. *eʾiltu u annu … uptaṭṭarū* (I 24): `ביום *מקדשו*` follows eBL's lemma (*ešertu*); Foster "justice" (*išartu*), Lenzi/Oshima "offering". If "justice", it would be `יושר` (reserved for the *išaru* family, allowed).
8. *rēšu* (I 78) = `משרת` by sense (and the commentary's gloss *ardu*), losing the pun with I 94 `שׂוּם לראש`; alternative: keep `ראש` in both with a note.
9. The accidental Hebrew pun `לְרָע … רֵעִי` (I 85: *lemnu* / *ibru*) – keep (both renderings are fixed) or change *lemnu* there to `רשע`-free `לרעה`?

### Parallels index (I)
| Line | Akkadian | Hebrew rendering | Bible | Tag | Comment |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| I 1 | *ludlul bēl nēmeqi* | אפאר את אדון החכמה | `אֲרוֹמִמְךָ יְהוָה כִּי דִלִּיתָנִי` (תהלים ל:ב) | equivalent | The thanksgiving opens with the praise verb; *ludlul* is the vow that closes Akkadian incantation prayers (Lenzi ch. 8). |
| I 1 | *ila muštāla* | אֵל מָתוּן | `אֵל רַחוּם וְחַנּוּן אֶרֶךְ אַפַּיִם` (שמות לד:ו) | divergence | A deliberating, slow god; but Ludlul's point is that his anger lasts one night. |
| I 2 | *eziz mūšī muppašir urrī* | זועם בלילה, מתפייס ביום | `כִּי רֶגַע בְּאַפּוֹ חַיִּים בִּרְצוֹנוֹ בָּעֶרֶב יָלִין בֶּכִי וְלַבֹּקֶר רִנָּה` (תהלים ל:ו) | equivalent | The closest parallel of the tablet: short anger, night to morning, in a thanksgiving for one raised from the pit. In the psalm weeping lodges with man; here night and day are the god's moods. |
| I 3 | *marduk* | מרדוך | `מְרֹדָךְ` (ירמיהו נ:ב); `כָּרַע בֵּל קֹרֵס נְבוֹ` (ישעיהו מו:א) | cognate | The name; *bēlu* "the Lord" (41) = Bēl. |
| I 5 | *kīma ūmi mehê … uggassu* | כסערה של סופה – … חמתו | `הִנֵּה סַעֲרַת יְהוָה חֵמָה יָצְאָה וְסַעַר מִתְחוֹלֵל` (ירמיהו כג:יט) | equivalent | Storm and *ḥēmâ* together; also Jer 30:23, Nah 1:3. |
| I 6 | *mānīt šēreti* | כרוח־קלה של בוקר | `מִתְהַלֵּךְ בַּגָּן לְרוּחַ הַיּוֹם` (בראשית ג:ח) | equivalent | The god in the gentle breeze; cf. 1 Kgs 19:12 `קוֹל דְּמָמָה דַקָּה` after storm and fire. |
| I 7 | *uzzuššu lā maḫār … rūbšu* | בזעמו – אין עומד מולו … חרונו | `לִפְנֵי זַעְמוֹ מִי יַעֲמוֹד וּמִי יָקוּם בַּחֲרוֹן אַפּוֹ` (נחום א:ו) | equivalent | *uzzu* = `זעם`, *rūbu* = `חרון`, *lā maḫār* = "who can stand": the same three elements in one verse. |
| I 7 | *abūbu rūbšu* | מבול – חרונו | `יְהוָה לַמַּבּוּל יָשָׁב` (תהלים כט:י) | equivalent | The flood as the image of divine anger. |
| I 8 | *kabattašu tayyārat* | כבדו שבה | `שׁוּב מֵחֲרוֹן אַפֶּךָ וְהִנָּחֵם` (שמות לב:יב) | equivalent | *târu* = `שוב`, the god turning from his *ḥārôn* (7); also Jonah 3:9. |
| I 8 | *mussaḫḫir karassu* | חוזרים מעיו | `עַל כֵּן הָמוּ מֵעַי לוֹ רַחֵם אֲרַחֲמֶנּוּ` (ירמיהו לא:יט) | equivalent | Mercy located in the god's innards; also Hos 11:8 `נֶהְפַּךְ עָלַי לִבִּי`. |
| I 9 | *nakbat qātīšu* | כובד ידיו | `כִּי יוֹמָם וָלַיְלָה תִּכְבַּד עָלַי יָדֶךָ` (תהלים לב:ד) | cognate | *nakbatu* < *kabātu* = כבד; also Job 23:2, 1 Sam 5:6. |
| I 10 | *rittuššu rabbat ukašša mīta* | כפו רכה – סועד את המת | `יְהוָה יִסְעָדֶנּוּ עַל עֶרֶשׂ דְּוָי` (תהלים מא:ד) | equivalent | "Rescues the dying" follows Foster/Lenzi; eBL's lemma (*kâšu* "delay") would mean "delays the dead". |
| I 13 | *uptattâ qabrātu* | נפתחו קברים | `הִנֵּה אֲנִי פֹתֵחַ אֶת קִבְרוֹתֵיכֶם` (יחזקאל לז:יב) | divergence | Same collocation, opposite direction (graves opened to receive / to release). *qabru* = קבר [cognate]. |
| I 13 | *ina libbātīšu* | בעברתו | `בְּעֶבְרַת יְהוָה צְבָאוֹת נֶעְתַּם אָרֶץ` (ישעיהו ט:יח) | equivalent | |
| I 14 | *ušatbi maqta* | הקים את הנופל | `מְקִימִי מֵעָפָר דָּל` (תהלים קיג:ז); `סוֹמֵךְ יְהוָה לְכָל-הַנֹּפְלִים` (תהלים קמה:יד); `מֵקִים מֵעָפָר דָּל` (שמואל א ב:ח) | equivalent | *tebû* Š = `הקים`, *maqtu* = `הנופל` (the destitute, Lenzi). |
| I 14 | *īnuššu* | עוֹיְנוֹ? | `עוֹיֵן אֶת דָּוִד` (שמואל א יח:ט) | cognate | eBL's reading (*ênu* "to look askance"); Lenzi reads *inūšu* "then". |
| I 15 | *inessû lamassu u šēdu* | ומתרחקות אלת־המגן ורוח־המגן | `אַל-תִּרְחַק מִמֶּנִּי` (תהלים כב:יב) | equivalent | *nesû* = `התרחק`; the protective spirits have no biblical counterpart (absent); the nearest is Ps 91:11 `כִּי מַלְאָכָיו יְצַוֶּה-לָּךְ`. |
| I 16 | *ana ša iskipūšu ilšu isaḫḫuršu* | ואל אשר דחהו אלו, חוזר אליו | `כִּי לֹא יִזְנַח לְעוֹלָם אֲדֹנָי` (איכה ג:לא) | equivalent | Rejection and return (Lam 3:31–32; Ps 77:8); in Ludlul the high god looks and the *personal* god returns. |
| I 17 | *akṣat ana surri ennettašu* | חמורה לרגע – ענשו | `בְּרֶגַע קָטֹן עֲזַבְתִּיךְ וּבְרַחֲמִים גְּדֹלִים אֲקַבְּצֵךְ` (ישעיהו נד:ז) | equivalent | Punishment of a moment, then mercy (18); Ps 30:6 `כִּי רֶגַע בְּאַפּוֹ`. |
| I 18 | *itâr ālittuš* | נהפך ליולדת | `כַּיּוֹלֵדָה אֶפְעֶה` (ישעיהו מב:יד) | equivalent | The strongest: in Isa 42:13–14 the god `כַּגִּבּוֹר` becomes `כַּיּוֹלֵדָה`, as Marduk the warrior (42) becomes a mother, the bull a cow (19–20). |
| I 18 | *ālittu* | יולדת | `צוּר יְלָדְךָ תֶּשִׁי וַתִּשְׁכַּח אֵל מְחֹלְלֶךָ` (דברים לב:יח) | equivalent | The Torah's god who gave birth. Divergence: Num 11:12 `הֶאָנֹכִי הָרִיתִי … אִם אָנֹכִי יְלִדְתִּיהוּ`. Also Isa 49:15, 66:13. |
| I 18 | *ikkarriṭ* (N "be merciful") | נכמר | `כִּי נִכְמְרוּ רַחֲמָיו אֶל אָחִיו` (בראשית מג:ל) | equivalent | The Hebrew verb of the moment of mercy; 1 Kgs 3:26 of a mother; Hos 11:8. Foster: "draws up". |
| I 19 | *rīmāniš uganna* | כראם מנגח | `וְקַרְנֵי רְאֵם קַרְנָיו בָּהֶם עַמִּים יְנַגַּח` (דברים לג:יז) | equivalent | Wild ox + butting in one verse; the god as wild ox: Num 23:22 `כְּתוֹעֲפֹת רְאֵם לוֹ`. *rīmu* = ראם [cognate]. Rests on the new reading *rīmāniš uganna* (Oshima; Piccin & Worthington). |
| I 20 | *kī araḫ būri ittanasḫara arkīšu* | כפרה (עם) עגל – חוזר וחוזר אחריו | `הָלְכוּ הָלֹךְ וְגָעוֹ` (שמואל א ו:יב) | equivalent | The cow attentive to her calf; Ps 103:13 `כְּרַחֵם אָב עַל-בָּנִים`. |
| I 21 | *zaqtā niṭâtūšu usaḫḫalā zumra* | עוקצות מכותיו – דוקרות את הגו | `כִּי חִצֵּי שַׁדַּי עִמָּדִי` (איוב ו:ד) | equivalent | The god's blows piercing the body; Job 16:13; Isa 53:4 `מֻכֵּה אֱלֹהִים`. |
| I 21–22 | *niṭâtu … ṣindūšu uballaṭū* | מכותיו … תחבושותיו מחיות | `כִּי הוּא יַכְאִיב וְיֶחְבָּשׁ יִמְחַץ וְיָדָו תִּרְפֶּינָה` (איוב ה:יח) | equivalent | Strong: blow and bandage from one hand. *ṣimdu* "bandage" = `תחבושת`. |
| I 21–22 | same | same | `כִּי הוּא טָרָף וְיִרְפָּאֵנוּ יַךְ וְיַחְבְּשֵׁנוּ` (הושע ו:א) | equivalent | Same pair. |
| I 22 | *uballaṭū namtara* | מחיות את הנמתר | `אֲנִי אָמִית וַאֲחַיֶּה מָחַצְתִּי וַאֲנִי אֶרְפָּא` (דברים לב:לט) | equivalent | *bulluṭu* = `החיה`; also 1 Sam 2:6. *namtaru* is the death demon (absent in the Bible as a demon). |
| I 23 | *gillata ušrašši* | ומקנה (לאדם) פשע | `וַיָּסֶת אֶת דָּוִד בָּהֶם` (שמואל ב כד:א) | equivalent | The god who makes man incur sin; also Job 9:20 `פִּי יַרְשִׁיעֵנִי`, Isa 45:7. |
| I 24 | *ina ūm išertīšu uptaṭṭarū eʾiltu u annu* | ביום *מקדשו* – נפטרו חוב ועוון | `כִּי בַיּוֹם הַזֶּה יְכַפֵּר עֲלֵיכֶם` (ויקרא טז:ל) | equivalent | A day on which guilt is released; the key noun is disputed (shrine / justice / offering). *paṭāru* = פטר [cognate]; *arnu* = עוון. |
| I 25 | *utukka rāʾiba ušarši* | אוטוכו (ו)רעד הקנה | `לְפָנָיו יֵלֶךְ דָּבֶר וְיֵצֵא רֶשֶׁף לְרַגְלָיו` (חבקוק ג:ה) | equivalent | Demons and plagues sent by the god; Job 4:14 `פַּחַד קְרָאַנִי וּרְעָדָה`. |
| I 26 | *ina têšu* | בלחשו | `צָקוּן לַחַשׁ מוּסָרְךָ לָמוֹ` (ישעיהו כו:טז) | equivalent | *tû* = `לחש`, the exorcist's word (*mašmaššu* `מלחש`); Ps 58:6. |
| I 27 | *mušmiddi … adad miḫiṣti erra* | מרבה את ה… של אדד, את מחץ ארה | `אַסְפֶּה עָלֵימוֹ רָעוֹת חִצַּי אֲכַלֶּה בָּם` (דברים לב:כג) | equivalent | The god heaping blows; the verb is uncertain (multiply / diminish). |
| I 28 | *musallim ili u ištari* | משלים את האל ואת האלה | `וְאִם-לֹא תַשְׁלִים עִמָּךְ` (דברים כ:יב) | cognate | *salāmu* = שלם; the personal god and goddess are absent in the Bible. |
| I 29 | *mimma libbi ilī ibarri* | כל אשר בלב האלים בוחן | `אֲנִי יְהוָה חֹקֵר לֵב בֹּחֵן כְּלָיוֹת` (ירמיהו יז:י) | divergence | *barû* = `בחן`; in the Bible God tests human hearts, here Marduk tests the gods'. Ps 7:10. |
| I 30 | *manāma ina ilī alaktašu ul īde* | איש מ[ן האלים] את הליכתו לא ידע | `מִי תִכֵּן אֶת רוּחַ יְהוָה וְאִישׁ עֲצָתוֹ יוֹדִיעֶנּוּ` (ישעיהו מ:יג) | equivalent | The unknowable way; Isa 55:8; Job 26:14; *ina ilī* restored. |
| I 32 | *ul ilammad ṭēnšu* | לא ילמד את דעתו | `וְהַחָכְמָה מֵאַיִן תִּמָּצֵא` (איוב כח:יב) | equivalent | |
| I 33 | *kabtat qāssu libbašu rēmēnī* | כבדה ידו – לבו רחום | `אֵל רַחוּם וְחַנּוּן` (שמות לד:ו) | cognate | *rēmēnû* = רחום. |
| I 34 | *gaṣṣu kakkašu* | אכזר נשקו | `תֵּהָפֵךְ לְאַכְזָר לִי בְּעֹצֶם יָדְךָ תִשְׂטְמֵנִי` (איוב ל:כא) | equivalent | Job calls God `אכזר`; Ludlul pairs it with life-giving inner parts. |
| I 35 | *ša lā libbīšu mannu* | שלא מלבו – מי | `כִּי לֹא מִלִּבִּי` (במדבר טז:כח) | equivalent | Same Hebrew idiom for "not of my will"; Job 9:12 `מִי יְשִׁיבֶנּוּ`. |
| I 36 | *ayyu lišālil qāssu* | איזהו ישמיט את ידו | `וְלָא אִיתַי דִּי יְמַחֵא בִידֵהּ` (דניאל ד:לב) | equivalent | No one can stay his hand; Isa 43:13. |
| I 37 | *kīma nūni ākulu rušumta* | כדג אכלתי רפש | `וְעָפָר תֹּאכַל כָּל יְמֵי חַיֶּיךָ` (בראשית ג:יד) | equivalent | Eating mud/dust as the lowest state of a creature (Lenzi: the bottom-feeding carp); Mic 7:17 `יְלַחֲכוּ עָפָר כַּנָּחָשׁ`. *akālu* = אכל [cognate]. |
| I 37 | same | same | `כִּי-אֵפֶר כַּלֶּחֶם אָכָלְתִּי` (תהלים קב:י) | equivalent | The lament psalm of the sick man (Ps 102). |
| I 38 | *īnunam-ma … kī uballiṭu mītūta* | חננני מיד – כמו החיה (מ)מיתה | `שְׁמַע-יְהוָה וְחָנֵּנִי` (תהלים ל:יא) | cognate | *enēnu* I = חנן; in Ps 30 also `חִיִּיתַנִי` (30:4). |
| I 39 | *lušalmid nišī qitruba gamālšin* | אלמד את האנשים: קרובה החמלה | `אֲלַמְּדָה פֹשְׁעִים דְּרָכֶיךָ` (תהלים נא:טו) | equivalent | The vow to teach others after deliverance; *lamādu* Š = למד [cognate]; Isa 56:1 `כִּי קְרוֹבָה יְשׁוּעָתִי`. |
| I 41 | *bēlu īninanni* | האדון ענשני | `אֲנִי הַגֶּבֶר רָאָה עֳנִי בְּשֵׁבֶט עֶבְרָתוֹ` (איכה ג:א) | equivalent | First-person lament of the punished man. |
| I 42 | *isbusu ittīya* | כעס עליי | `וַיַּחַר עָלַי אַפּוֹ וַיַּחְשְׁבֵנִי לוֹ כְצָרָיו` (איוב יט:יא) | equivalent | |
| I 43 | *iddânni ilī* | השליכני אֵלִי | `אֵלִי אֵלִי לָמָה עֲזַבְתָּנִי` (תהלים כב:ב) | equivalent | "My god" exactly, but the verb is *nadû* "cast off", not *ezēbu* (= עזב [cognate]). |
| I 43 | *iddânni* (*nadû*) | השליכני | `אַל-תַּשְׁלִיכֵנִי לְעֵת זִקְנָה כִּכְלוֹת כֹּחִי אַל-תַּעַזְבֵנִי` (תהלים עא:ט) | equivalent | Both verbs in one parallelism; Ps 51:13, 102:11. |
| I 43 | *šadâšu īli* | עלה אל הרו | — | absent | The personal god "goes up to his mountain" (disappears); no biblical counterpart. |
| I 44 | *ipparku ištarī* | חדלה אלתי | `חָדְלוּ קְרוֹבָי וּמְיֻדָּעַי שְׁכֵחוּנִי` (איוב יט:יד) | equivalent | *naparkû* = `חדל`; the personal goddess is absent in the Bible. |
| I 47 | *inneṭir bāltī* | נחלץ הדרי | `כְּבוֹדִי מֵעָלַי הִפְשִׁיט` (איוב יט:ט) | equivalent | Dignity stripped; Job 30:15 `תִּרְדֹּף כָּרוּחַ נְדִבָתִי`. *eṭēru* = `חלץ` in both senses (rescue / pull off). |
| I 47 | *dūtī ūtammil* | אוני הועם | `אֵיכָה יוּעַם זָהָב` (איכה ד:א) | equivalent | *wamālu* Dt = `הועם`; *dūtu* = `און` (Gen 49:3 `וְרֵאשִׁית אוֹנִי`). |
| I 49 | *iššaknānim-ma idāt piritti* | ונשמו לי אותות בהלה | `וְהָיוּ בְךָ לְאוֹת וּלְמוֹפֵת` (דברים כח:מו) | cognate | *ittu* = אות; the curses as "signs" in a man; Job 3:25 `כִּי פַחַד פָּחַדְתִּי וַיֶּאֱתָיֵנִי`. |
| I 50 | *uštēṣi bītuʾa kamâti arpud* | הוצאתי מביתי – בחוץ שוטטתי | `הֵן גֵּרַשְׁתָּ אֹתִי הַיּוֹם מֵעַל פְּנֵי הָאֲדָמָה` (בראשית ד:יד) | equivalent | Expulsion and wandering as punishment; *rapādu* is not נד (Gen 4:12). |
| I 50 | *kamâti* | בחוץ | `בָּדָד יֵשֵׁב מִחוּץ לַמַּחֲנֶה מוֹשָׁבוֹ` (ויקרא יג:מו) | equivalent | The sick man's place outside. |
| I 51 | *dalḫā têrētūʾa* | דלוחות הוראותיי | `וַתִּדְלַח מַיִם בְּרַגְלֶיךָ` (יחזקאל לב:ב) | cognate | *dalāḫu* = דלח; omens "muddied". |
| I 52 | *itti bārî u šāʾili* | עם הבוחן והשואל | `וְשֹׁאֵל אוֹב וְיִדְּעֹנִי וְדֹרֵשׁ אֶל הַמֵּתִים` (דברים יח:יא) | cognate | *šāʾilu* = שׁאל; the professions Deut 18:10–11 forbids. |
| I 52 | *alaktī ul parsat* | לא נחתכה הליכתי | `וַיִּשְׁאַל שָׁאוּל בַּיהוָה וְלֹא עָנָהוּ יְהוָה גַּם בַּחֲלֹמוֹת גַּם בָּאוּרִים גַּם בַּנְּבִיאִם` (שמואל א כח:ו) | equivalent | All channels of inquiry silent. |
| I 53 | *ina pî sūqi lemun egerrûʾa* | בפי הרחוב – רע קול־המקרה שלי | `וְהָאֲנָשִׁים יְנַחֲשׁוּ וַיְמַהֲרוּ וַיַּחְלְטוּ הֲמִמֶּנּוּ` (מלכים א כ:לג) | equivalent | A chance utterance taken as an omen; Gen 24:14. |
| I 54 | *šuttī pardat* | חלומי מבהיל | `וְחִתַּתַּנִי בַחֲלֹמוֹת וּמֵחֶזְיֹנוֹת תְּבַעֲתַנִּי` (איוב ז:יד) | equivalent | The sufferer's terrifying dreams; Dan 4:2 `יְבַהֲלֻנַּנִי`; Deut 28:66 `וּפָחַדְתָּ לַיְלָה וְיוֹמָם`. |
| I 55 | *šarru šīr ilī šamšu ša nišīšu* | המלך – בשר האלים, שמש אנשיו | `וּכְאוֹר בֹּקֶר יִזְרַח שָׁמֶשׁ` (שמואל ב כג:ד) | equivalent | The just king as sunrise; "flesh of the gods" is absent in the Bible. |
| I 56 | *libbuš ikkaṣir* | לבו נקשר | `וְנַפְשׁוֹ קְשׁוּרָה בְנַפְשׁוֹ` (בראשית מד:ל) | divergence | A "knotted" heart is angry in Akkadian, loving in Hebrew. |
| I 57 | *nanzāzū … uštanaddanū elīya* | העומדים לפניו … נדברים עליי | `גַּם יָשְׁבוּ שָׂרִים בִּי נִדְבָּרוּ` (תהלים קיט:כג) | equivalent | Courtiers conferring against the speaker. |
| I 57 | *taslīta* | רכילות | `לֹא תֵלֵךְ רָכִיל בְּעַמֶּיךָ` (ויקרא יט:טז) | equivalent | |
| I 57–58 | *paḫrū-ma … elīya* | התקבצו … עליי | `כִּי שָׁמַעְתִּי דִּבַּת רַבִּים מָגוֹר מִסָּבִיב בְּהִוָּסְדָם יַחַד עָלַי` (תהלים לא:יד) | equivalent | |
| I 59 | *napištašu ušatbakšu* | את נפשו אצוק | `תַּחַת אֲשֶׁר הֶעֱרָה לַמָּוֶת נַפְשׁוֹ` (ישעיהו נג:יב) | equivalent | Life "poured out"; Lam 2:12. |
| I 60–61 | *ušatbi têrtuš; qīptašu atammaḫ* | הקמתיו ממשרתו; את פקודתו אתפוש | `פְּקֻדָּתוֹ יִקַּח אַחֵר` (תהלים קט:ח) | equivalent | His office seized by another (and 103). |
| I 63 | *pî ḫanšē* | פי החמישים | `וַיִּשְׁלַח אֵלָיו שַׂר חֲמִשִּׁים וַחֲמִשָּׁיו` (מלכים ב א:ט) | equivalent | "Fifty" as a body of men; in Ludlul a number of "everyone" (and perhaps Marduk's fifty names). |
| I 65 | *ikṣurūnim-ma rikis* | קשרו (עליי) קשר | `וַיִּקְשֹׁר עָלָיו שַׁלֻּם בֶּן יָבֵשׁ` (מלכים ב טו:י) | equivalent | *kaṣāru riksa* = `קשר קשר`, the Hebrew idiom of conspiracy; the seven recall the seven demons. |
| I 66 | *lā pādû* | אינם פודים | `בְּרָעָב פָּדְךָ מִמָּוֶת` (איוב ה:כ) | cognate | *padû* "spare, release" = פדה. |
| I 67 | *ištēn šīršunu* | ובשר אחד להם | `וְהָיוּ לְבָשָׂר אֶחָד` (בראשית ב:כד) | equivalent | "One flesh" of conspirators. |
| I 68 | *nanḫuzū išātiš* | ניצתו כאש | `חֲמָתוֹ נִתְּכָה כָאֵשׁ` (נחום א:ו) | equivalent | |
| I 70 | *appatiš ītešʾū* | כבמתג בלמו | `בְּמֶתֶג-וָרֶסֶן עֶדְיוֹ לִבְלוֹם` (תהלים לב:ט) | equivalent | Ps 39:2 `אֶשְׁמְרָה לְפִי מַחְסוֹם`. |
| I 71 | *ḫašikkiš ēmi* | כחרש נהייתי | `וַאֲנִי כְחֵרֵשׁ לֹא אֶשְׁמָע וּכְאִלֵּם לֹא יִפְתַּח-פִּיו` (תהלים לח:יד) | equivalent | Strong: the sick man among enemies becomes as a deaf-mute; the ancient commentary glosses *ewû* "become" as *mašālu* "be like". Isa 53:7 `וְלֹא יִפְתַּח פִּיו`. |
| I 72 | *šagimmatī* | שאגתי | `רָחוֹק מִישׁוּעָתִי דִּבְרֵי שַׁאֲגָתִי` (תהלים כב:ב) | equivalent | Job 3:24 `וַיִּתְּכוּ כַמַּיִם שַׁאֲגֹתָי`. |
| I 73 | *šaqâtu rēšāya iknušā qaqqaršun* | ראשי הגבוה – נכנע ארצה | `נַעֲוֵיתִי שַׁחֹתִי עַד-מְאֹד` (תהלים לח:ז) | equivalent | |
| I 74 | *libbī kabbara* | לבי הכביר | `הֶן-אֵל כַּבִּיר וְלֹא יִמְאָס` (איוב לו:ה) | cognate | *kabāru* = כביר. |
| I 75 | *agašgû ittēʾ* | זאטוט הפנה לאחור | `יִרְהֲבוּ הַנַּעַר בַּזָּקֵן וְהַנִּקְלֶה בַּנִּכְבָּד` (ישעיהו ג:ה) | equivalent | The young humiliating the great; Job 30:1 `שָׂחֲקוּ עָלַי צְעִירִים מִמֶּנִּי`. |
| I 77 | *ša etelliš attallaku* | אשר כנגיד התהלכתי | `כְּמוֹ-נָגִיד אֲקָרְבֶנּוּ` (איוב לא:לז) | equivalent | Self-image as a prince before the fall; Job 29:7. |
| I 78 | *atūr ana rēši* | ונהפכתי למשרת | `הוּא יִהְיֶה לְרֹאשׁ וְאַתָּה תִּהְיֶה לְזָנָב` (דברים כח:מד) | equivalent | *rēšu* "head" = servant here (commentary: *ardu*) and "head" in 94; Deut 28:13, 44 the curse of reversal; Prov 30:22. |
| I 79 | *ēteme ēdāniš* | הייתי לבודד | `שָׁקַדְתִּי וָאֶהְיֶה כְּצִפּוֹר בּוֹדֵד עַל-גָּג` (תהלים קב:ח) | equivalent | Lev 13:46 `בָּדָד יֵשֵׁב`; divergence from Gen 2:18 `לְבַדּוֹ`. |
| I 80 | *turruṣā ubānātu* | שלוחות האצבעות | `שְׁלַח אֶצְבַּע וְדַבֶּר אָוֶן` (ישעיהו נח:ט) | equivalent | Pointing as a gesture of contempt/curse; Prov 6:13. |
| I 81 | *iṣabburā īnātu* | מצמצות העיניים | `שֹׂנְאַי חִנָּם יִקְרְצוּ-עָיִן` (תהלים לה:יט) | equivalent | Prov 10:10. |
| I 82 | *ālī kī ayyābi nekelmânni* | עירי – כצר הזעיפה אליי פנים | `וַיַּחְשְׁבֵנִי לוֹ כְצָרָיו` (איוב יט:יא) | equivalent | In Job God counts him a foe; here his city. Job 13:24. |
| I 83 | *nakratu … mātī* | נכרייה … ארצי | `נָכְרִי הָיִיתִי בְעֵינֵיהֶם` (איוב יט:טו) | cognate | *nakru* = נכר. |
| I 84 | *ana aḫî aḫī itūra* | לזר – אחי נהפך | `אַחַי מֵעָלַי הִרְחִיק וְיֹדְעַי אַךְ-זָרוּ מִמֶּנִּי` (איוב יט:יג) | equivalent | Strong: brother and stranger; *aḫû*/*aḫu* pun. |
| I 84 | same | same | `מוּזָר הָיִיתִי לְאֶחָי וְנָכְרִי לִבְנֵי אִמִּי` (תהלים סט:ט) | equivalent | Strong: `זר` + `נכרי` = I 84 + 83. Also Jer 12:6. |
| I 85 | *ana lemni u gallê itūra ibrī* | לרע – לגלו נהפך רעי | `וְזֶה-אָהַבְתִּי נֶהְפְּכוּ-בִי` (איוב יט:יט) | equivalent | The friend turned (`נהפך`) into an enemy; Lam 1:2; Ps 55:13–14. |
| I 86 | *tappû unaggaranni* | חברי … מלשין עליי | `מְלָשְׁנִי בַסֵּתֶר רֵעֵהוּ` (תהלים קא:ה) | equivalent | Jer 20:10 `הַגִּידוּ וְנַגִּידֶנּוּ כֹּל אֱנוֹשׁ שְׁלֹמִי`. |
| I 87 | *ana naq dāmi* | לנסוך דם | `בַּל-אַסִּיךְ נִסְכֵּיהֶם מִדָּם` (תהלים טז:ד) | equivalent | "Libation of blood"; Ps 55:21. |
| I 88 | *rūʾa ṭābu ukarraṣ napištī* | עמיתי הטוב – קורץ את נפשי | `וַאֲכַלוּ קַרְצֵיהוֹן דִּי יְהוּדָיֵא` (דניאל ג:ח) | cognate | *karāṣu* "slander" = Aramaic קרץ (from Akk. *karṣī akālu*). |
| I 88 | same | same | `גַּם-אִישׁ שְׁלוֹמִי אֲשֶׁר-בָּטַחְתִּי בוֹ אוֹכֵל לַחְמִי הִגְדִּיל עָלַי עָקֵב` (תהלים מא:י) | equivalent | The trusted friend who betrays (psalm of the sickbed). |
| I 89 | *īruranni ardī* | ארר אותי עבדי | `אֲשֶׁר לֹא תִשְׁמַע אֶת עַבְדְּךָ מְקַלְלֶךָ` (קהלת ז:כא) | equivalent | The slave cursing his master. *arāru* = ארר [cognate]. |
| I 89 | same | same | `לְעַבְדִּי קָרָאתִי וְלֹא יַעֲנֶה` (איוב יט:טז) | equivalent | The slave who no longer obeys. |
| I 89 | same | same | `אָרוּר כְּנָעַן עֶבֶד עֲבָדִים יִהְיֶה לְאֶחָיו` (בראשית ט:כה) | divergence | In the Torah the master curses the slave; here the reverse. |
| I 90 | *amtī … ṭapiltī iqbi* | אמתי … דיבתי אמרה | `גָּרֵי בֵיתִי וְאַמְהֹתַי לְזָר תַּחְשְׁבֻנִי` (איוב יט:טו) | equivalent | Slave woman turned against her master; Gen 16:4 `וַתֵּקַל גְּבִרְתָּהּ בְּעֵינֶיהָ`; Prov 30:23. |
| I 90 | *ṭapiltu* | דיבה | `כִּי שָׁמַעְתִּי דִּבַּת רַבִּים` (תהלים לא:יד) | equivalent | |
| I 91 | *īmurannī-ma mūdû šaḫāta īmid* | ראני מכירי – ובזווית נסמך | `וּפַחַד לִמְיֻדָּעָי רֹאַי בַּחוּץ נָדְדוּ מִמֶּנִּי` (תהלים לא:יב) | equivalent | Strong: acquaintances who see him flee. |
| I 91 | same | same | `וּכְמַסְתֵּר פָּנִים מִמֶּנּוּ נִבְזֶה וְלֹא חֲשַׁבְנֻהוּ` (ישעיהו נג:ג) | equivalent | The despised sufferer from whom people hide their faces. |
| I 91 | same | same | `רָאוּנִי נְעָרִים וְנֶחְבָּאוּ` (איוב כט:ח) | divergence | The same gesture (they see me and hide), in Job out of awe before his fall, here out of contempt after it. |
| I 92 | *ana lā šīrīšu iškunanni kimtī* | ללא־בשרה שמתני משפחתי | `כִּי אָחִינוּ בְשָׂרֵנוּ הוּא` (בראשית לז:כז) | equivalent | "Flesh" = kin in both languages; Gen 29:14; Isa 58:7; Ps 88:9 `שַׁתַּנִי תוֹעֵבוֹת לָמוֹ`; Job 19:14. |
| I 93 | *petâssu ḫaštu* | פתוחה לו שחת | `בּוֹר כָּרָה וַיַּחְפְּרֵהוּ וַיִּפֹּל בְּשַׁחַת יִפְעָל` (תהלים ז:טז) | equivalent | Commentary: *ḫaštu* = *šuttatu* "grave"; Ps 35:7, 30:10. |
| I 94 | *šakin ana rēši* | שׂוּם לראש | `וַיָּשִׂימוּ הָעָם אוֹתוֹ עֲלֵיהֶם לְרֹאשׁ` (שופטים יא:יא) | equivalent | *šakānu ana rēši* = `שׂם לראש` word for word; Deut 28:13. |
| I 95 | *dābib nullâtīya ilu rēṣūšu* | המספר עליי נבלות – אל עוזרו | `יִשְׁלָיוּ אֹהָלִים לְשֹׁדְדִים` (איוב יב:ו) | equivalent | The wicked who prosper with divine help; divergence from Ps 54:6 `הִנֵּה אֱלֹהִים עֹזֵר לִי`. |
| I 96 | *ana ša iqbû aḫulap* | לאשר אמר "רב!" | `רַב עַתָּה הֶרֶף יָדֶךָ` (שמואל ב כד:טז) | equivalent | *aḫulap* "enough!" = the plea that stops the plague; 1 Kgs 19:4. |
| I 96 | same | same | `וַיִּוָּעֲדוּ יַחְדָּו לָבוֹא לָנוּד לוֹ וּלְנַחֲמוֹ` (איוב ב:יא) | divergence | Job's friends come to console; here who pities dies. |
| I 97 | *īteme balāssu šēduš* | היו חייו לרוח־מגן | `מַדּוּעַ רְשָׁעִים יִחְיוּ עָתְקוּ גַּם-גָּבְרוּ חָיִל` (איוב כא:ז) | equivalent | The one who refused help prospers. |
| I 98 | *ul arši ālik idi gāmila ul āmur* | לא קניתי הולך לצדי, חומל לא ראיתי | `וָאֲקַוֶּה לָנוּד וָאַיִן וְלַמְנַחֲמִים וְלֹא מָצָאתִי` (תהלים סט:כא) | equivalent | Strong: no one to share his grief. |
| I 98 | same | same | `הַבֵּיט יָמִין וּרְאֵה וְאֵין-לִי מַכִּיר` (תהלים קמב:ה) | equivalent | Also Ps 22:12 `כִּי-אֵין עוֹזֵר`, Isa 63:5, Qoh 4:1, Lam 1:2. |
| I 99 | *uzaʾʾizū mimmûya* | חילקו את כל אשר לי | `יְחַלְּקוּ בְגָדַי לָהֶם וְעַל-לְבוּשִׁי יַפִּילוּ גוֹרָל` (תהלים כב:יט) | equivalent | Strong: the sufferer's goods divided while he lives, in Ps 22 (`אֵלִי אֵלִי`). *zâzu* D = `חלק`. |
| I 99 | *mimmûya* | כל אשר לי | `הִנֵּה כָל אֲשֶׁר לוֹ בְּיָדֶךָ` (איוב א:יב) | equivalent | Ps 109:11 `יְנַקֵּשׁ נוֹשֶׁה לְכָל-אֲשֶׁר-לוֹ`. |
| I 99 | *ṣindi u birti* | לאספסוף ולריקים | `בְּנֵי-נָבָל גַּם-בְּנֵי בְלִי-שֵׁם` (איוב ל:ח) | equivalent | The rabble; Num 11:4 `וְהָאסַפְסֻף`. |
| I 100 | *pī nārīya umaṭṭiṭū sakīka* | את פי נהרותיי מוטטו בטין | `סִתְּמוּם פְּלִשְׁתִּים וַיְמַלְאוּם עָפָר` (בראשית כו:טו) | equivalent | Stopping a man's water sources as hostility; 2 Kgs 3:19. *maṭāṭu* ~ מוט [cognate]. |
| I 101 | *ušassû alāla* | הרחיקו את ההידד | `הֵידָד הִשְׁבַּתִּי` (ישעיהו טז:י) | equivalent | The work cry silenced in the fields; Jer 48:33. |
| I 102 | *kī āli nakiri ušqammimū ālī* | כעיר אויב – הדמו את עירי | `כִּי יְהוָה אֱלֹהֵינוּ הֲדִמָּנוּ` (ירמיהו ח:יד) | equivalent | Silencing = destruction; Lam 1:1. |
| I 103 | *parṣīya ušalqû šanâm-ma* | את פולחניי – הביאו אחר לקחת | `פְּקֻדָּתוֹ יִקַּח אַחֵר` (תהלים קט:ח) | equivalent | Strong: "another" + "take" (*leqû* = לקח [cognate]). |

### The text on its own terms
- **The poem is a fulfilled vow of thanksgiving** (1, 37–40): *ludlul* "I will praise" is the closing promise of Akkadian incantation prayers; the hymn (1–36) praises a god whose anger always yields to mercy, and the speaker's first-person *lušāpi* / *lušalmid* (37, 39) set the programme of the whole poem: to show the anger (I–II), to teach that mercy is near (III–V) (Lenzi ch. 8).
- **One god, two hands** (5–36): every couplet pairs wrath (storm, flood, heavy hand, horns, barbed blows, demons, Adad and Erra) with mercy (morning breeze, gentle palm, bandages, incantation, reconciliation). Wrath sits in the hand and the weapon; mercy in the inner organs – belly and liver (8, 33–34). The warrior god "turns into a woman in childbirth" (18), the bull into a cow with her calf (19–20): mercy is imagined as female.
- **Marduk is the source of sin as well as of its release** (23–25): he "makes (a man) acquire sin" and "makes him acquire" the *utukku* demon by the same verb, and on his day guilt and debt are "untied" (*paṭāru*). Sin, demon and disease are one kind of thing – a bond that only the god's incantation (*tû*, 26) can loosen.
- **Personal religion as a household of protectors** (15–16, 28, 41–48): a man has a personal god and goddess and two protective spirits (*šēdu*, *lamassu*) "at his side"; the high god's frown makes them withdraw. The god "goes up to his mountain", the goddess "moves away", the spirit "splits off", the guardian "takes fright and looks for someone else" – and the man feels the loss as the loss of his dignity, virility, bearing and shelter (47–48: Steinert's reading, in Lenzi ch. 5). The supernatural and the social self are one.
- **Divination as the language of the world** (49–54): signs, the sheep's liver read by the *bārû* ("muddied", "swollen"), the *šāʾilu*'s dreams and incense, a chance word in the street (*egerrû*), the night's dream – every channel answers "evil" or nothing. In III three good dreams will answer.
- **The Kassite court** (55–72): the king is "flesh of the gods, sun of his people"; when his heart is "knotted" no pardon is possible. Seven courtiers speak in turn – "I'll make him pour out his life", "I've ousted him", "I'll seize his office", "I'll enter his house" – a lively, almost theatrical scene; the seven are likened to storm-demons and *utukku*s (the Seven of the exorcists' series).
- **Social death in stages** (73–104): the body bows (head, heart, chest, arms), the street points its finger, the palace squints, the city glowers, the land seems foreign; brother, friend, comrade, colleague, best friend, slave, slave-girl, acquaintance, kin turn away one by one; then property is parcelled out to the rabble, canals silted, the work song banished from the fields, the city silenced, his cultic offices given to an outsider. Status, kinship, property and cult are one web.
- **The world upside down** as the wisdom tradition's image of disaster: the slave curses his master in the assembly, the stripling pushes back the noble's chest, the slanderer is "set at the head" while the noble is "turned into a servant" – with the pun on *rēšu* "head / servant" (78, 94); whoever says "Enough! (Mercy!)" dies, whoever refuses help prospers (95–97).
- **Wordplay as theology:** *enēnu* I "to grant favour" (38) and *enēnu* II "to punish" (41) sound alike; *ušatbi* "he raised" is Marduk raising the fallen (14) and the courtier "raising" him from his post (60); *šūpîš* "openly" (89) echoes *lušāpi* "I will make manifest" (37); *ḫaššu … ḫanšē* "the fifth … the fifty" (63); *aḫû* "stranger" / *aḫī* "my brother" (84).
- **Time as lament** (105–110): day, night, month, year – sighing, wailing, daze, misery; the dove's moan; the fifth description of weeping (Lenzi). And the closing hope is measured in the same units: maybe tomorrow, maybe at the new moon (119–120) – Tablet II will open "a year passed".
- **The ancient readers:** an Assyrian commentary (K 3291) explained this tablet word by word (*napraku* "bar" = *pirku* "fraud" by homonymy; *ewû* "become" = *mašālu* "be like"; *rēšu* = *ardu* "slave"; *ḫaštu* = *šuttatu* "grave"), an Uruk scholar quoted I 76–77 "as said in Ludlul", and pupils in Babylon, Sippar, Kiš and Aššur copied its lines at school.

### Lines shared with other files
- Inside I: 1/3, 2/4 (identical), 9/11, 10/12 (word order follows the Akkadian), 29/31 (see Decisions).
- For later tablets: *aḫulap* = `רַב!` (III 35, 38, 55); *naparkû* = `חדל` (II 15); *aḫītu* = `הלאה` (II 105); *kimtu* = `משפחה` (II 119); *unūtu* = `כלים` (II 65); *têrtu* = `הוראה` (II 109); *teslītu* / *supû* = `תחנון` / `תחינה` (II 15, 23–24; V 39, 54); *ennettu* = `עונש` (III 3, 59); *ālik idi* "go at the side" = `הלך לצד` (II 113 *idīya ul illik*); *damiqtu* = `חסד` (II 28, V 106); *simtu* = `צביון` and *dūtu* = `און` (IV); *mītūtu* = `מיתה` (II, V); *šēdu* / *lamassu* (V); *rēmēnû* = `רחום` (III; Theodicy); *pelludû* / *parṣu* = `חוקות` / `פולחנים` (Theodicy 135). eBL's "cf." links (I 51–54, 80, 82, 92 → K 2765; 107 → CBS 1514) point to texts outside the corpora.

### Proposals for the shared renderings
1. **§6 title line:** *ludlul bēl nēmeqi ila muštāla* = `אֲהַלֵּל אֶת אֲדוֹן הַחָכְמָה, אֵל מָתוּן,` (since the 2026-10-23 harmonization `אֲפָאֵר אֶת אֲדוֹן הַחָכְמָה, אֵל מָתוּן,`: *dalālu* = `פֵּאֵר`) (*muštālu* = `מָתוּן`, as LH); I 3 `מַרְדּוּךְ, אדון החכמה, אֵל מתון,`.
2. **§5 anger lemmas, add:** *nakbatu* `כובד`; *ennettu* `עונש` + *enēnu* II `ענש`; *gaṣṣu* `אכזר`; *akṣu* `חמור` (vocalized); *šabbasû* `כועס`; *nekelmû* `הזעיף פנים`; *nadāru* `השתולל` / *nanduru* `משתולל`; *nalbubu* `זועף`.
3. **§2 personal religion, add:** *karāṭu* N `נִכְמָר`; *enēnu* I `חנן` [cognate]; *kâšu*/*kiāšu* D "help, rescue" `סעד`; *nêšu* Š `קיים (חיים)`; *naparkû* `חדל`; *bêšu* `זז`; *aḫītu* `הלאה`; *salātu* (of a spirit) `ניתק`; *šadâšu īli* `עלה אל הרו`; *ālik idi* `הולך לצד`; *egerrû* = `קוֹל־הַמִּקְרֶה`; *dalāḫu* `דלוח` [cognate]; *têrtu* "office" `משרה` (confirmed); *parṣu* = `פולחן` (replace `פֶּלַח`), *pelludû* = `חוקות` (both as Enūma eliš V 67); *tû* `לחש`.
4. **§3 body/social:** *bāštu* `הדר` (as Gilg. XI); *dūtu* `און`; *simtu* `צביון`; *tarānu* `מחסה`; *nanzāzu* `העומדים לפניו` (confirmed); *taslītu* `רכילות`; *qīptu* `פקודה`; *tamāḫu* `תפש`; *kaṣāru* `קשר` (*riksa kaṣāru* `קשר קשר`); *illatu* `חבורה`; *nagāru* D `הלשין`; *karāṣu* D "slander" `קרץ` [cognate]; *ḫaštu* `שחת`; *mūtawû* `השָּׂח`; *rēṣu* `עוזר`; ***aḫulap*** `רַב!` (always vocalized); *ṣiddu u birtu* `אספסוף וריקים`; *mimmû* `כל אשר לי`; *alālu* `הֵידָד`; *unūtu* (innards) `כלים`; *gitallutu* `פלצות`; *qitayyulu* `תמהון`; *idirtu* `צרה`; *adirtu* `קדרות` (correct §2's `חרדה`, which belongs to *adāru* II); *zammāru* `זמר`; *ṣarāpu* II Š `הצריח`; *ṣarāpu* I `צרב`; *nāpalû* `מענה`; *agašgû* `זאטוט`; *etellu* `נגיד`; *mutellu* `נעלה`; *appatu* `מתג`; *šagimmatu* `שאגה`; *kabāru* `כביר` (open decision 5); *ṣāltu* `ריב`, *puḫpuḫḫû* `קטטה`.
5. **Damqu family (§3):** *damiqtu* (noun) `חסד`; *damqu* (adj.) `נאה`; *ṭābtu* / *ṭābtiš* `טובות` (reserved-word allow-list already has *ṭābtu*).
6. **Always vocalize (add to §8 / verify TRAPS if wanted):** `אֵל` when it stands alone (reads "to"); `רַב` (*aḫulap*); `אוּלַי` (*tuša* "perhaps"); `הָאֵלָה` (already); `כְּפָרָה` (cow / atonement); `חֲמוּרָה` (severe / she-ass); `הַכָּבֵד` (heavy / liver); `כָּבְדָה`; `הַנָּאָה` (fine / pleasure); `שָׂמַתְנִי` (put / excommunicated); `הֵסֵב`; `אֲגַלֶּה`; `הִרְצוּ`.
7. **Convention:** parenthesized normalization words are not translated (as Gilgameš); `(לאדם)` may be added after *rašû* Š "make acquire".

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **Check a new rendering against every corpus, not only this one** (grep the `>` lines of all corpora with the Akkadian beside them): it found that the creation corpus had already fixed *pelludû* / *parṣu* (`חוקות` / `פולחנים`) and the law corpus *muštālum* (`המתון`), against a pre-set `פֶּלַח` in this corpus's renderings – and a cross-corpus collision (`כביר` = *gašru* in Gilgameš).
- **An eBL lemma and eBL's translation can disagree on the key verb of a hymn line** (I 10/12 *kâšu* "delay" vs "rescue"; I 24 *ešertu* "shrine" vs "justice"): translate one, name the other in the apparatus and the parallels index.
- **Ancient commentaries are a rank-4 source for single words** (K 3291: *ewû* = *mašālu*, *rēšu* = *ardu*, *ḫaštu* = *šuttatu*, *napraku* = *pirku*): they decided four renderings here.

## Tablet II

### Scope
Ludlul II, all **120 eBL lines** (1–120), in `annotated-II.rtl.md`; intro `translation--intro-II.rtl.md`; generated `translation-II.rtl.md` (120 lines, 6 footnotes). 1–57 and 84–120 are well preserved; 58–83 rest mainly on one or two manuscripts (NinNA2b, HuzNA1, SipNB1, AššNA2a–b) and have restorations or uncertain readings in 58–59, 65–66, 80–83. 26 manuscripts (Nineveh NinNA1–5, Aššur AššNA1–2, Ḫuzirina HuzNA1 – almost the whole tablet –, Sippar SipNB1–3, SipLB1, Babylon BabLB1, BabNB1a–b; school tablets BabLBSch1–2, BabNBSch1–2), the commentary K 3291 (NinNACom1, explains II 3, 7, 9, 11, 21, 24, 43, 44, 53, 57, 61, 69, 70, 88, 90, 96, 97, 98, 100, 101, 107), and four ancient quotations of II 1. eBL numbering = Lenzi/Oshima for this tablet. Sections (our headings): 1–3 a year passed; 4–9 god, goddess and the experts fail; 10–22 the impious man he is likened to; 23–32 his piety; 33–38 "who can learn the god's mind?"; 39–48 the reversals of human life; 49–58 winds, demons and diseases attack; 59–70 the body destroyed; 71–83 the *alû* (sleep, paralysis, silence); 84–98 the shut mouth, hunger, the house as prison; 99–107 whip, goad, dung; 108–113 the experts recoil, the gods do not take his hand; 114–120 the open grave and the gloaters.

### Sources consulted
eBL `reading-edition.md` (N, T = Foster, L, notes, all ATF lines; brackets checked against the ATF) and `glossary.md` (stems and meanings of *šâḫu*, *ziāqu*, *nâšu*, *paʾāṣu*, *nêtu*, *têʾu*, *ramû* III, *nêru*, *laʾābu*, *piṭru*, *abātu*, *abāku* II, *edēqu*, *balāṣu*, *napāqu*, *arāmu*, *qadūtu*, *edēlu*, *peḫû*, *laʾātu*, *šaḫāḫu*, *zâbu*, *esēqu*, *šaḫātu* III, *ešû* IV, *râṣu*, *ersû*, *šukānu*, *bussuru*, *napardû*, *eṭû*, *ḫabālu* II, *rimûtu*); Lenzi 2023 ch. 2 (Tablet II text and translation), ch. 3.2 (all Tablet II notes), ch. 6 (6.2–6.4: the anatomical and pathological terms, incl. *diʾu*, *šūlu*, Lamaštu, *luʾtu*, *ḫaḫḫu*, *alû*, *rimûtu*, *mišittu*, *mangu*, *naḫbalu*, *napraku*), ch. 11 (11.4: II 71–85, the *alû* in Udug-ḫul VIII, sleep paralysis); CCP 1.3 (K 3291 obv. 24′–51′, rev. 1–3); `wisdom_tools.py pars II` (II 51–57 → III 69–75 "cf."; II 77–78 → Gilgameš V 29–30; the rest point outside the corpora), `lemma` / `grep --gilg` for every shared or new word; eBL Tablet III 69–79 (to fix the reversal words for both sides); Gilgameš V 28–30, VII, VIII 49, IX 46, OB Schøyen₂ 39; the law corpus for *ḫabālu* / *ḫablu* (`חבל` / `חבול`). Every Bible quotation printed from `תנך-מנוקד/` and checked.

### Decisions
- **Personal-religion terms left open in `renderings.md` §2:** *nīš ili zakāru* (22) = `הזכיר את חיי (האל)` (*nīšu* "life, oath by the life" = `חיים`, as `חַי יְהוָה`, `חֵי פַרְעֹה`; *zakāru* = `הזכיר` as in 13, 19; `שבועה` stays free for *māmītu*); *maṣḫatu* (20) = `קמח־מַצְחַתּוּ` (Gilgameš IV), **not** §2's proposed `קֶמַח־מִנְחָה` (`מנחה` would import Gen 4:3–5; the Akkadian is a kind of flour); *ūm ili* (16) = `יום האל`, *eššēšu* = `חג־החודש` (as §2, first-occurrence note in 16); *appa enû* (14) = literal `שינה את אפו` (Lenzi: "change one's disposition", Dan 3:19), *šukênu* = `השתחוויה` (§2).
- **The demon/wind passage 51–57 (binding for III 69–75):** *imḫullu* `רוח־רעה`, *išid šamê* `יסוד השמים`, *ziāqu* `נשב`; *irat erṣeti* `חזה הארץ` (written `מן חזה`, never `מחזה` "spectacle"), *šâḫu* `נשף`, *diʾu* `כאב־הראש`; ***šūlu* (53) = `שׁוּלוּ`** (transliterated as a demon: eBL *šūlu* I, commentary = *eṭemmu*), but *šūlu* in 66 (*šūlu ḫaḫḫu*) = `שיעול` (open decision 1); *apsû* `אַפְּסוּ`; *utukku* `אוּטוּכּוּ`, *lā nêʾi* `שאין להפנותו לאחור` (*nêʾu* = `הפנה לאחור`, I 75; not שוב, which III 72 *utīr* needs); *ēkur* `אֵכּוּר`; *lamaštu* `לַמַשְׁתּוּ`, *qereb šadî* `קרב ההר`; *mê mīli* `מי הגאות`, *šuruppû* `צינה` (I 26), *nâšu* `נע`; *urqītu* `ירק` [cognate], *paʾāṣu* `פרץ`, *luʾtu* `רפיון`. See the table under "Lines shared".
- **Body and disease (renderings §3 applied, new words fixed):** *qaqqadu* `ראש`, *muḫḫu* `קדקד`, *pūtu* `מצח`, *labiānu* `עורף`, *kišādu* `צוואר`, *irtu* `חזה`, *tulû* `שָׁדַיִם`, *ṣēru* `גב`, *rēš libbi* `ראש הלב`, *qerbu* `קרב`, *unūtu* `כלים`, *ḫašû* `ריאה`, *mešrêtu* `אברים`, *lānu* `גוף`, *gattu* `קומה`, *zumru* `גו`, *pagru* `גוויה`, *šīru* `בשר`, *eṣemtu* `עצם`, *mašku* `עור`, *šerʾānu* `גיד`, *dāmu* `דם`, *zīmu* `זיו` [cognate], *urʾudu* `גרון` (new), *riksu* `פרק`, *idu* `זרוע` / `לצד`, *birku* `ברך`, *šēpu* `רגל`, *uznu* `אוזן`, *īnu* `עין`, *šaptu* `שפה`, *libbu* `לב`, *kabattu* `כָּבֵד`. Diseases: *murṣu* `חולי`, *siliʾtu* `מחלה`, *marāṣu* `חלה` (Š *šumruṣu* `נחלה`, of wounds, ↔ Jer 30:12), *diʾu* `כאב־ראש`, *šūlu* `שיעול`, *ḫaḫḫu* `ליחה` (new), *raʾību* `רעד` (I 25), *rimûtu* `קהות`, *mišittu* `שיתוק`, *mangu* `קשיון` (Gilgameš `*קשיון*`, here not italic), *luʾtu* `רפיון`, *uriqtu* `ירקון` (new, [cognate] Deut 28:22), *sakikkû* `תסמינים` (new), *niṭâtu* `מלקות`, *miḫiṣtu* `מחץ`, *bubūtu* "hunger" `רעבון` (new; Gilgameš *bubūtu* "food" `מזון` is another sense).
- **Experts and their failure:** *bārû* `הבוחן`, *bīru* `בחינה`, *arkata parāsu* `חתך את אחורי (הדבר)`; *šāʾilu* `השואל` (also 82, where Lenzi reads a generic "inquirer": the Hebrew keeps both), *maššakku* `מֻגְמָר` (new; `קטורת` stays for *qutrinnu*), *dīnu* `דין`, *wapû* Š `גילה` (7, 110, as I 37); *zaqīqu* `משב`, *bâlu* `עתר` (new), *uzna petû* `פתח את האוזן`; *mašmaššu* `מלחש`, *āšipu* `אשף`, *kikiṭṭû* `טקס` (new), ***kimiltu* `זלעפה`** (new anger word: every usual one is taken, renderings §5, §10.1), *paṭāru* `פטר`; *têrtu* `הוראה`, *ešû* Dt `נבוך` (new), *šaḫātu* III `נרתע` (Gilgameš I 188), *šiknu* `מראה` (new; ↔ Lev 13:3 `מַרְאֵה הַנֶּגַע`), *adannu* `מועד` (1, 111).
- **Piety (23–32):** *ḫasāsu* `הגה` (new; `זכר` would be confused with *zakāru* `הזכיר`), *tašīmtu* `תושייה` (new), *sakkû* `תקנה` (new; `נוהג` = *mû*, `חוקות` = *pelludû*), *ṭūb libbi* `טוב לב` [cognate ↔ Deut 28:47], *rēdûtu* `ההליכה אחר`, *nēmelu* `רווח` (Gilgameš), *tattūru* `יתרון` (new, = Qohelet's word, tagged), *ikribu* `תפילה`, *nigûtu* `רינה` (Gilgameš), *šūrû* `הורה` (new), *naṣāru* `נצר`, *mû* `נוהג`, *šūquru* `יקר־ערך`, *aḫāzu* Št `האחיז`, *tanattu* `תהילה`, *iliš* `כְּאֵל`, *mašālu* D `המשיל` (G `נמשל`, 22), *puluḫtu* `יראה`.
- **Theology (33–48):** *lū īde* `לו ידעתי`; *magāru* `רצה`; *damāqu* stative `נאה` (never `טוב`); *gullultu* `עבירה` (new sin word); *masāku* `נמאס` (new); *ṭēmu* `דעת`, *milku* `עצה`, *anzanunzû* `התהום` (not the cognate of `תְּהוֹם`, which is *tiāmtu*: tagged [equivalent]), ***ḫakāmu* = `הבין`** (renderings §4 left it here: by meaning; the cognate חכם noted), *alaktu* `הליכה`, *apâtu* `הרבים` (new); *adāru* I `התקדר` (40) vs *adāru* II `חרד` (48, eBL's reading of the crux *uštādir*); *surriš* `לרגע` (40, 103), *zamar* `מיד`; *ḫabāru* `צהל`, *elēlu* `שיר־גיל`, *lallaru* `מקונן`, *purīdu* `ירך`, *ṣibit appi* `אחיזת אף` (body measures of time kept); *šebû* `שבע` [cognate], *šanānu* `התחרה`, *awû* Gt `שח`, *dabābu* `סיפר`, *erkallu* `אֶרְכַּלָּה` (transliterated; `שאול` reserved).
- **Prison and humiliation (84–107):** *naḫbalu* `פח`, *napraku* `מחסום`, *sekēru* `סכר` [cognate], *edēlu* `נעל`, *peḫû* `חסום`, *mašqû* `שוקת` (2026-10-23 harmonization: `מַשְׁקֶה`), *daddaru* `לענה` (new; the Lam 3:15 contact is made by meaning and tagged), *laʾātu* `בלע`, *sīrāšu* `סובא`, *eršu* `ערש`, *mēseru* `מאסר`, *mūṣû* `יציאה`, *kišukku* `בית־כלא`, *târu ana* `נהפך ל־` (Gilgameš IV), *illurtu* `אזיקים`, *maškanu* `כבל`, *qinnāzu* `שוט`, *naṭû* `הלקה`, *ṣillû* `סלונים` (new; `קוץ` kept out, Gilgameš decision 8), *paruššu* `דרבן`, *saḫālu* `דקר`, *ziqtu* `דוקרנים`, *labāšu* `לבש` [cognate], *redû* `רדף` / *rēdû* `רודף` / *ridûtu* `רדיפה`, *napāšu* D `(יניח) לִנָּפֵשׁ` [cognate נפש], *nabalkutu* Gtn `התגלגלות`, *sapāḫu* `פיזר`, *rubṣu* `גללים`, *biātu* `לן`, *balālu* Dt `התבולל` [cognate], *tabāštānu* `צואה`.
- **The end (108–120):** *râṣu* `חש (לעזרתי)`, *qāta ṣabātu* `אחז את ידי`, *rêmu* `רחם` [cognate]; *kimaḫḫu* `גדיש` (§3), *ersû* `מוכן`, *šukānu* `תכשיט`, *adi lā* `עד לא` (not `טרם`), *mītūtu* `מיתה`, *bikītu* `בכית`; ***ḫabālu* `חבל` (*ḫabil* `חבול`)** [cognate], as LH (`שהחזק בחלש לא יחבול`, *ḫablu* `חבול`); *ḫādû* / *ḫādītu* `השמח / השמחה לאידי`, *bussuru* `בישר` [cognate], *napardû* `נהר` (new), *nawāru* `האיר`; *eṭû* `חשך` (Gilgameš §15; so *ekēlu*, 60, = `אָפֵל`, new), *arāmu* `עטה` (81, 93, 120; Gilgameš), *qerbu* I "near" `קרוב`, *mūdû* `מכיר`.
- **Other:** *zapurtu* `רעת־מזל` (new); *išartu* `יושר` (the cognate kept, its sense "prosperity" explained); *ippīru* `מדון` (new); *šanû* IV `משונה` [cognate]; *baṭālu* `ביטל` [cognate]; *šêṭu* `הזניח`; *mêšu* `בזה`; *naʾādu* Gt `זהירות`; *maḫû* `השתגע`; *mašû* `שכח`; *ešēru* N (50) `נתיישר` [cognate]; *munnišu* `מחליש`; *qâpu* `התמוטט`; *šūnuḫu* `מיוגע`; *emēdu* N `נסמך`; *têʾu* `חיפה`; *nêru* `הכה` (by meaning; Gilgameš `קטל` "kill"); *edēqu* `עטף` (new; not `לבש`, which would manufacture Judg 6:34); *šuškallu* `רשת`; *balāṣu* `נעוץ`; *namuššišu* `פסיעה`; *napāqu* `נחנק`; *qadūtu* `בוץ`; *esēqu* `חרוט`; *šaḫāḫu* `נשר`; *zâbu* `זב` [cognate]; *zaqru* `תמיר`; *urbatu* `גומא` (commentary: = papyrus); *uliltu* `*דבלה*` (eBL "dried fig", uncertain); *abāku* N `הוטל` (↔ Jer 22:28 `הוּטְלוּ … וְהֻשְׁלְכוּ`).
- Parenthesized normalization words ((u), (-ma), (iz)) are not translated (7, 11, 13, 15, 18, 23, 24), as in Tablet I.

### Open decisions for the user
1. ***šūlu*** (53, III 71): `שׁוּלוּ` (transliterated demon, eBL *šūlu* I, commentary = *eṭemmu*) vs `שיעול` (as 66, renderings §3, Lenzi "demonic cough"). eBL lemmatizes the same phrase in III 71 as *šūlu* III "cough"; the Tablet III translator should follow II 53 (`שולו הרע`) unless the coordinator decides otherwise.
2. ***maṣḫatu*** = `קמח־מַצְחַתּוּ` (Gilgameš) instead of renderings §2's `קֶמַח־מִנְחָה`.
3. ***kimiltu*** = `זלעפה` (a rare word; alternative `קצף־אל`, but *agāgu* = `קצף` is fixed).
4. ***ekēlu*** (60) = `אָפֵל` and ***eṭû*** (119) = `חשך` (Gilgameš); the flood corpus has *ekēlu* = `הֶחְשִׁיךְ`, so the two corpora disagree; Lenzi reads *īkil* (*ekēlu*) also in 120.
5. **II 28** *šumma*: `אכן` (Lenzi, Oshima: asseverative) vs Foster's "as if" (`כאילו`).
6. **II 67** *piṭrī*: `*פִּטְרִי*` (eBL *piṭru* "release", italic) vs Lenzi's *pitru* "belly" (`בטני`).
7. **II 70** *uliltu* = `*דבלה*` (eBL "dried fig") vs a neutral `כצמח־אוּלִלְתּוּ` (Lenzi: unidentified plant).
8. **II 88** *daddaru* = `לענה`: the natural bitter weed, but it creates the Lam 3:15, 19 contact by itself (tagged); alternative: transliterate `דַּדָּרוּ`.
9. **II 104** *paṭāru* D of joints = `פוטרו` (renderings §2 *paṭāru* = `פטר`, keeping the pun with 9) – the Hebrew reads oddly ("fired"); alternative `הותרו` (Gilgameš *paṭāru* = `הִתִּיר`).
10. **II 26** *tattūru* = `יתרון` (the exact sense, and Qohelet's word; tagged) – keep, or `עודף` to avoid the Qohelet echo?
