|---|---|---|---|
| 1–30 | 1–30 | 1–30 | identical |
| 31–ca. 103 | 31–ca. 103 | eBL − 1 (e.g. eBL 38 = BWL 37; eBL 61 = BWL 60) | Lambert counted one line too few from III 31 (Lenzi ch. 1 n. 84); where his numbered text ends was not checked |
| 61 | 61 (Oshima: the commentary line at 66) | 60 | the placement of NinNACom1 rev. 8 (*egâtīya ušābil šāra*) is disputed; eBL and Lenzi: 61 |
| 104, 106 | 104, 106 | commentary lines a, b | placed in the text by Oshima (BabNB1a = MS III.CBab) |
| 107–110 | 107–110 | – | fragments |
| `a+1` | Com. line a | Com. line c | NinNACom1 rev. 16–17 |
| `a+2` | Com. line b | Com. line d | rev. 18 |
| `b+1` | Com. line c | Com. line e | rev. 19–20 |
| 113–117 | (111–120 "still missing") | – | BabNB2a r 1′–5′ (new, eBL 2024–26) |
| 118 | Com. line d | Com. line f | rev. 21–22; placed by BabNB2a r 6′ |
| 119–120 | – | – | BabNB2a r 7′–8′ |
| (IV 1) | Com. line e | Com. line g | *itbuk mānaḫtašin* – eBL puts it at the head of Tablet IV |

Manuscript sigla (Lenzi → eBL): III.EAš = AššNA1; III.FAš = AššNA2; III.DSip = SipNB1 (Si 55); III.BSip = SipNB2; III.HSip = SipNB3; III.JSip = SipNB4 (IM 124581, Fadhil / Jiménez 2019); III.iSip = SipNBSch1; III.gSip = SipLBSch1; III.CBab = BabNB1a (+ BabNB1b, BM 39523); ComNin = NinNACom1.

### Sources consulted
eBL `reading-edition.md` (N, T = Foster, L, notes, every ATF line; brackets checked against the ATF) and `glossary.md` (stems of *edēḫu*, *ṭummumu*, *sakāku*, *epēqu*, *ebēṭu*, *ḫelû*, *esēru*, *šabāḫu*, *patānu*, *kapāpu*, *ramû*); `manuscripts.md`; Lenzi 2023 ch. 2 (Tablet III text and translation, l. 4004–4380 of `full-text.txt`), ch. 3.3 (all Tablet III notes and the commentary lines a–e), ch. 1 (numbering history, n. 84, 157–160, 201–207), ch. 6 (*šammāḫu*, *amīru*, *luʾu*, *ṭupuštu*, *lagāʾu*, *nakimtu*), ch. 7.3 (the dreams and the dream figures, the exorcist's craft), ch. 9 (the commentary's equations, *lagabbiš*, *amalu*, *šūṣû*); CCP 1.3 (K 3291 rev. 4–22); `wisdom_tools.py pars III` (21–22 ↔ 29–30, 35/38 ↔ 55, 51 ↔ I 41, 69–75 ↔ II 51–57, 9 ↔ Gilg. OB Schøyen₃ a+6′ – no Hebrew there), `lemma` for every content lemma against I and II (scratchpad script over all lemmas of III), `grep --gilg` for every new Hebrew word, and a normalization search over every annotated file of all corpora (melammu, ḫalāpu, pulḫātu, mušītu, šiptu, bīnu, ramāku, nalšu, qutru, imbaru, agû, ebēbu, elēlu). `renderings.md` incl. §10.1–10.2; Gilgameš renderings §0–§21; the table "Lines shared with other files" of § Tablet II. Every Bible quotation printed from `תנך-מנוקד/` (`check_bible_quotes.py --show`) before quoting.

### Decisions
- **Names (renderings §1):** Laluralimma = `לָלוּרָלִימָּה` (eBL *laluralima*; Sum. *lal₃-ur₂-alim-ma*; the commentary renders it *Ṭābi-utul-Enlil* "sweet is the lap of Enlil" – the "Tabi-utul-Enlil, check" of §1 is the same figure in Akkadian, not a second name); Ur-Nintinugga = `אוּר־נִינְתִּינוּגָּה` ("servant of Nintinugga", a healing goddess); Šubši-mešrê-Šakkan = `שׁוּבְּשִׁי־מֶשְׁרֵא־שַׁכַּן` (first mention of the sufferer's name; meaning explained at 44, footnote); Nippur = `נִיפּוּר`; *bābilû* = `הבבלי`; Marduk `מַרְדּוּךְ` (43, then plain).
- **The dream figures:** *ištânu* / *ištêt* "a singular one" = `אחד` / `אחת` in all three introductions (9 `עלם אחד`, 23 `טבול אחד`, 31 `עלמה אחת`); *ramku* "bathed (priest)" = `טבול` (Gilgameš *ramāku* = `טבל`; rejected: `כהן־טהרה`, an added noun); *eṭlu* `עלם`, *ardatu* `עלמה` (Gilgameš).
- **Dream formula 21–22 = 29–30:** `שניתי / שילשתי – וחלום רואה אני,` / `בחלום אשר ראיתי בלילה הזה:` – *šanû* III / *šalāšu* = `שנה` / `שילש` [cognate, 1 Kgs 18:34]; *šunat aṭṭulu mušītīya* takes the Gilgameš formula (`החלום אשר ראיתי בלילה הזה`, I 244, VII 165) with `ב־`.
- **Purification terms:** *mê šipti* = `מי קסם`, *šiptu* = `קסם` (as Enūma eliš, `בקסמו הטהור`), because *tû* = `לחש` (I 26, III 68) needs its own word; *bīnu* = `אשל`; *mullilu* = `מטהר`, *elēlu* family = `טהר`, *ellu* = `טהור` (45); *ebēbu* D (*ubbubu*) = `ניקה` (Gilgameš) – two roots for two lemmas; *ṣimdu* = `תחבושת` (I 22); *muššuʾu* "rub, massage" = `עִסָּה` (vocalized; also the name of the exorcists' series *Muššuʾu*); *kapāru* II "wipe" = `כִּפֵּר` [cognate] (89; Gilgameš §15); *nadû* of water and of a spell = `השליך` both (27–28: the repetition kept).
- ***aḫulap*** = `רַב!` at 35, 38, 55 (fixed). The three lines differ only where the Akkadian does (*iqbâ* `אמרה` 35; *iqbû* `אמרו` 38, 55; *šūnuḫ* `מיוגע הוא` 35, 55; *šumruṣ* `נחלה הוא` 38); `verify` lists 35/55 and 38/55 – justified in the apparatus.
- **II 51–57 → III 69–75:** every noun of II reused (`רוח־רעה`, `יסוד השמים`, `חזה הארץ`, `כאב־הראש`, `שׁוּלוּ רע`, `האַפְּסוּ שלו`, `אוּטוּכּוּ שאין להפנותו לאחור`, `האֵכּוּר`, `לַמַשְׁתּוּ`, `ההר`, `הצינה`, `הרפיון`), the coordinator's verbs used (*duppuru* D `הדף`, *kašādu* D `הֵנִיס`, *târu* D `השיב`, *elû* Š `העלה`, *nasāḫu* `נסח`, *šammu* `עשב`), and the rest fixed: *warādu* Š `הוריד`, *sakāpu* I `דחה` (I 16), *maḫāru* Š `הפגיש`, *agû* II `גל`, *tâmtu* `ים`, *išdu* `יסוד` (also "root" in 75, one word per lemma). III 71 *šūlu* = the demon `שׁוּלוּ` against eBL's lemma *šūlu* III "cough" (as II 53; open decision 1); III 100 *šūlu* "cough" = `השיעול` (as II 66).
- **Body restoration (mirrors II):** `ראש` (80), `עיניי` (82), `אוזניי` (84), `אף` (86), `שפתיי` (88), `פי` (90), `גרוני` (96), `ריאותיי` (100), `צווארי` (`a+1`); and the verbs of the disease reversed: *têʾu* II 59 `חיפו` → *tēʾātu* `חיפוי` (82); *petû uzni* II 8 `לא פתח את אוזני` → `פתח את שמיעתי` (85); *katāmu* II 87 `מכוסה` → `כוסה` (90); *napāqu* II 80 `נחנק` → `נחנקה`, `נחנק` (86, 96); *edēlu* II 86 `נעול` → *idiltu* `נעילתו` (99); *ramû* II 61 `הרפו את צווארי` → `צווארי, אשר רָפָה` (`a+1`, eBL lemma *ramû* II "throw" is a slip for *ramû* III); *enēšu* I 74 `החלישה` → `החלישן` (100); *pašāḫu* + *miḫiṣtu* I 35 `מי ירגיע את מחצו?` → `הרגיע את מחצו` (87); *napāšu* II 103 `לא (יניחני) לִנָּפֵשׁ לרגע` → `וְאֶנָּפֵשׁ [לרגע?]` (87).
- **New body / disease words:** *šinnu* `שן`; *lišānu* `לשון`; *luʾu* `ושט`; *šammāḫu* `המעי הגס`; *ṣupru* `ציפורן` (Gilgameš); *nešmû* `שמיעה`; *amīru* `שעווה`; *nipšu* `נשיפה` (Gilgameš); *ummu* "fever" `חום`; *rīdu* `רדיפה` (root of *redû* `רדף`, II 102); *ṭummumu* `נאטם`; *sakāku* `נסתם`; *ebēṭu* N `נכפת` (92, 94); *esēru* N `נאסר` [cognate; *mēseru* `מאסר`, II 95]; *birītu* "fetter" `חרצובות` (Isa 58:6; `כבל`, `אזיקים` taken); *lagabbu* `גוש`; *lagāʾu* `גלד`; *idiltu* `נעילה`; *ṭupuštu* `*שכבה*`; *pulḫâtu* (89) `*שלפוחיות*` (Lenzi, CAD *pulḫītu*; eBL lemma *puluḫtu*); *saḫḫu* `*נפיחות*`; *mašqītu* `משקה`; *iptennu* `ארוחה` (`סעודה` stays for *mākālû*); *umṣu* `רעב`; *šibḫu* / *šabāḫu* Št `משקע` / `שוקעו` (Gilgameš *šabāḫu* = `שקע`).
- **The remission of sin (51–62):** *nâḫu* (of the god's heart) `נח`; *pašāru* N of the liver `נתפייסה` (= I 2 `מתפייס`); *rēmēnû* `רחום`; *unnīnu* `שוועה` (new: `תחינה`, `תחנון`, `תפילה` are taken); *unnīna leqû* `לקח … שוועות` (↔ Ps 6:10 `תְּפִלָּתִי יִקָּח`); *nasḫuru* `חזרה` (*saḫāru* = `חזר`, I 16, 20); *mêšu* `בזה` (as II 17; here "disregard (sin)"); *akāšu* D `הצעיד (מעליי)` (Gilgameš OB *akāšu* `צעד`); *pišertu* `שחרור` (`פטר` / `התיר` taken; the pun with 52 *ippašru* noted); *egītu* `שגגה` (renderings §2 *egû* = `שגה`); *māmītu* `שבועה`, *arratu* `מארה`; *narbû* `גדולה`; *dalālu* `הלל` (= I 1 *ludlul* `אהלל`; since the 2026-10-23 harmonization `פֵּאֵר`, `אֲפָאֵר`); *wapû* Š `גילה` (I 37).
- ***wabālu*** in this tablet: `הביא` (44–45, "brought" to a person; II 20, Gilgameš) and `הוביל` [cognate יבל] (61 Š `נתן לרוח להובילן`, 77 Št `הוֹבִילָהּ`, 106 G `מוביל`), **never `נשא`**, which is kept for *našû* (1, 23, 27, 42). Reason: so that the reader sees that the poem does *not* say "carry sin" with *našû* (Heb. `נשא עון`), but has the wind "carry off" the negligences. Rejected: `נשא` (Gilgameš allows it for *wabālu* by sense), `סבל`, `השיא` (*našû* Š in Gilgameš). This is a deviation from the Gilgameš reservation of `הוביל` for *warû* (which does not occur in Ludlul or the Theodicy) – see Proposals.
- **Other fixed words:** *atāru* `יתר` / `יתיר` [cognate, Dan 2:31] (2, 9); *šikittu* `תואר`; *minâtu* `מידות`; *šarāḫu* D `מפואר`; *edēḫu* D `*מרוקם*` (variant *udduq* = *edēqu* `עטף`, II 71); *zaqāru* D `מזדקר`; *melammu* `זוהר`, *pulḫātu* `אימות` (Gilgameš / Enūma eliš); ***ḫalāpu* `מכורבל`** (1 Chr 15:27) – not Gilgameš's `התעטף`, because `עטף` is this corpus's *edēqu* (II 71); *ḫamû* N `קָהוּ`; *êru* `התעורר`, *ērūtu* `עֵרוּת`; *ṣallūtu* `יְשִׁינָה` and *ṣalālu* (inf.) `יְשִׁינָה` (one root; `שינה` stays for *šittu*); *mukīl rēši* `מחזיקי ראשי` (literal); *qâlu* "be silent" `נדם` (Gilgameš); *ripittu* `שוטטות` (root of *rapādu* `שוטט`); *ṭeḫû* G `נגש`, D `הגיש`; *šarratu* `מלכה`; *kabittu* (of a person) `נכבדת`; *ašābu* `ישב`; *lā tapallaḫ* `אל תירא` (renderings §2 *palāḫu* = `ירא`; the oracle formula); *šakānu* Š `הֵשִׂים`; *ayyumma* `אֵיזֶהוּ`; *ṭarru* `מזוקן`; *apāru* `חבש`, *agû* I `כתר`; *lēʾu* `לוח`; *muttabbilu* `מטפל` (`משרת` is *rēšu*, I 78); *paqādu* `הפקיד` [cognate]; *šipirtu* `שליחות` (with *šapāru* `שלח`); *ittu damqatu* `אותו הַנָּאָה`; *kullumu* `הֶרְאָה`; *ḫepû* N `נבקע`; *qutru* `עשן` (`קטורת` = *qutrinnu*); *imbaru* `ערפל`; *nalšu* `טל`; *reḫû* `נִתּוּךְ`; *lazzu* `מתמשך`; *sû* `אֶבֶן־סוּ` (transliterated); *šār bēri* `שלושת אלפים ושש מאות בֵּרוּ`; *niṭlu* `הבטה` (`מבט` is Gilgameš *imru*); *nawāru* D `האיר`; *mašāšu* `שפשף`; *qû* `קדרה`; *rūšu* `לכלוך`; *nabāṭu* Št `הבהיק`; *ṣabāriš* `למלל` (*ṣabāru* Gt, I 71); *wabālu* Št "converse" `להחליף (דברים)`; *ḫadādu* `*הוּחַדָּה*`; *atmû* `שיחה`; *ṭiābu* D `היטיב`; *irtu* II "song" `שירה`; *malīlu* `חליל`; *ḫelû* D `*עִלְּזָהּ*`; *epēqu* Dt `*נקרש*`; *maḫāru* "receive" `קיבל` (98, 106; "confront" Š `הפגיש`, 74); *nagāgu* `נהק`; *kitmuru* `נערם`; *šapāku* `שפך`; *amālu* Dt `הוּעַם` (I 47); *ḫarû* III `*סנסן*`; *ṣabāru* II `*עיקום*`; *erû* Gtn (104, against eBL's lemma *târu*) `*נתרוקן*`; *pišannu* `סל` (`כַּסַּל`, vocalized); *rakāsu* N `נרכס`; *zâzu* Dt `חולקו` (I 99); *athû* `שותף`; *erēnu* "root" `שורש`; *kapāpu* `כפף` [cognate]; *patānu* D `אימץ`; *kinnû* `צוק`; *amalu* `כְּאַמָּלוּ` (transliterated: `עץ` is reserved for *iṣu*); *zaqāpu* `זקף` [cognate]; *gāmir abāri* `גומר־חוסן` (*abāru* `חוסן`); *umāšu* `*היאחזות*` (Gilgameš II); *šūṣû* `הַמּוּצָא` (*aṣû* Š; `גרש` kept out of the line, Gen 3:24, 4:14 tagged); *nakimtu* `*גידול*`; *ṣepēru* / *ṣapāru* `*גזם*`; *karāṣu* `קרץ` [cognate].
- **Brackets:** by the ATF, word by word: no brackets where the root survives in one manuscript although N brackets it (6 *ṣal[lū]tī*, 21 vs 29, 33, 46, 47, 53 *[ilq]û* – BabNB4 *[i]l-qu*, 54 *[nas]ḫuršu* – BabNB4, 55 *iqbû* – SipNB3/BabNB4, 69, 71, 73, 90, 98); brackets where the root is broken in all (14, 21, 38, 44–45, 48, 51–52, 55 *šūnuḫ*, 56–57, 62, 86 *ni[pissu]*, 89 *ip[ṭur]*, 91, 92, 94).

### Open decisions for the user
1. **III 71 *šūla lemna*** = `שׁוּלוּ רע` (the demon, as II 53) against eBL's lemma *šūlu* III "cough"; alternative `שיעול רע` here and in II 53 (renderings §3, Lenzi "demon/cough"). III 100 has the cough (`השיעול`) in any case.
2. ***šiptu*** = `קסם` (Enūma eliš) vs `לחש` (which would merge *šiptu* and *tû*, both in this tablet: 23, 28 vs 68). `קסם` evokes the forbidden `קֹסֵם קְסָמִים` (Deut 18:10) – tagged as a [divergence] at 23; an alternative is `לחש־קסם` or transliteration *šiptu*.
3. ***wabālu*** = `הוביל` (61, 77, 106) – a deviation from the Gilgameš reservation of `הוביל` for *warû*; alternative: `נשא` (Gilgameš's "by sense"), at the price of the *našû* / `נשא` distinction (1, 23, 27, 42) and of an artificial `נשא עון` echo at 61.
4. **III 36 *lā tapallaḫ*** = `אל תירא` (renderings §2: *palāḫu* = `ירא`) – the oracle formula; alternative `אל תפחד` if "physical fear keeps `פחד`" (§2) is read strictly.
5. **III 12 *ḫalāpu*** = `מכורבל` instead of the Gilgameš `התעטף` (taken here by *edēqu*, II 71); alternative: let *ḫalāpu* have `עטוף` and move *edēqu* (II 71) to another verb.
6. **III 89 *pulḫâssina*** = `*שלפוחיותיהן*` (Lenzi, CAD *pulḫītu* "blister"; Foster "sores") against eBL's lemma *puluḫtu* "fear" (`אימותיהן`, which would echo 12).
7. **III 104 *ittarrû*** = `*נתרוקן*` (*erû* Gtn, with eBL's translation and Lenzi) against eBL's lemma *târu* (`שב`, the reserved Gen 3:19 root) and Oshima's *tarû* D "cramped".
8. **III 39** read as a question (Foster: "Whoever saw such a sight in a dream?") or as the description of Ur-Nintinugga opening a fourth dream (Lenzi, Fadhil / Jiménez); the Hebrew `אֵיזֶהוּ אשר … בחן בחינה?` keeps both.
9. **III 118 *šūṣû*** = `הַמּוּצָא` (literal, *aṣû* Š) vs `המגורש` (the meaning, but Gen 3:24 / 4:14 `גרש` would be manufactured).
10. **Cross-corpus collisions accepted:** *ebēṭu* `נכפת` (Gilgameš *kasû* = `כפת`); *lagabbu* `גוש` (Gilgameš *kiṣru* "lump" = `גוש`; here *kiṣru* "knot" = `קשר`, 89); *unnīnu* `שוועה` (Gilgameš *supû* = `שוועה`); *šūṣû* `הַמּוּצָא` beside Gilgameš *ṣītu* `מוֹצָא` (vocalized apart).

### Progress log
- 2026-10-19: read the brief, the guidelines (whole), corpus.md, renderings.md (incl. §10.1–10.2), the Gilgameš renderings (§0–§21 and §12 settled), the format model (Gilg. VII 1–60), `annotated-II.rtl.md` (all `>` lines with N, header, sample entries), `project.md` § Tablet I and § Tablet II (decisions, open decisions, own-terms, shared lines, proposals, lessons); the whole eBL edition of III (all ATF) and `manuscripts.md`; Lenzi ch. 2 (III), 3.3, 1 (numbering), 6 (terms), 7.3, 9 (commentary); CCP 1.3 rev. 4–22. `pars III`; lemma lookup of every content lemma of III against I–II by script; normalization search over all corpora for the purification, aura and weather words.
- Drafted 1–28, 29–68, 69–99, 100–120 as four scratch files; eBL's N lines and Foster's T inserted by script (`assemble.py` in the scratchpad), so the backtick lines are eBL's exactly; every Bible verse printed with `--show` before quoting.
- Pass (a) fidelity: every content word checked against the parse; brackets re-checked against the ATF word by word (changed 6 `ישינתי`, 53 `לקח`, 86 `[נשיפתו]`, 89 `[פטר]`); parenthesized normalization words not translated (24 *u*, 61 *u*, 99 *-ma*) except 37 (*u*, *ina*: needed for the syntax, noted); additions in `( )`: 59 `(מעליי)`, 91 `(אותו)`, 94 `(דברים)`, a+2 `(אותו)`.
- Pass (b) consistency: 69–75 checked noun by noun against II 51–57 (all identical); the body words against II 59–98; 21/29 and 22/30 identical where the Akkadian is; 35/38/55 differ only by the Akkadian; `lemma` / `grep --gilg` on every new Hebrew word (collisions found and avoided: `עטף` → *ḫalāpu* `מכורבל`; `מבט` → *niṭlu* `הבטה`; `קשר`, `כבל`, `אזיקים`, `אסר` → *ebēṭu* `נכפת`, *birītu* `חרצובות`; `תחנון` → *unnīnu* `שוועה`; `סעודה` → *iptennu* `ארוחה`; `רפש` → *rūšu* `לכלוך`; `התחדד` → *ḫadādu* `הוּחַדָּה`; `צהל`, `שמח` → *ḫelû* `עִלְּזָהּ`; `עץ` → *amalu* transliterated; `שוב` kept only at 72; `נחש` not written at 49; `גרש` not written at 118); fixed one wrong tag (72: [equivalent], not [cognate]); always-vocalized words checked (`כָּבְדָה`, `הַכָּבֵד`, `כָּבֵד`, `כְּבֵדוֹ`, `כְּאֵל`, `רַב`, `הַנָּאָה`, `שׁוּלוּ`, `שַׁכַּן`, `בֵּרוּ`, `הֶרְאָה`, `כִּפֵּר`, `קָהוּ`).
- Pass (c) Hebrew: read the generated translation; vocalized the ambiguous forms (`הֶחֱלוּנִי` "window", `הַנַּחֲלֶה` "inheritance", `עִסָּה` "dough", `כַּסַּל` "fool/loin", `הַמּוּצָא`, `הוֹבִילָהּ`, `וְאֶנָּפֵשׁ`); the section heading of 51–68 changed (`נישא` → `מובלות`, keeping `נשא` for *našû*); remaining oddities are deliberate literalisms (6 `שוטטו את ישינתי`, 20, 61, 74, `b+1`).
- `check_bible_quotes.py` on the annotated file: 9 mismatches in the first run (bare references taken as Genesis, ketiv `בַּשְּׁליִשִׁת`, `עֲלֵהֶם`, `יוּבָל`) fixed → 0; final run on annotated-III, translation--intro-III, translation-III and project.md: 0 problems. `wisdom_tools.py build` (121 lines, 15 footnotes); `verify III --strict`: problems 0 (two repeated-line differences, 35/55 and 38/55, justified by the Akkadian).

### Parallels index (III)

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| III 1 | *kabtat qāssu ul aleʾʾi našâša* | כָּבְדָה ידו – לא יכולתי לשאתה! | `כִּי יוֹמָם וָלַיְלָה תִּכְבַּד עָלַי יָדֶךָ` (תהלים לב:ד) | cognate | *kabātu* + *qātu* = `כבד` + `יד`; Ps 32 is a sick man's psalm of forgiven sin (32:5), the course of Ludlul III; "day and night" also in III 7. |
| III 1 | *kabtat qāssu* | כָּבְדָה ידו | `יָדִי כָּבְדָה עַל-אַנְחָתִי` (איוב כג:ב) | cognate | MT "my hand", LXX "his hand". |
| III 1 | *kabtat qāssu* | כָּבְדָה ידו | `וְאַכְפִּי עָלֶיךָ לֹא-יִכְבָּד` (איוב לג:ז) | cognate | Elihu promises the opposite; 33:7a `אֵמָתִי` ↔ III 2 *puluḫtu*. |
| III 1 | *kabtat qāssu* | כָּבְדָה ידו | `וַתִּכְבַּד יַד יְהוָה אֶל הָאַשְׁדּוֹדִים` (שמואל א ה:ו) | cognate | The god's heavy hand as disease. |
| III 1 | *ul aleʾʾi našâša* | לא יכולתי לשאתה | `כְּמַשָּׂא כָבֵד יִכְבְּדוּ מִמֶּנִּי` (תהלים לח:ה) | equivalent | A burden too heavy to bear; Ps 38:3 `וַתִּנְחַת עָלַי יָדֶךָ`. |
| III 1 | *našâša* | לשאתה | `גָּדוֹל עֲוֹנִי מִנְּשֹׂא` (בראשית ד:יג) | divergence | *našû* = `נשא` [cognate]; Ludlul's unbearable thing is the god's hand, not the sin. |
| III 2 | *atrat puluḫtašu* | יתירה אימתו | `וְזִיוֵהּ יַתִּיר … וְרֵוֵהּ דְּחִיל` (דניאל ב:לא) | cognate | *atāru* = Aram. `יתיר`. |
| III 7 | *urra u mūša ištēniš annaššar* | [י]ום ולילה – יחדיו נגרע אני | `כֵּן הָנְחַלְתִּי לִי יַרְחֵי-שָׁוְא וְלֵילוֹת עָמָל מִנּוּ-לִי` (איוב ז:ג) | equivalent | Suffering measured in days and nights; *annaššar* "I am diminished" is eBL's new reading (old *anassus* "I moan"). |

| III 8 | *šuttu … šumruṣāni* | חלום … הֶחֱלוּנִי | `וְחִתַּתַּנִי בַחֲלֹמוֹת וּמֵחֶזְיֹנוֹת תְּבַעֲתַנִּי` (איוב ז:יד) | equivalent | Dreams as part of the torment (before they become the cure). |

| III 24 | *bīnu* | אשל | `וַיִּטַּע אֶשֶׁל בִּבְאֵר שָׁבַע` (בראשית כא:לג) | equivalent | Identification of the tree (Tamarix), not a cognate. |

| III 26 | *ana ubbubīka išpuranni* | לנקותך שלחני | `מִנִּסְתָּרוֹת נַקֵּנִי` (תהלים יט:יג) | equivalent | "Cleanse me" from hidden faults (III 61 *egâtu*); *ebēbu* D = `ניקה`. |

| III 28 | *šipat balāṭi iddâ umaššiʾ zumrī* | קסם חיים השליך, עִסָּה את גווי | `וְקָרָא בְּשֵׁם יְהוָה אֱלֹהָיו וְהֵנִיף יָדוֹ אֶל הַמָּקוֹם וְאָסַף הַמְּצֹרָע` (מלכים ב ה:יא) | divergence | Naaman expects exactly this (an invocation and a hand over the body); the prophet refuses the rite and sends him to bathe (5:14 `וַיִּטְבֹּל`) – cf. *ramku* "the bathed one". |
| III 31 | *banû zīmūša* | יפה זיוה | `זִיוֹהִי שְׁנוֹהִי` (דניאל ה:ו) | cognate | *zīmu* = Aram. `זִיו`. |
| III 32 | *iliš mašlat* | כְּאֵל משולה | `אֱלֹהִים רָאִיתִי עֹלִים מִן הָאָרֶץ` (שמואל א כח:יג) | equivalent | A figure who looks divine; there too `אַל תִּירְאִי` (III 36). |
| III 35 | *iqbâ aḫulap magal šūnuḫ-ma* | אמרה: "רַב! מאוד מיוגע הוא!" | `רַב עַתָּה הֶרֶף יָדֶךָ` (שמואל ב כד:טז) | equivalent | *aḫulap* "Enough! (Mercy!)" = `רַב!` (fixed, I 96): the cry that stops the blow; = III 38, 55. |
| III 35 | *aḫulap* (commentary: *adi mati*) | רַב! | `וְאַתָּ (וְאַתָּה) יְהוָה עַד-מָתָי` (תהלים ו:ד) | equivalent | **Via the ancient commentary**: K 3291 explains *aḫulapi* = *adi mati* "how long?" – the psalmist's cry. Also Isa 6:11 `וָאֹמַר עַד מָתַי אֲדֹנָי`. |
| III 36 | *lā tapallaḫ* | "אל תירא!" | `אַל תִּירָא אַבְרָם אָנֹכִי מָגֵן לָךְ` (בראשית טו:א) | equivalent | The oracle-of-salvation formula, in a vision; Akk. *lā tapallaḫ* is its exact counterpart (Esarhaddon's prophecies). |
| III 36 | *lā tapallaḫ* | "אל תירא!" | `אַל תִּירָא כִּי עִמְּךָ אָנִי` (ישעיהו מא:י) | equivalent | Same formula; 41:10 `אִמַּצְתִּיךָ` ↔ III a+2 *upattin* `אימץ`. |
| III 36 | *lā tapallaḫ* | "אל תירא!" | `וַיֹּאמֶר אֵלַי אַל תִּירָא דָנִיֵּאל` (דניאל י:יב) | equivalent | In the river vision, after the seer has fallen silent (cf. III 9–14); also Dan 10:19, Judg 6:23. |
| III 39 | *ša ina šāt mūši ibrû bīra* | אשר בשעת הלילה בחן בחינה | `וּבֹחֵן לִבּוֹת וּכְלָיוֹת` (תהלים ז:י) | equivalent | *barû* = `בחן` (renderings §2); here extispicy at night. |
| III 43 | *Marduk-ma išpuranni* | "מַרְדּוּךְ הוא ששלחני | `אִם-יֵשׁ עָלָיו מַלְאָךְ מֵלִיץ אֶחָד מִנִּי-אָלֶף לְהַגִּיד לְאָדָם יָשְׁרוֹ` (איוב לג:כג) | divergence | Elihu's messenger to the dying sick man (33:19–28), who "is gracious to him" and announces his restoration (33:24–26); Ludlul's messenger is an exorcist with a bandage, not an intercessor. |
| III 44 | *ubilla ṣimda* | הבאתי [תחבושת] | `כִּי הוּא יַכְאִיב וְיֶחְבָּשׁ יִמְחַץ וְיָדָו תִּרְפֶּינָה` (איוב ה:יח) | equivalent | The god who strikes binds up; *ṣimdu* = `תחבושת` (I 22). Restored word (*ṣi[mda]*, von Soden; older *ṣimra* "prosperity"). |
| III 44 | *ṣimdu* | [תחבושת] | `כִּי הוּא טָרָף וְיִרְפָּאֵנוּ יַךְ וְיַחְבְּשֵׁנוּ` (הושע ו:א) | equivalent | Same; Ps 147:3 `וּמְחַבֵּשׁ לְעַצְּבוֹתָם`. Rests on a restoration. |
| III 44 | *ubilla ṣimda* | הבאתי [תחבושת] | `יִשְׂאוּ דְּבֶלֶת תְּאֵנִים וְיִמְרְחוּ עַל הַשְּׁחִין וְיֶחִי` (ישעיהו לח:כא) | equivalent | A healer sends a dressing to the sick man; in Hezekiah's thanksgiving (Isa 38), which has the same sequence: sickness, prayer, sign (38:7), sins cast away (38:17), healing. |
| III 44 | *Šubši-mešrê-Šakkan* | שׁוּבְּשִׁי־מֶשְׁרֵא־שַׁכַּן | `אִישׁ הָיָה בְאֶרֶץ עוּץ אִיּוֹב שְׁמוֹ` (איוב א:א) | divergence | Job is named in the first verse; Ludlul's sufferer only here, in the god's message, two-fifths into the poem. |
| III 46 | *ana muttabbilīya qātuššu ipqid* | למטפליי – לידו הפקיד | `בְּיָדְךָ אַפְקִיד רוּחִי` (תהלים לא:ו) | cognate | *paqādu* + *qātu* = `הפקיד ביד`; there man entrusts his spirit to God, here God's envoy entrusts the cure to a human attendant. |
| III 47 | *ina munatti išpura šipirta* | בתנומת שחר שלח שליחות | `אָז יִגְלֶה אֹזֶן אֲנָשִׁים וּבְמֹסָרָם יַחְתֹּם` (איוב לג:טז) | equivalent | God's message in the slumber of the night (33:15). |
| III 48 | *ittuš damqata nišīya uktallim* | את אותו הַנָּאָה – לאנשיי [הֶרְאָה] | `עֲשֵׂה-עִמִּי אוֹת לְטוֹבָה וְיִרְאוּ שֹׂנְאַי וְיֵבֹשׁוּ` (תהלים פו:יז) | cognate | *ittu damiqtu* = `אוֹת לְטוֹבָה` (*ittu* = `אות`), a sign that others see; Ludlul's "people" vs the psalm's "enemies". Verb restored. |
| III 48 | *ittuš* | אותו | `וְזֶה לְּךָ הָאוֹת מֵאֵת יְהוָה` (ישעיהו לח:ז) | cognate | The sign of Hezekiah's healing. |
| III 49–50 | *ina siliʾti īriku … murṣī arḫiš iggamir* | במחלה אשר ארכה … חוליי מהרה נגמר | `וַאֲרֻכָתְךָ מְהֵרָה תִצְמָח` (ישעיהו נח:ח) | divergence | "Long" (*arāku*) and "quickly" side by side; Heb. `אֲרֻכָה` "healing" is a sound-alike, not the same sense. |
| III 50 | *murṣī arḫiš iggamir* | חוליי מהרה נגמר | `שִׁוַּעְתִּי אֵלֶיךָ וַתִּרְפָּאֵנִי` (תהלים ל:ג) | equivalent | The healing told by the one who gives thanks; Isa 38:9. |
| III 51 | *ultu ša bēlīya libbašu inūḫu* | אחרי אשר לב אדוני [נח] | `וַהֲנִחֹתִי חֲמָתִי בָּךְ וְסָרָה קִנְאָתִי מִמֵּךְ וְשָׁקַטְתִּי וְלֹא אֶכְעַס עוֹד` (יחזקאל טז:מב) | equivalent | The god's wrath "at rest" ends the anger; *nâḫu* = `נוח` [cognate]. Verb restored. |
| III 51 | *libbašu inūḫu* | לב … [נח] | `וַיָּרַח יְהוָה אֶת רֵיחַ הַנִּיחֹחַ` (בראשית ח:כא) | divergence | After the flood the god is appeased by sacrifice; here no sacrifice is mentioned – the heart rests, then the healing follows. |
| III 52 | *ša Marduk rēmēnî kabattašu ippašru* | של מרדוך הרחום – כְּבֵדוֹ [נתפייסה] | `אֵל רַחוּם וְחַנּוּן אֶרֶךְ אַפַּיִם` (שמות לד:ו) | cognate | *rēmēnû* = `רחום`; the refrain I 2 (*muppašir* `מתפייס`) fulfilled. |
| III 53 | *ilqû unnīnīya* | לקח את שוועותיי | `שָׁמַע יְהוָה תְּחִנָּתִי יְהוָה תְּפִלָּתִי יִקָּח` (תהלים ו:י) | cognate | *unnīna leqû* "take (= accept) a supplication" = `תפלתי יקח`, the same verb (*leqû* = `לקח`); in Ps 6, which also has `עַד-מָתָי` (III 35). |
| III 54 | *nasḫuršu ṭāba ukallimanni* | את חזרתו הטובה – הֶרְאַ[נִי?] | `פְּנֵה-אֵלַי וְחָנֵּנִי` (תהלים כה:טז) | equivalent | The god's turning to the supplicant; *ṭābu* = `טוב`. |
| III 56 | *šēressu ana šūpê igmil napišta* | [את אשמתו?] לגלות – חמל [על הנפש] | `וְאַתָּה חָשַׁקְתָּ נַפְשִׁי מִשַּׁחַת בְּלִי` (ישעיהו לח:יז) | equivalent | The god spares the sick man's life; *napišta* restored. |
| III 57 | *narbîšu ana dulluli* | [את גדולתו] לפאר | `גַּדְּלוּ לַיהוָה אִתִּי וּנְרוֹמְמָה שְׁמוֹ יַחְדָּו` (תהלים לד:ד) | equivalent | Praise of the god's greatness after deliverance; *dalālu* is the poem's first verb (I 1 *ludlul*). |
| III 58 | *imēš arnī šertī* | בזה את עווני, את אשמתי? | `מִי אֵל כָּמוֹךָ נֹשֵׂא עָוֹן וְעֹבֵר עַל פֶּשַׁע` (מיכה ז:יח) | equivalent | The god "passes over" sin (*mêšu* "disregard"); *arnu* = `עוון`. First explicit mention of the sufferer's sin. |
| III 58 | *imēš arnī* | בזה את עווני | `לֹא כַחֲטָאֵינוּ עָשָׂה לָנוּ וְלֹא כַעֲוֹנֹתֵינוּ גָּמַל עָלֵינוּ` (תהלים קג:י) | equivalent | Ps 103: forgiveness (103:3, 10, 12) and healing (103:3) in one psalm. |
| III 58 | *imēš arnī* | בזה את עווני | `כִּי הִשְׁלַכְתָּ אַחֲרֵי גֵוְךָ כָּל חֲטָאָי` (ישעיהו לח:יז) | equivalent | Hezekiah, healed of his sickness. |
| III 59 | *ukkiš ennettī gillatī* | הצעיד (מעליי) את עונשי, את פשעי | `כִּרְחֹק מִזְרָח מִמַּעֲרָב הִרְחִיק מִמֶּנּוּ אֶת-פְּשָׁעֵינוּ` (תהלים קג:יב) | equivalent | Sins sent away; *gillatu* = `פשע`. |
| III 59 | *ukkiš ennettī* | הצעיד את עונשי | `יִכְבֹּשׁ עֲוֹנֹתֵינוּ וְתַשְׁלִיךְ בִּמְצֻלוֹת יָם כָּל חַטֹּאותָם` (מיכה ז:יט) | equivalent | Sins removed to a far place (the sea; in Ludlul the wind, III 61). |
| III 61 | *egâtīya ušābil šāra* | את שגגותיי – נתן לרוח להובילן | `וְנָשָׂא הַשָּׂעִיר עָלָיו אֶת כָּל עֲוֹנֹתָם אֶל אֶרֶץ גְּזֵרָה` (ויקרא טז:כב) | divergence | Sins transferred to a carrier and sent far away: the goat in the Torah, the wind in Ludlul. The Akkadian verb is *wabālu* (`הוביל`, cognate יבל), not *našû* (`נשא`). |
| III 61 | *egâtīya ušābil šāra* | נתן לרוח להובילן | `וַעֲוֹנֵנוּ כָּרוּחַ יִשָּׂאֻנוּ` (ישעיהו סד:ה) | divergence | Reversed image: there the sin is the wind that carries man off; here the wind carries the sin off. |
| III 61 | *egâtu* | שגגותיי | `שְׁגִיאוֹת מִי-יָבִין מִנִּסְתָּרוֹת נַקֵּנִי` (תהלים יט:יג) | equivalent | Faults of negligence, unknown to the sinner; the commentary glosses *egâti* = *ḫiṭâti* "sins". |
| III 61 | *ušābil šāra* | נתן לרוח להובילן | `מָחִיתִי כָעָב פְּשָׁעֶיךָ וְכֶעָנָן חַטֹּאותֶיךָ` (ישעיהו מד:כב) | equivalent | Sins vanishing into the air like cloud and mist (cf. III 77–81). |
| III 62 | *māmītī arratī* | [שבועתי?], מארתי | `וַתִּתַּךְ עָלֵינוּ הָאָלָה וְהַשְּׁבֻעָה` (דניאל ט:יא) | equivalent | The pair "oath-curse" + "curse" poured on the sinner; *arratu* = `מארה` [cognate of ארר]. First word restored. |
| III 68 | *uṭeḫḫâm-ma tâšu ša balāṭi u šulmi* | הגיש אליי את לחשו – של חיים ושלום | `יִשְׁלַח דְּבָרוֹ וְיִרְפָּאֵם וִימַלֵּט מִשְּׁחִיתוֹתָם` (תהלים קז:כ) | equivalent | The god's word / spell that heals; *šulmu* = `שלום` [cognate]. |
| III 69 | *uduppir imḫulla ana išid šamê* | הדף רוח־רעה אל יסוד השמים | `כְּהִנְדֹּף עָשָׁן תִּנְדֹּף` (תהלים סח:ג) | equivalent | Driving away like smoke (cf. III 77); Ps 1:4 `כַּמֹּץ אֲשֶׁר-תִּדְּפֶנּוּ רוּחַ`. |
| III 69–75 | (the reversal of II 51–57) | הדף … הֵנִיס … הוריד … השיב … דחה … הפגיש … נסח | `וְהֵסִיר יְהוָה מִמְּךָ כָּל חֹלִי` (דברים ז:טו) | absent | The Bible removes diseases, but has no map of disease-demons with homes at the ends of the cosmos, each sent back to its place. |
| III 72 | *utukka lā nêʾi utīr ēkurriš* | אוּטוּכּוּ שאין להפנותו לאחור – השיב אל האֵכּוּר | `עַד שׁוּבְךָ אֶל הָאֲדָמָה כִּי מִמֶּנָּה לֻקָּחְתָּ` (בראשית ג:יט) | equivalent | *târu* = `שוב` (reserved): each "returned" to where it came from; the only `שוב` of the tablet. The pun *lā nêʾi* / *utīr* is kept by II's `הפנה לאחור`. |
| III 75 | *išid lūti ittasaḫ kīma šammi* | את יסוד הרפיון נסח כעשב | `יַחְתְּךָ וְיִסָּחֲךָ מֵאֹהֶל וְשֵׁרֶשְׁךָ מֵאֶרֶץ חַיִּים` (תהלים נב:ז) | cognate | *nasāḫu* = `נסח` with the "root" (*išdu*) torn from the earth. |
| III 75 | *kīma šammi* | כעשב | `כִּי כֶחָצִיר מְהֵרָה יִמָּלוּ וּכְיֶרֶק דֶּשֶׁא יִבּוֹלוּן` (תהלים לז:ב) | equivalent | Evil as grass that perishes. |
| III 76 | *šitta lā ṭābta reḫâ ṣalāli* | שינה לא טובה, נִתּוּךְ יְשִׁינָה | `כִּי נָסַךְ עֲלֵיכֶם יְהוָה רוּחַ תַּרְדֵּמָה` (ישעיהו כט:י) | equivalent | Sleep "poured out" on a man (*reḫû*); Job 33:15 `בִּנְפֹל תַּרְדֵּמָה עַל-אֲנָשִׁים`. |
| III 77 | *kīma qutri … uštābil* | כעשן אשר השמים מתמלאים בו – הוֹבִילָהּ | `לָכֵן יִהְיוּ כַּעֲנַן בֹּקֶר וְכַטַּל מַשְׁכִּים הֹלֵךְ כְּמֹץ יְסֹעֵר מִגֹּרֶן וּכְעָשָׁן מֵאֲרֻבָּה` (הושע יג:ג) | equivalent | **One verse with the three images of III 77–81**: smoke (77), cloud / fog (79), dew of the night (81) – all things that vanish at dawn. |
| III 77 | *kīma qutri* | כעשן | `כְּהִנְדֹּף עָשָׁן תִּנְדֹּף` (תהלים סח:ג) | equivalent | Smoke driven off; in the Psalm the wicked, here the disease. |
| III 78 | *ūʾa ai …* | "אוי!", "אהה!" | `וְנָסוּ יָגוֹן וַאֲנָחָה` (ישעיהו לה:י) | equivalent | The cries of pain removed (objects of *ušatbi*, III 79). |
| III 79 | *ušatbi imbariš* | הקים כערפל | `מָחִיתִי כָעָב פְּשָׁעֶיךָ וְכֶעָנָן חַטֹּאותֶיךָ` (ישעיהו מד:כב) | equivalent | Lifted like mist; Hos 13:3 `כַּעֲנַן בֹּקֶר`. |
| III 80 | *lazza muruṣ qaqqadi ša sûʾiš kabtu* | את חולי הראש … אשר כְּאֶבֶן־סוּ כָּבֵד | `כִּי עֲוֹנֹתַי עָבְרוּ רֹאשִׁי כְּמַשָּׂא כָבֵד יִכְבְּדוּ מִמֶּנִּי` (תהלים לח:ה) | cognate | Head and heaviness (*kabtu* = `כבד`). |
| III 81 | *issuḫ kīma nalši mūši elīya uštessi* | נסח כטל הלילה – מעליי הרחיק | `וְכַטַּל מַשְׁכִּים הֹלֵךְ` (הושע יג:ג) | equivalent | Dew that vanishes; *nalšu* = `טל`. |
| III 81 | *elīya uštessi* | מעליי הרחיק | `כִּרְחֹק מִזְרָח מִמַּעֲרָב הִרְחִיק מִמֶּנּוּ אֶת-פְּשָׁעֵינוּ` (תהלים קג:יב) | equivalent | *nesû* Š = `הרחיק`: the psalm removes sins, Ludlul the headache – in Ludlul the two are one category. |
| III 82–83 | *tēʾātu īnāya … unammir niṭlī* | את חיפוי עיניי … האיר את הבטתי | `אָז תִּפָּקַחְנָה עֵינֵי עִוְרִים` (ישעיהו לה:ה) | equivalent | Eyes opened; Ps 146:8 `יְהוָה פֹּקֵחַ עִוְרִים`. |
| III 83 | *unammir niṭlī* | האיר את הבטתי | `מִצְוַת יְהוָה בָּרָה מְאִירַת עֵינָיִם` (תהלים יט:ט) | equivalent | "Enlightening the eyes" (*nawāru* D = `האיר`). |
| III 84–85 | *uznāya … ḫašikkiš … iptete nešmâya* | אוזניי … כחרש … פתח את שמיעתי | `וְאָזְנֵי חֵרְשִׁים תִּפָּתַחְנָה` (ישעיהו לה:ה) | equivalent | The deaf man's ears opened: *ḫašikku* ≈ `חרש`, *petû* = `פתח`; Isa 35:5–6 lists eyes, ears, legs, tongue – Ludlul eyes, ears, nose, lips, mouth, teeth, tongue. |
| III 84 | *uznāya* | אוזניי | `אָז יִגְלֶה אֹזֶן אֲנָשִׁים` (איוב לג:טז) | equivalent | God "opens the ear" in the night vision. |
| III 85 | *iptete nešmâya* | פתח את שמיעתי | `אָזְנַיִם כָּרִיתָ לִּי` (תהלים מ:ז) | equivalent | The god opens the ears. |
| III 86 | *appī … unappiqu nipissu* | אפי … נחנקה [נשיפתו] | `כֹּל אֲשֶׁר נִשְׁמַת רוּחַ חַיִּים בְּאַפָּיו` (בראשית ז:כב) | equivalent | The nose as the seat of breath (Gen 2:7). Noun restored. |
| III 87 | *upaššiḫ miḫiṣtašū-ma anappuš* | הרגיע את מחצו – וְאֶנָּפֵשׁ | `שָׁבַת וַיִּנָּפַשׁ` (שמות לא:יז) | cognate | *napāšu* = `נפש` Niphal "breathe freely, be refreshed"; there God, here the healed man. |
| III 88–89 | *šaptāya … ikpur* | שפתיי … כִּפֵּר | `וַיַּגַּע עַל פִּי וַיֹּאמֶר הִנֵּה נָגַע זֶה עַל שְׂפָתֶיךָ וְסָר עֲוֹנֶךָ וְחַטָּאתְךָ תְּכֻפָּר` (ישעיהו ו:ז) | cognate | **The lips "wiped" (*kapāru* = `כִּפֵּר`, the physical sense behind Heb. `כִּפֶּר`)**: in Isaiah the seraph's coal on the lips removes sin; in Ludlul Marduk wipes the sores of the lips – and in Ludlul sin and disease are one (58–61). |

| III 89 | *kiṣiršina ipṭur* | ואת קשרן [פטר] | `הַתֵּר אֲגֻדּוֹת מוֹטָה` (ישעיהו נח:ו) | equivalent | Healing as untying a knot (*paṭāru*, the verb of releasing sin, I 24; II 9). Verb restored. |

### The text on its own terms
- **The message dream as the god's channel outside the experts** (9–47): four dreams, each introduced by "one" (*ištânu*, *ištêt*) and "came in to me", in the *munattu*, the light sleep before dawn that was itself a source of torment (8) and becomes the channel of rescue (11, 47). Repeated dreams prove a dream true; the dreamer does not understand the first one (18: "who is the man the king sent?") and has no interpreter – his attendants are silent and confused (19–20). Lenzi ch. 7.3: Marduk, having made the professionals fail, heals through dream figures who *are* professionals.
- **The dream figures and the craft of exorcism** (23–28, 39–46): a "bathed one" sent by the famous exorcist Laluralimma of Nippur, with tamarisk, incantation water and an "incantation of life", who throws water on the patient and massages his body – the *Muššuʾu* ("rubbing") therapy; and the exorcist Ur-Nintinugga of Babylon, bearded, capped, carrying a writing board (scholarly literacy as the badge of authority), who brings Marduk's bandage in his "pure hands" and hands it to the human attendants. The poem defends the exorcist's craft at the very moment it bypasses the failed human exorcists (Lenzi ch. 7).
- **Two radiant, nameless figures** (9–14, 31–38): a towering young man "clad in radiance (*melammu*), robed in dread (*pulḫātu*)" – the divine aura worn like clothing – whose sight numbs the flesh; and a beautiful young woman, "queen of the people, noble of the land", "like a god" but keeping her distance, who sits by the patient and pronounces the two formulas of divine mercy: *aḫulap* "Enough!" (glossed by the ancient commentator as "How long?") and *lā tapallaḫ* "Fear not!" (Lenzi: perhaps the sufferer's personal god and goddess returning).
- **The god's heart "at rest" as the precondition of everything** (51–55): healing does not come because a rite succeeded but because "my lord's heart rested" and "merciful Marduk's liver was released" – heart and liver as the two seats of the god's mood (the refrain of I 2, "angry by night, relenting by day", fulfilled). Only now does the sufferer call Marduk "my lord" (51; Lenzi), and only now are his "supplications accepted" (53).
- **Sin as an object, removal as transport** (56–62): sin, guilt, punishment, crime, "negligences", oath-curse and curse are named one after another for the first time in the poem – the sufferer never confessed a known sin; at the moment of release he accepts that there was some. They are "disregarded", "walked off", their "release" granted, and the negligences "given to the wind to carry off" – like the objects of the *Šurpu* rites into which sin was transferred and then burnt, thrown into water or scattered to the wind (and like the goat of Lev 16).
- **Healing as the reversed demon list** (69–75): each of the seven attackers of II 51–57 is sent back to the home it came from – the evil wind to the base of heaven, the head-disease to the breast of the earth, the *šūlu* down to its Apsû, the "unreturnable" *utukku* "returned" to the Ekur, Lamaštu pushed up to the mountain, the chill made to face the sea's wave, debility uprooted like a plant. Disease is spatial: it has a source in the cosmos, and to cure it is to put it back where it belongs.
- **Vanishing images from the incantations** (76–83): bad sleep carried off like smoke filling the sky, the "woe!" and "alas!" lifted like fog from the earth, the head-disease removed like the dew of night, "3,600 leagues away" – the same chain (smoke, fog, 3,600 double-hours) as in a *Bīt rimki* incantation against a curse (Lenzi): the poem borrows the exorcist's own language for the cure.
- **The body as the exorcist sees it** (80–118): the healing runs from head to toe, organ by organ, each with its former symptom in a relative clause – eyes "covered with the deposit of death", ears blocked "like a deaf man's", nose choked by fever, lips with sores, mouth shut, teeth clenched "bound together", tongue tied, throat choking "as on a lump", gullet swollen, lungs rasping like a donkey, the large intestine shrunk with hunger "like a reed basket", the bent neck, the nails. The vocabulary is technical and rare (*šammāḫu*, *amīru*, *luʾu*, *ṭupuštu*, *lagāʾu*, *nakimtu*: Lenzi ch. 6), the language of the exorcists' diagnostic and therapeutic series.
- **Healing as untying and opening** (85, 89, 93, 99): wax removed and hearing "opened", the "knot" of the lips "released" (*paṭāru*, the verb of releasing sin), the "fetters" of the teeth "opened", the "lock" of the gullet "opened": disease is a binding (cf. II 95–98, the body as a prison and fetter), cure is release.
- **Domestic and craft similes** (91, 96–97, 104, `a+2`, `b+1`): the mouth scoured "like a pot", the throat once choking "on a lump", the song that "warbled like a flute", the intestine "bound like a basket", the neck made firm "like a crag" and planted "like a tree", the physique like a wrestler's – the poet heals with the images of the kitchen, the workshop, the music hall and the wrestling ground.
- **Return to society** (48, 107, 118): the god's good sign is shown "to my people"; possessions once divided are perhaps recovered by the companions (107, restored); and the nails, overgrown "like an outcast's" (*šūṣû*, "one sent out"; the commentary: "one whom Ištar sends out to the fire"), are pared – a sign of re-entry into human life (Oshima). Tablet V will complete the return at Esagil's gates.
- **The name** (44): the sufferer is named only here, in the god's message: Šubši-mešrê-Šakkan, "O Šakkan, bring wealth into being!" – a parental prayer-name that his ruin made ironic and his restoration fulfils; a man of that name is known from Kassite documents under Nazi-Maruttaš.
- **The ancient readers** (K 3291): the Assyrian commentator explains the rare words by common ones (*lagāʾu* = *šiktu*, *umṣu* = *bubūtu*, *iptennu* = *mākalû*, *amīru* = "ear-shit", *malīlu* = *embūbu*, *erēnu* = *šuršu*, *kinnû* = *šadû*), translates the Sumerian name Laluralimma into Akkadian (*Ṭābi-utul-Enlil*), hears in *aḫulap* the psalmist's "How long?", equates the negligences with sins, and preserves three lines and part of a fourth that no other manuscript has.

### Lines shared with other files
- **II 51–57 → III 69–75:** copied word for word from the table of § Tablet II (see Decisions); differences only where the Akkadian differs (direction `מן` → `אל`, the verbs of return, *agû tâmati* for *mê mīli*, *išid lūti … kīma šammi* for *itti urqīti erṣeta*). III 71 keeps II 53's demon `שׁוּלוּ` (open decision 1).
- **II body words → III 80–118** (see Decisions); **I 35 → III 87** (`הרגיע את מחצו`), **I 41 → III 51**, **I 2 → III 52** (`נתפייסה`), **I 22 → III 44–45** (`תחבושת`), **I 37 → III 56** (`גילה` / `לגלות`), **I 1 → III 57** (`הלל`), **I 20 / I 16 → III 54** (`חזר` / `חזרתו`), **I 96 → III 35, 38, 55** (`רַב!`), **I 99 → III 107** (`חילקו` / `חולקו`), **I 47 → III 102** (`הוּעַם`), **II 103 → III 87** (`לִנָּפֵשׁ לרגע` / `וְאֶנָּפֵשׁ [לרגע?]`), **II 61 → III a+1**, **II 86 → III 99**, **II 8, 74 → III 84–85**, **II 59–60, 73 → III 82**, **II 87 → III 90, 96**, **II 80 → III 86, 96**, **II 66 → III 100**.
- **Inside III:** 21/29, 22 = 30, 35/38/55 (see Decisions); 9/23/31 (`אחד` / `אחת`); 13/34 (`בא` / `באה`); 11/47 (`בתנומת שחר`); 15/26/43/47 (`שלח`).
- **Gilgameš:** 22 = 30 uses the Gilgameš formula `החלום אשר ראיתי בלילה הזה` (I 244, VII 165) with `ב־`; eBL's "cf." of III 9 to Gilg. OB Schøyen₃ a+6′ has no Hebrew in that corpus.
- **For later tablets:** IV 1 (*itbuk mānaḫtašin*) is the commentary's line "e/g"; the body words of III (*šinnu* `שן`, *lišānu* `לשון`, *luʾu* `ושט`, *ṣupru* `ציפורן`) and *athû* `שותף` (107) for V; *ṣimdu* `תחבושת`; *šiptu* `קסם` / *tû* `לחש`; *ebēbu* D `ניקה` / *elēlu* D `טיהר` (V's Esagil gates and purification); *wabālu* `הביא` / `הוביל`.

### Proposals for the shared renderings
1. **§1 names:** Laluralimma `לָלוּרָלִימָּה` (= *Ṭābi-utul-Enlil* of the commentary: delete "Tabi-utul-Enlil (… check)" as a separate figure); Ur-Nintinugga `אוּר־נִינְתִּינוּגָּה`; the sufferer `שׁוּבְּשִׁי־מֶשְׁרֵא־שַׁכַּן` (confirmed; meaning noted at III 44); Nippur `נִיפּוּר`; *bābilû* `הבבלי`.
2. **§2 personal religion / ritual, add:** *šiptu* `קסם` (*mê šipti* `מי קסם`; *šipat balāṭi* `קסם חיים`) beside *tû* `לחש`; *ramku* `טבול`; *bīnu* `אשל`; *mullilu* `מטהר`, *elēlu* D `טיהר`, *ellu* `טהור`; *ebēbu* D `ניקה`; *muššuʾu* `עִסָּה`; *kapāru* II `כִּפֵּר` (always vocalized); *unnīnu* `שוועה`; *nasḫuru* `חזרה (הטובה)`; *egītu* `שגגה`; *pišertu* `שחרור`; *lā tapallaḫ* `אל תירא`; *aḫulap* (fixed) with the commentary's *adi mati* noted; *ittu damqatu* `אות נאה`; *šipirtu* `שליחות`; *lēʾu* `לוח`.
3. **§3 body, add:** *šinnu* `שן`; *lišānu* `לשון`; *luʾu* `ושט`; *šammāḫu* `המעי הגס`; *ṣupru* `ציפורן`; *nešmû* `שמיעה`; *amīru* `שעווה`; *nipšu* `נשיפה`; *ummu* (fever) `חום`; *rīdu* `רדיפה`; *ṭummumu* `נאטם`; *sakāku* `נסתם`; *ebēṭu* `נכפת`; *esēru* `נאסר` [cognate]; *birītu* `חרצובות`; *lagabbu* `גוש`; *lagāʾu* `גלד`; *idiltu* `נעילה`; *mašqītu* `משקה`; *iptennu* `ארוחה`; *umṣu* `רעב`; *saḫḫu* `נפיחות`; *kitmuru* `נערם`; *athû* `שותף`.
4. **§5 nature / images, add:** *qutru* `עשן`; *imbaru* `ערפל`; *nalšu* `טל`; *agû* II `גל`, *agû* I `כתר`; *melammu* `זוהר`, *ḫalāpu* `מכורבל` (open decision 5); *šār bēri* `שלושת אלפים ושש מאות בֵּרוּ`; *kinnû* `צוק`; *amalu* `אַמָּלוּ` (transliterated).
5. ***wabālu*** in this corpus: `הביא` (bring to someone) / `הוביל` [cognate] (carry off, convey; Š, Št), never `נשא` while *našû* = `נשא` is in the same text; record as a deviation from Gilgameš §10 (*warû* = `הוביל` there; *warû* does not occur in Ludlul or the Theodicy) – open decision 3.
6. **Always vocalize (add):** `הֶחֱלוּנִי` (window), `הַנַּחֲלֶה` (inheritance), `עִסָּה` (dough), `כַּסַּל` (fool / loin), `הַמּוּצָא` (origin), `קָהוּ`, `הוֹבִילָהּ`, `וְאֶנָּפֵשׁ`, `יְשִׁינָה`, `הֵשִׂים`, `כְּאַמָּלוּ`, `הוּחַדָּה`, `עִלְּזָהּ`.
7. **verify / renderings:** the reserved `נחש` stays out where a reading *MUŠ* "snake" is only conjectural (III 49); `עץ` stays out for *amalu* (`a+2`); `שוב` appears in III only at 72 (*târu* D).

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **A reversal passage must be mirrored also in its verbs of disease, not only in its nouns:** the healing of III 80–118 reuses not only II's organ words but the very verbs of the attack (`חיפו` → `חיפוי`, `כוסה`, `נחנק`, `נעול` → `נעילה`, `הרפו` → `רפה`, `לא פתח את אוזני` → `פתח את שמיעתי`), and that makes the cure legible as the undoing of each symptom; a table of shared *verbs* in the earlier file would have saved a lemma search.
- **When one Akkadian word has the Hebrew cognate of a key biblical verb, keep that verb for it even inside a tablet where a near-synonym would read better:** *našû* = `נשא` and *wabālu* ≠ `נשא` made visible that Ludlul never says "carry sin" (Mic 7:18) but "gave the negligences to the wind to carry off" (Lev 16:22 is a [divergence], not an echo); likewise *kapāru* `כִּפֵּר` on the lips (Isa 6:7) and *kapāpu* / *zaqāpu* `כפף` / `זקף` (Ps 145:14).
- **Read the edition's translation against its own lemma in the commentary-only lines and the rare medical words:** eBL lemmatizes III 104 *ittarrû* as *târu* (the reserved `שוב`!), *a+1* *irmû* as *ramû* II "throw", III 71 *šūla* as "cough" where II 53 has the demon, and III 89 *pulḫâtu* as "fear", while its own translation (Foster) follows other words; translating the lemma blindly would have put the Gen 3:19 root into the intestine.
- **Bracket by the ATF, word by word, across all manuscripts:** several words that N brackets are preserved in one manuscript (III 53 *ilqû*, 54 *nasḫuršu*, 55 *iqbû* in BabNB4 / SipNB3), and a reconstructed word whose root survives in one copy is not a restoration.

## Tablet IV

### Scope
Ludlul IV, all **53 eBL labels** (`1`, `a+1`–`a+23`, `a+30`, `b+1`, `c+1`–`c+17`, `d+1`–`d+10`; note that eBL has no `a+24`–`a+29`: at least six lines are lost between `a+23` and `a+30`), in `annotated-IV.rtl.md`; intro `translation--intro-IV.rtl.md`; generated `translation-IV.rtl.md` (53 lines, 11 footnotes). Preservation: the poorest tablet of the poem. No continuous manuscript; four non-overlapping fragments – **SipNB1** (Si 728, Sippar; `a+1`–`a+12`), **BabLBSch1** (BM 46282, a Late Babylonian school tablet from Babylon; `a+13`–`a+23`, new in eBL, not in Lenzi), **NinNA1** (K 9724, Nineveh; `c+1`–`c+17`), **NinNA2** (BM 123392, Nineveh; `d+1`–`d+10`) – plus the commentary K 3291 (**NinNACom1**, rev. 23–38: `1`, `a+3`, `a+7`, `a+20`, `a+30`, `b+1`, `c+10`, `c+14`?, `c+16`, `d+6`), the unpublished school tablet BabNBSch1 (= `c+6`–`c+8`, identified by Hätinen) and the quotation BabNBQuo1 (end of `1`). `b+1` is known only from the commentary. The fragments are ordered by the commentary lines they share (Oshima 2014); the gaps between them are of unknown length. Sections (our headings): `1` the fatigue poured out; `a+1`–`a+12` knees, feet, body (Sippar fragment); `a+13`–`a+23` from prison to light, bread and honey, the body polished like bronze (school tablet); `a+30`, `b+1` the dimmed virility shines, the river ordeal (commentary lines); `c+1`–`c+17` "your god, your goddess", the slave mark, *šigû*, the street Kunuš-kadru (Nineveh fragment 1); `d+1`–`d+10` "let him who neglected Esagil learn from me" (Nineveh fragment 2).

**Concordance** (eBL's own former labels from its notes; Lenzi 2023 = Oshima 2014, with Lenzi's sections A–C and commentary letters; Lambert 1960 = BWL, not available: his commentary letters inferred from Lenzi ch. 3 n. 50 and the Tablet III concordance, Lenzi's letter + 2 up to k):

### Sources consulted
eBL `reading-edition.md` (N, T = Foster where given – a+13–a+19, a+21–a+23 have none –, L, notes, every ATF line; brackets checked against the ATF) and `glossary.md` (stems and meanings of *kasû* III, *ebēṭu* II, *būṣu* "hyaena; rock partridge", *nawû* II, *mammû* "frost; a copper mineral", *dašāpu*, *napardû*, *kīlu*, *šigû*, *qadādu*, *bâʾu*, *egû*, *muttatu*, *mašādu* "to beat; to full (cloth)", *abbuttu*, *bêru*, *ummulu*); `manuscripts.md`; Lenzi 2023 ch. 1.3 (the textual basis of Tablet IV, Oshima's sections A–C), ch. 2 (l. 4335–4450 of `full-text.txt`), ch. 3.4 (all notes, incl. the river ordeal, *abbuttu*, *šigû*, Kunuš-kadru, `d+6`), ch. 10.2 (the Ḫarran inscription's reuse of `d+6`); CCP 1.3 (K 3291 rev. 23–38, with Lenzi's translation); the law corpus (LH §2 river ordeal, §127, §146, §226–227: *muttatu*, *abbuttu*, `תספורת העבדות`); `wisdom_tools.py pars IV` (only c+14 = c+15 by identical normalization; a+3 "cf." a text outside the corpora), a scratchpad lemma index of the פירוק bullets of I–III, the Gilgameš and the other corpora (every content lemma of IV), `grep --gilg` for every new Hebrew word. Every Bible quotation printed from `תנך-מנוקד/` (`check_bible_quotes.py --show`) before quoting.

### Decisions
- **The body restored (a+3–a+12)**, with the words of II–III: *birku* `ברך` (II 78), *šēpu* `רגל` (II 79, 98), *purīdu* `ירך` (II 42), *pagru* `גוויה`, *mešrêtu* `אברים`, *minâtu* `מידות` (III 10, the dream youth's "limbs"). New: ***kasû* "bind" = `רִתֵּק`** (Nah 3:10 `רֻתְּקוּ בַזִּקִּים`), not the Gilgameš `כפת`, because *ebēṭu* = `נכפת` (III 92, 94) stands in the same line (a+3) – one Hebrew word per lemma; ***būṣu* = `חוּגְלָה`** (the commentary's "cave bird" = rock partridge; eBL T "partridge"; Gilgameš *būṣu* "hyaena" = `צבוע` is the homonym) – not the biblical `קוֹרֵא` (1 Sam 26:20, tagged), which contains the root `קרא` reserved for *nabû*; *nawû* N "turn to desert" = `שָׁמַם` (I 5 *nāmû* `שממה`); *dunnu* = `עוצמה` (Gilgameš); *šuklultu* = `שכלול` [cognate *šuklulu* ~ שכלל]; ***ḫalāpu* in a+10 = `חמק`** ("slip out like a snake", Gilgameš *ḫalāpu* I `חָמַק`), against III 12 `מכורבל` ("slip into" a garment): one lemma, two opposite senses (open decision 3); *ṣerru* = `נחש` (reserved, licensed).
- **The school tablet (a+13–a+23):** *kīlu* "confinement" = `כלא` [cognate; II 96 *kišukku* `בית־כלא` – same Hebrew root for two lemmas of one root]; *ṣītu* = `מוצא` (Gilgameš); *ekletu* = `חשכה`, *nawirtu* = `נהרה` [cognate] (Gilgameš), *šakānu ana* = `שׂם ל־` (a+16, = Isa 42:16); *napardû* Š = `הנהיר` (N `נהר`, II 118, IV a+30, V 60); *eṭûtu* = `עלטה` (Gilgameš VII 190); *akalu* `לחם`, *simat* `הראוי ל־` (Tablet I); *dašāpu* D = `המתיק` (V 58 *duššupu* `ממותק`; the root is that of *dišpu* ~ `דבש`, noted); ***lallāru* "white honey" = `נופת`** (`דבש` would hide the *dašāpu* / *dišpu* root; *lallaru* "wailer", II 42 `כמקונן`, is a homonym, noted); ***mammû* = `חלאה`** (by the commentary's gloss *šuḫtu* "verdigris, rust" and eBL's "grime"; the dictionary's "frost" noted; Ezek 24:6, 11 `חֶלְאָתָהּ` of a copper pot) and ***šuḫtu* = `חלודה`**; *zakû* D = `זיכך` [cognate זכך] (not `נקי`); *ruššû* = `אדמומית` (noun, IV 1, a+20; Gilgameš) / `אדמדם` (adjective, V 65); *siparru* = `נחושת` (EST); *nabāṭu* Š = `הבהיק` (III 91); *ebēbu* D = `ניקה` (III 26).
- **a+30 = the reversal of I 47** (*dūtī ūtammil* `אוֹנִי הוּעַם`): *dūtī ummultu ittapardi* = `אוֹנִי הַמּוּעָם – נָהַר` (*ummulu* = `מועם`, the same root `עמם`).
- **b+1, the river ordeal:** *itû* = `גבול` (Gilgameš, Enūma eliš), *nāru* = `נהר`, *dīnu* = `דין`, ***bêru* N = `ייברר`** (legal "be clarified, sifted"; `בחר` is Gilgameš *ḫiāru*). The commentary's gloss ***ḫuršān* "river ordeal"** is noted as a **homonym** of *ḫuršānu* "mountain, hill" (Gilgameš `רָמָה`) – the line itself has *itê nāri*, so no Hebrew word for *ḫuršān* is needed in the `>` line.
- **The audience (c+5–c+6):** *ilkunu* = `[האל] שלכם` (not `אֵלֵיכֶם`, which unvocalized reads "to you"); *ištarkunu* = `אֵלַתְכֶם`; *kilallān* = `שניהם`.
- **The slave mark (c+10):** ***abbuttu* = `תספורת העבדות`** (the law corpus's LH §146, §226–227); *muttatu* = `מחצית (הראש)` (LH §127 `מַחֲצִיתוֹ`; Foster / Lenzi "forehead" in the apparatus); *mašādu* N = `*נחבטתי*` ("to beat; to full cloth", italic; Lenzi: "combed"?, Mayer: "struck by hemiplegia"); ***pašāru* N "be released" = `שוחרר`**, so that it pairs with *pišertu* = `שחרור` (III 60; IV c+16); the renderings' *pašāru* N "relent" = `נתפייס` (I 2, III 52) is the other sense of the lemma.
- **c+11–c+16:** *erinnu* "neck-stock" = `סוגר` (Ezek 19:9); *šibbu* = `חגור` (Gilgameš); ***qadādu* = `קִידָּה`** [cognate קדד, Gen 24:26]; ***šigû* transliterated `שִׁיגּוּ`** (the name of a penitential prayer genre, "Mercy!"; possibly the source of `שִׁגָּיוֹן`, Ps 7:1, Hab 3:1 – tagged [cognate?]); ***Kunuš-kadru* = `כּוּנוּשׁ־כַּדְרוּ`** (street name transliterated, meaning "Submit, O arrogant!" and the commentary's "narrow street" in the note); *bâʾu* = `עבר` (I 80).
- **d+1–d+10:** Zarpānītu `צַרְפָּנִיתוּ` (§1); *lā pāliḫ ili* = `לֹא־יְרֵא אֵלוֹ` (renderings §2 *pāliḫ ili* = `יְרֵא אֵל`); *egû* = `שגה` (§2; III 61 *egâtu* `שגגות`; the commentary: = *ḫaṭû* "sin"); ***ina qāti amāru* = literal `בידי יִרְאֶה`** (the idiom "learn from my example" explained in the note; the Hebrew keeps the hand and the seeing, which V 69 and V 113 take up); *amatu* = `דבר`; ***maruštu* = `תלאה`** (Gilgameš *marṣātu* `תלאות`; also V 113); *epēšu* stative 1 sg. = `עוּשֵּׂיתִי`; *šērtu* = `אשמה` (III 56).
- Parenthesized words of the normalization are not translated; c+14 and c+15 (identical normalization) have identical Hebrew, and the extra *-ia* of the c+15 manuscript is in the note.

### Open decisions for the user
1. ***būṣu* (a+3) = `חוּגְלָה`** (rabbinic/modern "partridge") vs the biblical partridge `קוֹרֵא` (1 Sam 26:20; Jer 17:11), which would put the reserved root `קרא` into the line; or `צבוע` (eBL's lemma "hyaena", against the commentary).
2. ***mammû* (a+20) = `חלאה`** (by the commentary's *šuḫtu* "rust" and the copper image of a+21) vs the dictionary's "frost" (`קרח`), which makes the line read "rubbed off the ice".
3. ***ḫalāpu*: III 12 `מכורבל` ("slip into a garment") and IV a+10 `חמק` ("slip out like a snake")** – one lemma, two Hebrew words by sense; alternative: one word for both (`חמק` in Gilgameš).
4. ***mašādu* (c+10) = `*נחבטתי*`** (eBL "beat", CAD "struck"; also "to full cloth") vs Lenzi's "combed" (of the slave hair-style) or Mayer's "struck by hemiplegia".
5. ***pašāru* N "be released" (c+10) = `שוחרר`** (with *pišertu* `שחרור`) – a third Hebrew root for *pašāru* beside `פשר` (dreams, Gilgameš) and `נתפייס` (relent, renderings §2). The coordinator may prefer to record the sense split in §2.
6. ***šigû* transliterated `שִׁיגּוּ`** vs a translation (`קינת תחינה`): transliteration keeps the genre name and the possible link to `שִׁגָּיוֹן`.
7. ***kīlu* = `כלא`** shares the Hebrew root with *kišukku* `בית־כלא` (II 96) – deliberate (cognate), but two lemmas.

- 2026-10-19: read the brief, the guidelines (whole), corpus.md, renderings.md (incl. §10.1–10.3), the Gilgameš renderings (§0–§21 and the settled §12), the format model (Gilg. VII 1–60 and `annotated-III.rtl.md` header and entries), all `>` lines of I–III with eBL's N (scratchpad dump), `project.md` § Tablet I–III (decisions, open decisions, own terms, shared lines, proposals, lessons); the whole eBL edition of IV (all ATF) and `manuscripts.md`, glossary entries of the rare words; Lenzi ch. 1.3, 2, 3.4, 10.2; CCP 1.3 rev. 23–38. `pars IV`; a scratchpad lemma index of I–III and a cross-corpus lemma search (`xc.py`) for every content lemma of IV.
- Drafted all 53 entries in one file; every Bible verse printed with `--show` before quoting.
- Pass (a) fidelity: brackets re-checked against the ATF word by word (a+3 `[כפותות]` – only the ending survives in the commentary; a+5 `[רג]ליי`; a+10 `כ[נחש]`; a+15 `כ[לא]`; a+16 `[לנהרה?]`; a+18 `[היטיב]`; a+21 `[את חלודתי]`; c+5 `[האל]`; c+9 `החייני` unbracketed – the root survives; c+10 `שוחר[רתי]`; c+16 `[כּוּנוּשׁ־]כַּדְרוּ`; d+5 `[אֵלָתוֹ]`); additions in `( )`: c+10 `(הראש)`; uncertain words marked `?` or italic (a+10, c+4, c+10, c+11, c+12).
- Pass (b) consistency: every shared lemma checked against I–III (`ברך`, `רגל`, `ירך`, `גוויה`, `אברים`, `מידות`, `שפה`, `שפשף`, `הבהיק`, `און`/`הועם`, `נכפת`, `ניקה`, `שוועה`, `שחרור`, `אשמה`, `עבר`, `ירא`, `שגה`); collisions found and avoided: *kasû* (`כפת` taken by *ebēṭu*) → `רתק`; partridge `קורא` (root `קרא`) → `חוגלה`; `דבש` (root of *dašāpu*) → *lallāru* `נופת`; `אֵלֵיכֶם` ("to you") → `[האל] שלכם`; `בחר` (Gilgameš *ḫiāru*) → *bêru* `ייברר`. Reserved roots by hand: `שׂם` (a+16, c+8 = *šakānu*), `נחש` (a+10 = *ṣerru*), `טוב` (1, a+18 = *ṭiābu*), `ניקה` (a+23 = *ebēbu*); no `שוב`, `קרא`, `טיט`, `עפר`.
- Pass (c) Hebrew: read the generated `translation-IV.rtl.md`; changed the c+ heading and the header (`אֵלְכֶם` → `האל שלכם`); vocalized the ambiguous forms (`רוּתְּקוּ`, `כְּחוּגְלָה`, `שָׁמְמוּ`, `שָׂם`, `אוֹנִי הַמּוּעָם – נָהַר`, `יִיבָּרֵר`, `קִידָּה`, `שִׁיגּוּ`, `לֹא־יְרֵא`, `יִרְאֶה`, `עוּשֵּׂיתִי`).
- `check_bible_quotes.py`: 0 problems after the first run's ketiv/qere quotations (Isa 52:2, Ps 51:4) were shortened. `wisdom_tools.py build`; `verify IV V --strict`: no problems.

### The text on its own terms
- **Healing is a social and legal process, not only a bodily one** (`a+3`–`d+10`): after the body (knees, feet, limbs, mouth, lips, skin) comes release from "confinement" (`a+15`), the river ordeal "where people's case is decided" (`b+1`), the removal of the slave hair-style (`c+10`), the neck-stock perhaps replaced by a belt (`c+11`–`c+12`), a public walk through a named street "in a state of release" (`c+16`), and a public address to the community (`c+5`–`d+7`). The sufferer re-enters society by stages, each ritually or legally marked.
- **The river ordeal as public confirmation** (`b+1`): the Babylonian ordeal plunged the accused into the divine River, which "cleared" (*ubbubu*, the verb of `a+23` `ניקני`) or "seized" him (LH §2). Oshima: here it did not prove innocence but displayed Marduk's pardon; the ancient commentator recognized the phrase *itê nāri* as *ḫuršān*, the technical term.
- **Illness as slavery** (`c+10`): the sufferer had worn the *abbuttu*, the half-shaved hair-style that marked a slave (LH §146, §226–227); his release is told as manumission. The commentator glosses *abbuttu* with *birītu* "fetter", tying the line to the fetters of II 97–98 and III 93.
- **The body as metal and as cult statue** (`a+20`–`a+21`): the commentator read "he wiped off the *mammû*" as cleaning the patina of a copper object, and the next line has the body polished "like bronze". The image is that of refurbishing a divine statue (cf. V 38 *tēdištu*, the word for renewing statues): the sufferer is restored as the temple's images are.
- **The snake that slips its skin** (`a+10`, if read so): the renewed body "slipped out like a snake" – the same animal that in Gilgameš XI steals rejuvenation is here the image of the sufferer's renewal.
- **Darkness made light, prison opened, food sweet again** (`a+15`–`a+19`): the newly identified school tablet adds the classic triad of the thanksgiving genre – prison, darkness, hunger – each reversed (`a+18` "bread, befitting people" made good in the mouth: eating bread is what makes one human, as for Enkidu in Gilgameš).
- **Penitential prayer in the temple** (`c+14`–`c+15`): *šigû* is a cry ("Mercy!") and a genre of penitential prayer recited in the temple after misfortune; Hätinen sees a *šigû* ritual at Esagil as a central theme of Tablet IV, with Tablet V's procession as its sequel.
- **A named street with a speaking name** (`c+16`): Kunuš-kadru, "Submit, O arrogant!", a (narrow) street of Babylon: the man who submitted walks it free. Walking in the street is the Babylonian sign of full recovery (a *tamītu* query asks of a dying man "will he walk his city's street in health?").
- **The didactic turn** (`c+5`–`d+7`): the speaker addresses "your god, your goddess", the "non-fearers of god and goddess", and "whoever was negligent toward Esagil": "let him see by my hand". The poem presents itself as a lesson for a community; the Ḫarran inscription of Nabonidus later reworked this into a warning to future kings not to neglect Sîn's temple (Lenzi ch. 10.2).
- **The ancient commentator at work** (K 3291): *mānaḫtu* = "illness", *būṣu* = "cave bird" (choosing between homonyms "hyaena" / "partridge"), *šuklultu* = "stature", *mammû* = "patina", *mašāšu* = *kapāru*, *dūtu* = "appearance", *itê nāri* = "river ordeal", *abbuttu* = "fetter", Kunuš-kadru = "narrow street", *egû* = "to sin": eight of the tablet's lines survive only or mainly because he explained them.

### Lines shared with other files
- **Reversals of I–III:** a+3 ← II 78 (`ברכיי`), a+5 ← II 79, 98 (`רגליי`), a+8 ← II 67, 105 (`אבריי`), a+9 ← III 10 (`מידות`), a+18 ← II 19, 88 (`לחם`, `דגן … כלענה`), a+19 ← I 117, II 85 (`שפתיי`), a+20 ← III 91, 95 (`שפשף`), a+21 ← III 91 (`הבהיק`), a+23 ← III 26 (`לנקותך` → `ניקני`), a+30 ← I 47 (`אוֹנִי הוּעַם` → `אוֹנִי הַמּוּעָם – נָהַר`), c+6, d+2–d+3 ← I 43–44, II 4–5, 112–113 (`אֵלִי`, `אֵלָתִי`), c+7 ← III 53 (`שוועה`), c+10, c+16 ← III 60 (`שחרור`), c+16 ← I 80 (`צועד` / `צעדתי`), d+4–d+5 ← II 18, 25 (`ירא`), d+6 ← III 61 (`שגגות` → `שגה`), I 39 (`אֲלַמֵּד`).
- **Inside IV:** c+14 = c+15 (identical normalization, identical Hebrew).
- **For Tablet V:** *pišertu* / *pašāru* N (`שחרור` / `שוחרר`); *maruštu* `תלאה` (V 113); *ina qāti amāru* `בידי יראה` → V 69 `ראו בני בבל`, V 113 `[מי אשר תלאה] ראה`; *napardû* `נהר` (V 60); *dašāpu* `המתיק` → V 58 `ממותק`; *ruššû* `אדמומית` → V 65 `אדמדם`; Esagil, Zarpānītu first vocalized here and again in V.
- **Gilgameš / other corpora:** *ḫalāpu* `חמק` (Gilgameš); *būṣu* homonym (Gilgameš `צבוע`); *ḫuršānu* homonym (Gilgameš `רמה`); *abbuttu*, *muttatu* (law corpus LH §127, §146, §226–227); LH §2's river ordeal (`זיככו`, *ebēbu* D – our `ניקה`).

### Proposals for the shared renderings
1. **§1 names:** Kunuš-kadru `כּוּנוּשׁ־כַּדְרוּ` (street; meaning in a note); Zarpānītu `צַרְפָּנִיתוּ` and Esagil `אֵסַגִּיל` confirmed.
2. **§2 ritual / personal religion, add:** *šigû* `שִׁיגּוּ` (transliterated genre name; ↔ `שִׁגָּיוֹן` noted); *qadādu* `קִידָּה` / `קד` [cognate]; *egû* `שגה` confirmed (with the commentary's *ḫaṭû*); *ina qāti amāru* `בידי ראה` (literal, idiom in the note); *lā pāliḫ ili* `לֹא־יְרֵא אֵל`; the river ordeal *itê nāri* `גבול הנהר` and the commentary's *ḫuršān* "river ordeal" (homonym of *ḫuršānu* "mountain" `רמה`).
3. **§3 body / society, add:** *kasû* "bind" `רתק` (beside *ebēṭu* `נכפת`); *abbuttu* `תספורת העבדות` (law corpus); *muttatu* `מחצית`; *mašādu* `*נחבט*`; *pašāru* N "be released" `שוחרר` (beside *pišertu* `שחרור`; "relent" stays `נתפייס`); *kīlu* `כלא`; *erinnu* `סוגר`; *šibbu* `חגור`; *dunnu* `עוצמה`; *šuklultu* `שכלול`; *nawû* N `שמם`; *ummulu* `מועם`; *maruštu* `תלאה`.
4. **§5 nature / images, add:** *būṣu* (bird) `חוגלה`; *lallāru* "white honey" `נופת`; *dašāpu* `המתיק`; *mammû* `חלאה` (open decision 2); *šuḫtu* `חלודה`; *siparru* `נחושת`; *eṭûtu* `עלטה`; *ekletu* `חשכה`; *nawirtu* `נהרה`; *zakû* D `זיכך`.
5. **Always vocalize (§8), add:** `רוּתְּקוּ`, `כְּחוּגְלָה`, `שָׁמְמוּ`, `נָהַר` (verb; vs "river"), `הַמּוּעָם`, `יִיבָּרֵר`, `קִידָּה`, `שִׁיגּוּ`, `יִרְאֶה` (see; vs "fear"), `לֹא־יְרֵא`; and `[האל] שלכם` for *ilkunu* (never unvocalized `אליכם`).

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **A fragmentary tablet needs a concordance with four columns, not two:** eBL renumbered Tablet IV twice (its own former `d+`–`j+` labels, now `1`, `a+`–`d+`), Lenzi/Oshima use sections A–C plus commentary letters, Lambert used other letters; and a new manuscript (BabLBSch1) inserted eleven lines that no concordance had. Build the table from the edition's own "previous line numbers" notes and the shared commentary lines.
- **In a commentary-only line, the commentary's gloss can decide between homonyms that the edition's lemma confuses** (*būṣu* "hyaena" vs "partridge"; *mammû* "frost" vs "patina"; *ḫuršānu* "mountain" vs "river ordeal"): translate the line by the gloss, name the lemma in the apparatus, and record the choice as an open decision.
- **Check the reserved roots inside ordinary nouns of the natural Hebrew** (the partridge `קוֹרֵא` contains `קרא`; `אֵלֵיכֶם` "your gods" reads "to you"): grep the planned Hebrew of every noun, not only verbs.

## Tablet V

### Scope
Ludlul V, all **120 eBL lines**, in `annotated-V.rtl.md`; intro `translation--intro-V.rtl.md`; generated `translation-V.rtl.md` (120 lines, 18 footnotes). Preservation: 1–90 mostly preserved (1–24 on AššNA2, BabLB2, BabLB3; 25–53 on the great Babylon tablet BabLB1 = Lenzi's MS ABab, AššNA1 = VAT 9303, AššNA4; 54–90 on AššNA1–3, SipNBSch1, KišLBSch1, HuzNA1); 91–112 very broken (two non-overlapping manuscripts, AššNA4 and BabLB3, whose relative position is uncertain, then BabLB1 and AššNA3); 113–120 (AššNA3, BabLB1, BabLB2) the poem's close, with traces of a colophon on BabLB2. The commentary K 3291 (NinNACom1, rev. 39–44) explains 14–17, 23, 30; a Babylonian quotation tablet (BabLBQuo1) has 14–15; an Uruk commentary (UrkLBQuo1) quotes 17. eBL's parallels: 18 = RS 25.460 43′ (the Akkadian hymn to Marduk from Ugarit, "the Ugarit righteous sufferer"); "cf." links for 78, 93, 98, 107 to texts outside the corpora (Si.4 = a royal inscription). Sections (our headings): 1–8 "My lord relieved me" – from the pit and the Ḫubur; 9–24 right and left hand, the lion, the grave-digger, the mourners, the gloaters; 25–29 street and square; 30–36 "had not the lord …" – "yes!"; 37–40 washing, bathing, prostration; 41–53 the twelve gates of Esagil; 54–66 prayer, incense, sacrifice, libation, offerings to the temple's spirits and doors; 67–76 the Babylonians see: "Marduk can revive from the grave"; 77–90 "all who exist – praise Marduk!"; 91–112 blessings for king Nazi-Maruttaš, Šubši-mešrê-Šakkan; 113–120 the close.

**Concordance** (Lenzi 2023 = Oshima 2014; SAACT 7 (Annus & Lenzi 2010) and Lambert 1960 from Lenzi's notes):

Manuscript sigla (Lenzi → eBL): ABab = BabLB1; V.BBab = BabLB2 (BM 34650); V.CBab = BabLB3 (BM 77253); V.DAš = AššNA1 (VAT 9303); V.EAš = AššNA2; V.FAš = AššNA3; V.GAš = AššNA4 (KAR 116, lost); V.jKiš = KišLBSch1; V.iSip = SipNBSch1; ComNin = NinNACom1.

### Sources consulted
eBL `reading-edition.md` (N, T = Foster, L, notes, every ATF line; brackets checked against the ATF) and `glossary.md` (meanings and stems of *esēpu*, *karāšu* II, *dekû* II, *šadādu*, *kašû* II, *wašāru*, *belû* II, *ṭarādu*, *manû* IV, *mesû* II, *elēlu* II, *rimku*, *salāḫu*, *šâlu*, *maḫāru*, *paqādu*, *našāqu*, *ṣelû* II, *palāqu*, *šapṭu*, *etamdu*, *angubbû*, *deššû*, *elēṣu*, *tarqītu*, *qerītu*, *ṣarāšu* (no meaning given), *elēpu*, *seḫpu*, *rêṣu*, *lalû*, *buʾʾû*, *tēmīqu*, *utnēnu* I–II, *kalû* V, *napsamu*, *gerru* II, *ākilu*, *ekēmu*, *ṣamādu* II, *pašāḫu*, *eṭemmūtu*, *šaknu*, *kânu*, *dalīlu* [KA.TAR!], *zamāru*, *tanittu*, *ištartu*, *lūman*, *anna*, *bakkû*, *bakkītu*, *mukaššidu*, *marru* II, *ḫādû*); `manuscripts.md`; Lenzi 2023 ch. 2 (l. 4452–4720 of `full-text.txt`), ch. 3.5 (all Tablet V notes: the quatrains, *lūman lā*, *anni*, the gates and their order, the learned hermeneutics of the gate names after Lenzi 2015b, the offerings, the third-person shift, Nazimaruttaš), ch. 10.2 (Ḫarran: V 69–73, Schaudig 2021 on V 73); CCP 1.3 (K 3291 rev. 39–44); the law corpus (LH §2), the creation corpus (Enūma eliš: *šigaru*, *igisû*, *šulmānu*, *erbu*, *dalīlu*, *mumaʾʾeru*, *baʾūlātu*), the Gilgameš corpus (*dannatu*, *qāta ṣabātu*, *ekēmu*, *šadādu*, *qēbiru*, *ṣurru*, *mesû* + *malû*, *kurunnu*, *marû*, *ṭabāḫu*, *ḫimētu*, *lalû*, *papallu*, *nizmatu*, *asakku*, *ḫašurru*) and the flood corpus (*marru* `מעדר`, *ṭarādu* `שיגר`); `wisdom_tools.py pars V`, the scratchpad lemma index of I–III and the cross-corpus lemma search for every content lemma of V, `grep --gilg`. Every Bible quotation printed from `תנך-מנוקד/` before quoting.

### Decisions
- **Gate names (41–53) – the method, fixed once:** the name is transliterated from eBL's normalization (`../renderings.md` §1: *Kā-…*), vocalized, followed in the `>` line by a parenthesized, quoted Hebrew translation of its Sumerian elements, worded with the Hebrew of the line's gift, so that "in the Gate of X I received X" is visible: `בְּכָא־חֶגַל ("שער השפע") – שפע …`. Same name, same gloss (44 = 52 `כָּא־סִילִימָה ("שער השלום")`; 41 = 46 `כָּא־אוּתוּ־אֵאָה ("שער צאת השמש")`). Where the pun works through sign values, the gloss gives the analytic reading and the note gives the conventional meaning (50 KA.TAR "praise" read as KA "mouth" + TAR "ask": `("שער הפה השואל")` – `שָׁאַל פִּי`). The twelve: *ka-utu-eʾa* `כָּא־אוּתוּ־אֵאָה` "Gate of Sunrise"; *ka-ḫegal* `כָּא־חֶגַל` "abundance" → `שפע`; *ka-lammara-bi* `כָּא־לַמָּרָא־בִּי` "protective goddess" → `אלת־מגן`; *ka-silima* `כָּא־סִילִימָה` "peace, well-being" → `שַׁלְמוֹן` (44) / reconciliation (52); *ka-namtila* `כָּא־נַמְתִּילָה` "life" → `חיים`; *ka-ude-babbara* `כָּא־אוּדֶה־בַּבַּרָּה` "shining wonder" → `האירו`; *ka-namtaga-duḫa* `כָּא־נַמְתַּגָּה־דוּחָה` "release of sin" → `חובי נפטר`; *ka-a-sikila* `כָּא־אָה־סִיקִילָה` "pure water" → `מי טהרה`; *ka-katara* `כָּא־כַּתַרָּה` "praise" / "mouth asks" → `שאל פי`; *ka-ašer-duḫuda* `כָּא־אָשֶׁר־דוּחוּדָה` "release of sighing" → `נפטרה אנחתי`; *ka-ḫili-sud* `כָּא־חִילִי־סוּד` "sprinkled with luxuriance" (`שהוזה עליו עונג`, *lalû* `עונג`).
- **Offerings and libations (54–66), with renderings §2:** *supû* `תחינה`; ***tēmīqu* `עתירה`** (open decision 3); ***utnēnu* (noun 40, verb 54) `התפללות` / `התפלל`** (the other prayer words are taken: *teslītu* `תחנון`, *ikribu* `תפילה`, *unnīnu* `שוועה`, *sullû* `התחנן`); *qutrinnu* `קטורת` [cognate]; *ṣelû* Š `הצית` (not `הקטיר`); ***maḫāru* Š "present" `הקריב`** (by sense, tagged [equivalent] with Lev 7:12; III 74 Š "make confront" `הפגיש` is the other sense; `הגיש` is *ṭeḫû* D); *erbu* `הכנסה` (Enūma eliš); ***ṭātu* `שׁוֹחַד`** (the dictionary's sense; 1 Kgs 15:19; open decision 4); *igisû* `תשורות` (Enūma eliš); *etamdu* (Gt of *emēdu*) `סמוכות (זו לזו)` (*emēdu* = `סמך`, II 58); *palāqu* D `שחט` [equivalent]; *ṭabāḫu* `טבח` [cognate]; *līʾu* `פר`; *marû* `מריא` [cognate]; *šapṭu* `(צאן) *משובח*`; *naqû* Gtn `ניסכתי וניסכתי` (no `שוב ושוב`); *kurunnu* `סובא מובחר`; *duššupu* `ממותק` (IV a+19 `המתיק`); *karānu* `יין`; *taqqītu* `נסך`; *mākālû* `סעודה`; *deššû* `דשנות`; *elēṣu* Š `העליץ`; *sippu* `מזוזה`, *šigaru* `בריח`, *mēdelu* `מנעול`, *daltu* `דלת` (Neh 3:3's triad); *ḫimētu* `חמאה`; *ṭuḫdu* `רוב`; *ašnan* `דגן`; ***tarqītu* `מרקחת`** [cognate רקח]; *ḫašurru* `חַשׁוּרוּ` (transliterated, Gilgameš); *šēdu* / *lamassu* `רוח־המגן` / `אלת־המגן`; *angubbû* `אַנְגוּבּוּ` (transliterated); *libittu* `לבנה` [cognate].
- **The refrain particle (31–33):** ***anna* / *anni* = `הֵן!`** (vocalized) – a short, solemn "yes!" (Mishnaic `הֵן` "yes"), standing at the line end as in the Akkadian; rejected: `כֵּן` (the root כון is *kânu*, 83), `אכן` (II 28 *šumma*), `אָמְנָם`.
- **"Had it not been" (30, 34):** ***lūman lā* = `לוּלֵא`** – the Hebrew particle is built like the Akkadian (*lū* + *man* + *lā* / `לוּ` + `לֹא`): [cognate] for *lū* ~ `לוּ`; the parallel Ps 94:17 etc. is then structural. *wašāru* D = `התיר` (Ps 146:7; `שלח` is *šapāru* here); *ezēbu* = `עזב` (reserved cognate), meaning "leave (alive)" – the Hebrew keeps the ambiguity Lenzi sees ("abandoned" / "spared").
- **The rescue (1–24):** *pašāḫu* D `הרגיע` (III 87); ***ṣamādu* "bandage" = `חבש`** (the root of *ṣimdu* `תחבושת`, I 22, III 44–45; Job 5:18, Hos 6:1; Gilgameš *ṣamādu* "harness" = `רתם` is another sense; III 41 *apāru* "wear headgear" = `חבוש` – a Hebrew homonym, open decision 2); *paṭāru* `פטר`; *bulluṭu* `החיה`; *ḫaštu* `שחת` (I 93); *ekēmu* `חטף` (5, 17, 18; Gilgameš); *esēpu* `אסף`; *karašû* `אסון` (I 14); *dekû* `העיר` (Gilgameš); *Ḫubur* `חֻבּוּר`; *šadādu* `משך`; *dannatu* `מצוקה` and *qāta ṣabātu* `אחז ביד` (Gilgameš OB, II 112); *imniš* / *šumēla* `בימין` / `בשמאל`; *maḫāṣu* `מחץ`, *māḫiṣu* `מוחץ`; *šaqû* D `הגביה` (II 5); *nadû* `השליך` (G and Š, the Š with `(מידו)`); *gerru* `כפיר` (the commentary: = UR.MAḪ "lion"; Gilgameš *mīrānu* `גור` kept apart); *napsamu* `רסן` (the commentary: bit for a horse's mouth; `מחסום` is *napraku*); *mukaššidu* `מְנִיסִי` (*kašādu* D `הֵנִיס`, III 70); *aṣpu* `קלע`, *assukku* `אבן־קלע`; *saḫāru* D `החזיר`; *qēbiru* `קובר` [cognate]; *marru* `מעדר` (flood corpus); *bakkû* / *bakkītu* `הַבּוֹכֶה` / `הַבּוֹכָה עליי` (vocalized apart); *ṣurru* `צור`; *qubû* `קינה`; *kalû* `כלא`; *ḫādû* / *ḫādītu* `השמח` / `השמחה לאידי` (II 117–118); *ūʾa* `אוי` (III 78).
- **30–36:** *belû* `כבה` (Gilgameš); *erkallu* `אֶרְכַּלָּה` (II 47); ***ṭarādu* `שיגר`** (flood corpus; `רדף` is *redû*); *eṭemmūtu* `אִטּוּת` (vocalized; *eṭemmu* `אִט`); *asakku* `אַסַּכּוּ` (Gilgameš); *manû* N `נמנה` (35 and 46: counted with the demon / with the living); *šalamtu* `נבלה`.
- **37–40:** *mesû* `רחץ`, *malû* `פרע` (Gilgameš XI 254); *elēlu* D `טיהר`; *rimku* `טבילה` (*ramāku* `טבל`); *tēdištu* `חידוש` [cognate]; *ummu* `אם`; *teslītu* `תחנון`; *appa labānu* `ליטוף אף` (literal, renderings §2).
- **67–90:** *qerītu* `משתה`; *mārū Bābili* `בני בבל`; *qibītu* `אמרה`; *wapû* Š `גילה`, Št `נגלה`; *narbû* `גדולה`; *amār šamšīšu* `לראות את שִׁמְשׁוֹ` (pun on AMAR.UTU noted); *etēqu* `חלף` (G) / `העביר` (Š, 26); *ša lā … mannu / ela … ayyītu* = I 35–36's `שלא … מי / מבלעדי … איזו`; *mītūtu* `מיתה` with I 38's `(מ)`; *qiāšu* `העניק`; *leʾû* `יכול`; ***amrat* (stative of *amāru*) = `רְאוּיָה`** (root ראה: "seen fit, experienced"); *eṭēru* `חלץ`; *ēma* `באשר`; *rapāšu* Gt `רחבים`; *gīru* `גִּירָה` (transliterated fire god); *napāḫu* N `מנופח` (I 51); *ziāqu* `נשב`; *karāṣu* `קרץ` [cognate], *kerṣu* `קריצה`; *šakittu napšata* `שׂוּמֵי נפש`; *petâ purīda* `פתוחי ירך` (II 42); *apâtu* `הרבים` (II 38); *dalālu* `הלל` (I 1; `הַלְּלוּ` vocalized; since the 2026-10-23 harmonization `פֵּאֵר`, `פארו`); *bêlu* `משל`; *reʾû* `רעה` [cognate]; *dadmū* `יישובים`; *mīlu* `גאות` (II 56); *nagbu* `מעמקים` (Gilgameš I 1); *parakku* `דוכן`; *rêṣu* D `עזר` (*rēṣu* `עוזר`, I 95); *maqtu* `הנופל` (I 14).
- **91–120:** *nannāru* `נַנָּרוּ` (transliterated, guidelines §6.7); *bursaggu* `בּוּרְסַגּוּ`; *palû* `שלטון`; *elēpu* Št `השתרג`; ***šuršu* `שורש` [cognate]** (but see open decision 6); *ṣarāšu* `*ינביט*`; *papallu* `שתיל`; Nazi-Maruttaš `נָזִי־מָרוּתַּשׁ`; *ṣalmat qaqqadi* `שחורי הראש`; *rēmu* `רחמים` [cognate, "womb / mercy"]; *šarḫu* `מפואר`; *nabû* N `ייקרא` (reserved, licensed); *damiqtu* `חסד`; *lalû* `עונג`; *šebû* `שבע` [cognate]; *rašû* `קנה`; *nizmatu* `מאווי`; *baʾūlātu* `נתינים`; Sumer `שׁוּמֶר` (vocalized), Akkad `אַכַּד` (biblical); *mumaʾʾeru* `מצוה` (Enūma eliš); ***maruštu* `תלאה`** (IV d+8); *arnu* `עוון`; *mānaḫtu* `יגיע` (IV 1), *pašāḫu* Št `יירגע`; ***kabātu* D "honour" = `כיבד` [cognate]** (the "heavy" hand of I 9, III 1 becomes honour); *šulmāniš* `בשלום`; *ṭūb šīri u ḫūd libbi* `טוב בשר ושִׂמְחַת לב`; *buʾʾû* `חיפש`; *zamāru* `זמר`; ***dalīlu* `הילולים`** (*dalīla dalālu* `הללו את הילוליו`; since the 2026-10-23 harmonization `תִּפְאֶרֶת`, `פארו את תפארת[ו?]`); *tanittu* `תהילה`; *ṭiābu* `טוב` [cognate].
- **Reserved strings licensed by lemma:** `קבר` (18, 41, 75), `עזב` (34), `טוב` (55, 66, 118, 120), `רחמים` (104), `חסד` (106), `ייקרא` (106), `שׁב`/`שבה` (41, 102: *târu*), `שׂים` (77, 81), `שלום` / `שלמון` / `בשלום` (44, 52, 116: *šulmānu*, *šulmāniš*, and the gate name *ka-silima* – see the tool fix in the Progress log).

### Open decisions for the user
1. **The gate method** (41–53): transliteration + quoted Hebrew gloss in the line (chosen) vs transliteration only with the gloss in a footnote (cleaner line, pun invisible) vs a Hebrew name only (`בשער השפע – שפע`), which would contradict renderings §1.
2. ***ṣamādu* (2) = `חבש`** (root of *ṣimdu* `תחבושת`) – the same Hebrew verb as III 41 *apāru* ("wearing his crown", `חבוש כתרו`, from Gilgameš). Alternative: III 41 to `עוטה` / `חבוש` stays and *ṣamādu* = `קשר` (but *kaṣāru* = `קשר`, I 65).
3. ***tēmīqu* (54) = `עתירה`** – shares the root with *bâlu* `עתר` (II 8). Alternatives: `העמקה` (the cognate root עמק of *emēqu*, odd Hebrew), or `תפילה` (taken by *ikribu*).
4. ***ṭātu* (56) = `שׁוֹחַד`** (dictionary "bribe, baksheesh; voluntary gift to a king"; 1 Kgs 15:19) – strong in Hebrew; alternative `מתנה` (Foster "gift"), losing the Deut 10:17 contrast.
5. ***anna* = `הֵן!`** (31–33) vs `כֵּן!` or `אכן`.
6. ***šuršu* (97) = `שורש`** [cognate], while III a+1 rendered *erēnu* "root" also `שורש` (following the commentary's *erēnu* = *šuršu*): two lemmas, one Hebrew word; proposal: III a+1 *erēnu* → `עיקר` or `בסיסו`.
7. ***maḫāru* Š = `הקריב`** (56) – a Torah sacrificial verb by sense (tagged); alternative `הגשתי` (collides with *ṭeḫû* D, III 68).
8. ***wašāru* D (30) = `התיר`** – Lenzi's negative reading ("neglected") is in the note; alternative `הרפה` (but `הרפו` is *ramû*, II 61).
9. **V 34 *ezēbu* `עזבני`** in the sense "left (me) alive": keep the ambiguous reserved cognate (chosen), or add `(בחיים)`.
10. **V 59 four recipients or two** (Lenzi / CAD vs Lambert / Foster): the Hebrew lists four with commas; the alternative reading needs `אנגובו, (שומרי) לבנת אסגיל`.

### Progress log
- 2026-10-19: read the whole eBL edition of V (all ATF) and `manuscripts.md`, glossary entries of the rare words; Lenzi ch. 2, 3.5 (all notes), 10.2; CCP 1.3 rev. 39–44. `pars V` (18 = RS 25.460 43′; 78, 93, 98, 107 "cf." outside the corpora); the scratchpad lemma index of I–III (every content lemma of V; `lemV.txt`) and the cross-corpus search (`xc.py`); every Bible verse printed with `--show` before quoting.
- Drafted 1–120 in one file after IV, with the reversal table of I–II beside me (the gloaters, the grave, the mourners, the hand, the head, the street, the gate, the god and goddess).
- Pass (a) fidelity: brackets re-checked against the ATF (1–4 `[אדו]ני`; 5 `[מן? השחת?]`; 7 `[מן האסון]`; 9 unbracketed – BabLB3 keeps *dan-n[a-ti]*; 22 `לא[ידי]`; 26 `בכיכר` unbracketed – *re-* and *-bit* in two manuscripts; 27 `[שער העיר?]`; 28 `[הגביר?]`; 37 `טי[הרתי?]`; 38 `והבה[קה]`; 42 `נ[תיישר?]`; 43 `[אליי]`; 60 `[ב]`; 61 `[בסעודות]`; 62 `[מזוזה]`; 68 `במשתא[ות האנשים?]`; 69 `[האד]ון`, `⟨בני⟩`; 70 `גדולת[ו]`; 82 `[הרב]ים`; 84 `[כ]ל`; 85 `[יר]עה`; 86 `המע[מ]קים`; 87 `[דו]כן`; 99 `[נָזִי־]מָרוּתַּשׁ`; 113 `[מי אשר תלאה]`; 118 `[טוב בשר]`); parenthesized normalization words not translated (31 none, 36 *(anni)*, 40 *(u)*, 84 *(-ma)*, 102 *(u)*); additions in `( )`: 13 `(מידו)`, 27 `(ב)`, 42 `(אליי)`, 44 `(לי)`, 56 `(זו לזו)`, 57 `(צאן)`, 73 `(מ)`, 83 `(להם)`, 84 `(על)`, 106 `(שמו)`; the gate glosses in `("…")`.
- Pass (b) consistency: every shared lemma checked against I–IV (`אדוני`, `הרגיע`, `פטר`, `החיה`, `שחת`, `אסון`, `אחז את ידי`, `הגביה את ראשה/ראשי`, `מחץ`, `השליך`, `השמח לאידי`, `אוי`, `רחוב`, `כיכר`, `כבה`, `אֶרְכַּלָּה`, `נמנה`, `נבלה`, `טיהר`, `ניקה`, `תחינה`, `תחנון`, `קבר`, `שוב`, `נגש`, `אלת־מגן`, `חוב`/`פטר` (I 24), `אנחה`, `סעודה`, `נסך`, `כָּבֵד`, `נהר`, `גילה`, `גדולה`, `שִׁמְשׁוֹ`, `מיתה`, `ירך`, `הרבים`, `הלל`, `גאות`, `הנופל`, `רחמים`, `חסד`, `עוון`, `יגיע`, `טוב`); collisions found and avoided: `גור` (Gilgameš *mīrānu*) → *gerru* `כפיר`; `מחסום` (*napraku*) → *napsamu* `רסן`; `שלח` (*šapāru*) → *wašāru* `התיר`; `רדף` (*redû*) → *ṭarādu* `שיגר`; `הגיש` (*ṭeḫû* D) → *maḫāru* Š `הקריב`; `מעמקים` (*nagbu*, 86) → *tēmīqu* `עתירה`; `כון` (*kânu*, 83) → *anna* `הן`; `החליף` (would read "exchange") → *etēqu* Š `העביר`; `תפילה` / `תחנון` / `שוועה` / `התחנן` → *utnēnu* `התפלל`. Reserved roots by hand: no `שאול` (*erkallu* transliterated; 1 Sam 2:6 tagged), no `עפר` / `טיט` (80 *kerṣu* `קריצה`), no `גואל` (Ps 103:4 only in the apparatus), no `עולם`; `שוב` only for *târu* (41, 102), `קרא` only for *nabû* (106).
- Pass (c) Hebrew: read the generated translation; vocalized the ambiguous forms (`הֵעִירַנִי`, `מְנִיסִי`, `הֶחֱזִיר`, `הַבּוֹכֶה` / `הַבּוֹכָה`, `לוּלֵא`, `הִתִּירַנִי`, `כָּבְתָה`, `הֵן`, `שׁוּגַּרְתִּי`, `לְאִטּוּת`, `עֲזָבַנִי`, `אַסַּכּוּ`, the gate names, `שַׁלְמוֹן הוּפְקַד`, `הוּזֵּיתִי`, `שָׁאַל פִּי`, `רַגְלֵי`, `הִצַּתִּי`, `שׁוֹחַד`, `אַנְגוּבּוּ`, `הִנְהַרְתִּי`, `דְּשֵׁנוֹת`, `הֶעֱלַצְתִּי`, `חַשׁוּרוּ`, `שִׁמְשׁוֹ`, `רְאוּיָה`, `שׂוּמָה`, `גִּירָה מְנוּפָּח`, `שׂוּמֵי`, `הַלְּלוּ`, `נַנָּרוּ`, `בּוּרְסַגֵּיהֶן`, `נָזִי־מָרוּתַּשׁ`, `יִיקָּרֵא`, `יִשְׂבַּע`, `שׁוּמֶר`, `מְצַוֵּה`, `יִירָגַע`, `תְּכַבְּדֵהוּ`, `שִׂמְחַת`, `הִלְּלוּ`, `הִילּוּלָיו`).
- **Tool fix (minimal, reported):** `wisdom_tools.py` RESERVED, the `שלם` check: added *šulmān* (V 44 *šulmānu*, V 116 *šulmāniš* – the cognate) and *ka-silim* to the allowed prefixes, and the check now also tests the allowed prefixes against the line's normalization tokens (one line in `verify`), because eBL does not lemmatize the gate name *ka-silima* (V 44, 52), whose meaning "peace, well-being" is the licensing word of the gloss `("שער השלום")`. Without it `verify --strict` reported three false positives; `../renderings.md` §8 says to widen the allow-list rather than bend the Hebrew.
- `check_bible_quotes.py` on annotated-IV/V, translation--intro-IV/V, translation-IV/V and this section: the first run found 7 problems in V (ketiv/qere words in Ps 30:4 and Job 33:28, references without the book name, one quotation spanning two verses) – fixed → 0. `wisdom_tools.py build` (IV 53 lines, 11 footnotes; V 120 lines, 18 footnotes); `verify IV V --strict`: no problems.
