| V 54–55 | *utennēn … qutrinnī* | התפללתי … קטורות | `תִּכּוֹן תְּפִלָּתִי קְטֹרֶת לְפָנֶיךָ` (תהלים קמא:ב) | equivalent | Prayer and incense. |
| V 55 | *qutrinnī ṭābūti … ušaṣli* | קטורות טובות … הִצַּתִּי | `עֹלוֹת מֵיחִים אַעֲלֶה-לָּךְ עִם-קְטֹרֶת אֵילִים` (תהלים סו:טו) | cognate | *qutrinnu* = `קטורת` [cognate]; the votive thanksgiving with incense (Ps 66:13–15); *ṭābu* = `טוב` [cognate]. |
| V 56 | *ušamḫir erba ṭaʾta igisê* | הקרבתי הכנסה, שׁוֹחַד, תשורות | `אִם עַל תּוֹדָה יַקְרִיבֶנּוּ וְהִקְרִיב עַל זֶבַח הַתּוֹדָה` (ויקרא ז:יב) | equivalent | *maḫāru* Š "make (the god) receive" ≈ `הקריב` (by sense; open decision 7); the thanksgiving offering (Lev 7:11–15). |
| V 56 | *ṭaʾta* | שׁוֹחַד | `הָאֵל הַגָּדֹל הַגִּבֹּר וְהַנּוֹרָא אֲשֶׁר לֹא יִשָּׂא פָנִים וְלֹא יִקַּח שֹׁחַד` (דברים י:יז) | divergence | Marduk and Zarpānītu receive *ṭātu*, the gift that wins a lord's favour; the God of Deuteronomy "takes no bribe". |
| V 56 | *ṭaʾta* | שׁוֹחַד | `הִנֵּה שָׁלַחְתִּי לְךָ שֹׁחַד כֶּסֶף וְזָהָב` (מלכים א טו:יט) | equivalent | `שֹׁחַד` as a gift sent to a king = *ṭātu*'s sense ("voluntary gift to king"). |
| V 57 | *upalliq lê marî* | שחטתי פרים מריאים | `וַיִּזְבַּח שׁוֹר וּמְרִיא` (שמואל ב ו:יג) | cognate | *marû* = `מְרִיא` [cognate]; David's sacrifice during the ark's procession into the city. |
| V 57 | *uṭṭabbiḫ* | טבחתי | — | cognate | *ṭabāḫu* = `טבח` [cognate] (Gilgameš VIII 131 `כבשים מְרִיאִים טבח`). |
| V 57 | *upalliq … uṭṭabbiḫ* | שחטתי … טבחתי | `לְךָ-אֶזְבַּח זֶבַח תּוֹדָה` (תהלים קטז:יז) | equivalent | The thanksgiving sacrifice of one saved from death; also Ps 107:22, Jonah 2:10. |
| V 57 | *upalliq … uṭṭabbiḫ* | שחטתי … טבחתי | `אָבוֹא בֵיתְךָ בְעוֹלוֹת; אֲשַׁלֵּם לְךָ נְדָרָי` (תהלים סו:יג) | equivalent | Entering the house to pay vows (cf. I 1, the vow to praise). |
| V 57 | *upalliq … uṭṭabbiḫ* | שחטתי … טבחתי | `וַאֲנִי בְּקוֹל תּוֹדָה אֶזְבְּחָה לָּךְ אֲשֶׁר נָדַרְתִּי אֲשַׁלֵּמָה` (יונה ב:י) | equivalent | |
| V 58 | *attanaqqi kurunna duššupa* | ניסכתי וניסכתי סובא מובחר ממותק | `בַּקֹּדֶשׁ הַסֵּךְ נֶסֶךְ שֵׁכָר לַיהוָה` (במדבר כח:ז) | equivalent | A beer libation in the Torah too; *naqû* = `נסך` (renderings §2). |

| V 59 | *šēdu lamassu angubbû libitti Esagil* | רוח־המגן, אלת־המגן, האַנְגוּבּוּ, לבנת אסגיל | — | absent | Offerings to the temple's protective spirits, attendant gods and its very bricks: no biblical counterpart. *libittu* = `לבנה` [cognate]. |

| V 61 | *libbašun ušāliṣ* | את לבם הֶעֱלַצְתִּי | `עָלַץ לִבִּי בַּיהוָה` (שמואל א ב:א) | equivalent | *elēṣu* ~ `עלץ` (not a secure cognate). |
| V 61 | *mākālê deššûti* | [בסעודות] דְּשֵׁנוֹת | `יֹאכְלוּ עֲנָוִים וְיִשְׂבָּעוּ` (תהלים כב:כז) | equivalent | The thanksgiving meal; *deššû* (root of *dešû* ~ `דשא`), rendered by sense `דשנות`. |
| V 62 | *sippī šigara mēdela dalāti* | [מזוזה], בריח, מנעול, דלתות | `וַיַּעֲמִידוּ דַּלְתֹתָיו מַנְעוּלָיו וּבְרִיחָיו` (נחמיה ג:ג) | equivalent | The same parts of a gate; in Ludlul they receive offerings (Nabonidus's Ebabbar cylinder: oil for jamb, bolt, bar, doors). |
| V 62 | *sippī … dalāti* | מזוזה … דלתות | `וְהִגִּישׁוֹ אֶל הַדֶּלֶת אוֹ אֶל הַמְּזוּזָה` (שמות כא:ו) | divergence | Door and doorpost as a place of rite (the slave's ear), not as recipients of offerings. [absent] Offerings to doors and bolts themselves. |
| V 62 | *dalāti* | דלתות | `כִּי-שִׁבַּר דַּלְתוֹת נְחֹשֶׁת; וּבְרִיחֵי בַרְזֶל גִּדֵּעַ` (תהלים קז:טז) | equivalent | Doors and bolts in a thanksgiving psalm (the prisoners' release). |
| V 63 | *ella ḫimēta ṭuḫdi ašnan* | טהור, חמאה, רוב דגן | `וְיָצַק עָלֶיהָ שֶׁמֶן` (ויקרא ב:א) | equivalent | Oil (?), butter and grain; *ḫimētu* = `חמאה` [cognate]. |
| V 66 | *tarqīt ḫašurri ṭāba* | מרקחת? חַשׁוּרוּ טובה | `רֹקַח מִרְקַחַת מַעֲשֵׂה רֹקֵחַ` (שמות ל:כה) | cognate | *tarqītu* (root *raqāḫu* / *ruqqû* "prepare aromatics") ~ `רקח` [cognate]; eBL's new reading (Jiménez). |
| V 66 | *tarqīt … elīšunu* | מרקחת … עליה[ם] | `וּמָשַׁחְתָּ בוֹ אֶת אֹהֶל מוֹעֵד וְאֵת אֲרוֹן הָעֵדֻת` (שמות ל:כו) | divergence | Perfumed oil on the sanctuary and its objects; in Ludlul on the gates' parts and spirits. |
| V 67 | *qerēti mārū Bābili* | משתאות? בני בבל | `וַאֲכַלְתֶּם שָׁם לִפְנֵי יְהוָה אֱלֹהֵיכֶם וּשְׂמַחְתֶּם` (דברים יב:ז) | equivalent | The community's feast at the sanctuary. |
| V 67 | *qerēti* | משתאות | `יֹאכְלוּ עֲנָוִים וְיִשְׂבָּעוּ; יְהַלְלוּ יְהוָה דֹּרְשָׁיו` (תהלים כב:כז) | equivalent | The rescued man's thanksgiving meal with the poor (Ps 22:23–27). |
| V 69 | *īmurū-ma mārū Bābili kī uballaṭu bēlu* | ראו ⟨בני⟩ בבל איך מחיה [האד]ון | `וַיִּתֵּן בְּפִי שִׁיר חָדָשׁ תְּהִלָּה לֵאלֹהֵינוּ: יִרְאוּ רַבִּים וְיִירָאוּ` (תהלים מ:ד) | equivalent | The many see and are moved: the individual rescue becomes public testimony (and the "new song", V 119). |
| V 69 | *īmurū-ma mārū Bābili* | ראו בני בבל | `נְדָרַי לַיהוָה אֲשַׁלֵּם; נֶגְדָה-נָּא לְכָל-עַמּוֹ` (תהלים קטז:יח) | equivalent | Thanksgiving before the whole people. |
| V 69 | *īmurū-ma mārū Bābili* | ראו בני בבל | `וַיָּבֹאוּ אֵלָיו כָּל אֶחָיו וְכָל אַחְיֹתָיו וְכָל יֹדְעָיו לְפָנִים וַיֹּאכְלוּ עִמּוֹ לֶחֶם בְּבֵיתוֹ` (איוב מב:יא) | divergence | Job's restoration ends with kin and acquaintances eating with him; Ludlul's with the whole city praising the god. |
| V 70 | *pâtu kalîšina ušāpâ narbîšu* | הפיות, כולן, גילו את גדולת[ו] | `וֶעֱזוּז נוֹרְאֹתֶיךָ יֹאמֵרוּ` (תהלים קמה:ו) | equivalent | *wapû* Š = `גילה` (I 37: the vow to "make manifest" fulfilled by all mouths). |
| V 71 | *mannum-ma iqbi amār šamšīšu* | מי זה אמר: לראות את שִׁמְשׁוֹ? | `בָּאוֹר תִּרְאֶה` (איוב לג:כח) | equivalent | Elihu: the soul redeemed from the pit "sees the light". The pun *amār šamšīšu* ~ AMAR.UTU (Marduk) (Hurowitz). |

| V 72 | *etēq sūqīšu* | לחלוף ברחובו | `אֶתְהַלֵּךְ לִפְנֵי יְהוָה בְּאַרְצוֹת הַחַיִּים` (תהלים קטז:ט) | equivalent | Walking the street as recovery (IV c+16; reversal of I 80). |

| V 75 | *Marduk ina qabri bulluṭa ileʾʾe* | מרדוך – מן הקבר להחיות יוכל | `אֲנִי אָמִית וַאֲחַיֶּה מָחַצְתִּי וַאֲנִי אֶרְפָּא` (דברים לב:לט) | equivalent | The god who kills and revives, strikes and heals (V 4, 10, 75); *qabru* = `קבר` [cognate]. Revival from the brink of death, not resurrection. |
| V 75 | *ina qabri bulluṭa* | מן הקבר להחיות | `יְהוָה מֵמִית וּמְחַיֶּה; מוֹרִיד שְׁאוֹל וַיָּעַל` (שמואל א ב:ו) | equivalent | Hannah's song. |
| V 75 | *ina qabri bulluṭa* | מן הקבר להחיות | `לֹא-אָמוּת כִּי-אֶחְיֶה; וַאֲסַפֵּר מַעֲשֵׂי יָהּ` (תהלים קיח:יז) | equivalent | Living in order to tell (cf. V 69–70). |
| V 75 | *ina qabri bulluṭa* | מן הקבר להחיות | `יַסֹּר יִסְּרַנִּי יָּהּ; וְלַמָּוֶת לֹא נְתָנָנִי` (תהלים קיח:יח) | equivalent | Chastised but not given to death – Ludlul's theology in one verse. |
| V 76 | *ina karašê eṭēra amrat* | מן האסון לחלץ רְאוּיָה | `כִּי חִלַּצְתָּ נַפְשִׁי מִמָּוֶת` (תהלים קטז:ח) | equivalent | *eṭēru* = `חלץ` (renderings §2). |

| V 79 | *mû illakū iziqqu šāru* | המים הולכים, נושבת הרוח | `אֵשׁ וּבָרָד שֶׁלֶג וְקִיטוֹר; רוּחַ סְעָרָה עֹשָׂה דְבָרוֹ` (תהלים קמח:ח) | equivalent | Fire (78 *Girra*), wind and water among the elements of praise. |

| V 80 | *šūt Aruru ikruṣu keressin* | אשר אֲרוּרוּ קרצה את קריצתן | `וַיִּיצֶר יְהוָה אֱלֹהִים אֶת הָאָדָם עָפָר מִן הָאֲדָמָה וַיִּפַּח בְּאַפָּיו נִשְׁמַת חַיִּים; וַיְהִי הָאָדָם לְנֶפֶשׁ חַיָּה` (בראשית ב:ז) | divergence | A goddess pinches off clay for every human being (Gilg. I 100: Aruru and Enkidu); Genesis: God forms one man of dust and breathes into him. `עפר` / `טיט` not used (*kerṣu*). |

| V 81 | *šakittu napšata* | שׂוּמֵי נפש | `כֹּל אֲשֶׁר נִשְׁמַת רוּחַ חַיִּים בְּאַפָּיו` (בראשית ז:כב) | equivalent | All that breathes. |
| V 82 | *apâtu mala bašâ Marduk dullā* | [הרב]ים, כל אשר ישנם – את מרדוך פארו! | `כֹּל הַנְּשָׁמָה תְּהַלֵּל יָהּ: הַלְלוּ-יָהּ` (תהלים קנ:ו) | equivalent | Universal call to praise; *dalālu* = `הלל` (I 1 `אֲהַלֵּל`: the private vow becomes the call to humankind); not cognate. |
| V 82 | *apâtu … dullā* | הרבים … פארו | `הַלְלוּ אֶת-יְהוָה כָּל-גּוֹיִם; שַׁבְּחוּהוּ כָּל-הָאֻמִּים` (תהלים קיז:א) | equivalent | |
| V 84 | *kal nišī libēl* | (על) [כ]ל האנשים ימשול | `וְיֵרְדְּ מִיָּם עַד-יָם; וּמִנָּהָר עַד-אַפְסֵי-אָרֶץ` (תהלים עב:ח) | equivalent | Blessing of universal rule (god or king). |
| V 85 | *lirʾē kal dadmē* | [יר]עה את כל היישובים | `וַהֲקִמֹתִי עֲלֵיהֶם רֹעֶה אֶחָד וְרָעָה אֶתְהֶן` (יחזקאל לד:כג) | cognate | *reʾû* = `רעה` [cognate]; the shepherd-king. |
| V 86 | *mīlu ina nagbi* | הגאות מן המע[מ]קים | `בִּרְכֹת שָׁמַיִם מֵעָל בִּרְכֹת תְּהוֹם רֹבֶצֶת תָּחַת` (בראשית מט:כה) | equivalent | Fertility from the deep (II 56: the flood that brought the chill). |
| V 89 | *uriṣṣā maqta* | עזרו? את הנופל? | `סוֹמֵךְ יְהוָה לְכָל-הַנֹּפְלִים; וְזוֹקֵף לְכָל-הַכְּפוּפִים` (תהלים קמה:יד) | equivalent | eBL's new reading; *maqtu* = `הנופל` (I 14). |
| V 95 | *ūmī palêšu* | ימי שלטונו | `חַיִּים שָׁאַל מִמְּךָ נָתַתָּה לּוֹ; אֹרֶךְ יָמִים עוֹלָם וָעֶד` (תהלים כא:ה) | equivalent | Long reign for the king. |
| V 97 | *lištēlipū šuršūšu* | ישתרגו? שורשיו | `וְהָיָה כְּעֵץ שָׁתוּל עַל מַיִם וְעַל יוּבַל יְשַׁלַּח שָׁרָשָׁיו` (ירמיהו יז:ח) | cognate | *šuršu* = `שֹׁרֶשׁ` [cognate]; the blessed as a tree; the verb restored. |
| V 97 | *šuršūšu* | שורשיו | `שָׁרְשִׁי פָתוּחַ אֱלֵי-מָיִם` (איוב כט:יט) | cognate | Job's memory of his blessed past. |
| V 98 | *Esagil … liṣarriš papalla* | אֵסַגִּי[ל …] … *ינביט* שתיל | `שְׁתוּלִים בְּבֵית יְהוָה; בְּחַצְרוֹת אֱלֹהֵינוּ יַפְרִיחוּ` (תהלים צב:יד) | equivalent | Planted in the god's house, flourishing in its courts (Foster restores "in the shadow of Esagil"); also `צַדִּיק כַּתָּמָר יִפְרָח` (תהלים צב:יג), `דְּשֵׁנִים וְרַעֲנַנִּים יִהְיוּ` (תהלים צב:טו). |

### The text on its own terms
- **Thanksgiving as a fulfilled vow** (1–8, 82, 119–120): the poem opened with *ludlul* "I will praise" (I 1), the closing formula of incantation prayers; Tablet V delivers it – first four terse lines "my lord – me" (relieved, bandaged, released, revived), then the public praise of all mouths and of "all who exist", and finally "praising you is sweet". The individual vow becomes a song that the community sings (119).
- **Death as a place with a geography** (5–8, 30–36): the pit, the grave, "disaster", the river Ḫubur, the netherworld *erkallu*; death is a descent and a state – one "goes to ghosthood" (*eṭemmūtu*), is "counted as flesh for the *asakku* demon", "goes as a corpse". The refrain *anni* "yes!" makes the near-death a dramatic dialogue of the sufferer with himself.
- **The funeral already under way** (18–22): the grave-digger with his shovel, the male mourner with the flint knife for cutting himself, the female keener, the male and female gloaters – the social machinery of death, each stopped by Marduk. The grave-digger line is a stock motif already in the Ugarit hymn to Marduk (13th c.): Babylonian thanksgiving had a repertory older than Ludlul. The shovel is also Marduk's own emblem.
- **The god's two hands** (9–11): Marduk "took my hand", "struck me with the right", "raised my head with the left": the blow that wakes and the hand that lifts are one gesture (Lenzi: the poem's divine-human dynamic in three lines).
- **Purification before entry** (37–40): the matted hair of mourning washed, a ritual bath (*rimku*, the name of the royal purification series *Bīt rimki*), "renewal" (*tēdištu*, the word for renewing divine statues), prayer and "nose-stroking" – the returning man is treated like a refurbished cult image before he enters the temple.
- **The temple as a sequence of gifts** (41–53): twelve gates of Esagil, each named in Sumerian, each giving what its name says – abundance, protective goddess, well-being, life, release of guilt, purifying water, release of sighing, reconciliation – read through the scholars' sign-value hermeneutics (KA = mouth, TAR = ask, DU₈ = release; Lenzi 2015b). The procession runs from the "Gate of Sunrise" (the sun coming up from the netherworld = the man come back from the grave) to the kiss on Zarpānītu's feet. The order of the gates differs between manuscripts (Babylon vs Aššur vs Kiš): the sequence may have been adapted to local knowledge of the temple.
- **The ritual sequence and the offering list** (54–66): prayer, incense, presentation of income, "bribe" and gifts, slaughter of fattened bulls and fine sheep, repeated libations of sweetened beer and pure wine, libations and meals for the protective spirits, the divine attendants and the very bricks of Esagil, and oil, butter and grain for the door-jambs, bolts, locks and doors – the temple building itself is a living recipient of gratitude (cf. Nabonidus's Ebabbar cylinder).
- **The Babylonians as a community of witnesses** (67–76): a feast for the citizens, a hall built at Marduk's command; "the Babylonians saw how the lord revives", "who would have said he would see his sun? walk his street?" – the healed man is proof of the god's power to the city, and the poem shifts to the third person: we now see him from outside (Foster). The questions answer the opening hymn (I 35–38) word for word.
- **Marduk and Zarpānītu as a couple** (29, 52–53, 73–76, 104): every statement about the god has its pair about the goddess – he revives from the grave, she rescues from disaster; "Marduk's mercy (*rēmu*, also 'womb')" stands beside her. Personal religion's god-and-goddess pair (I 43–44) is mirrored at the top of the pantheon.
- **Cosmic praise and the clay of humanity** (77–82): wherever there is earth and sky, sun and fire, water and wind, all that Aruru pinched from clay, all that breathes and walks, all humankind – "praise Marduk!". Creation is invoked not as a story but as the extent of the audience.
- **The king and the man** (91–112): broken lines bless a king – long reign, roots and shoots "in Esagil", rule over "the black-headed" – and name Nazi-Maruttaš and Šubši-mešrê-Šakkan "of Sumer and Akkad, the land's governor". The poem ends where courtly life is restored: the king who once turned away (I 55–58) is blessed, and the courtier honoured by god, goddess and king (115–117).
- **The universal close** (113–120): "whoever has seen distress – may his sin be released, his weariness relieved"; the story of one man becomes a blessing for every sufferer, and the last words address Marduk: "praising you is sweet".
- **The ancient readers:** the Nineveh commentary explains *gerru* "lion", *napsamu* "horse's bit", *assukku* "stone", *aṣpu* in its Assyrian form, *kiḫullû* "mourning rite", and *lūman* = "now"; a Late Babylonian scholar in Uruk quotes V 17 as a proof text for the rare word *assukku*; schoolboys in Sippar and Kiš copied the gate passage.

### Lines shared with other files
- **Reversals of I–II (same Hebrew):** 9 ← II 112 (`את ידי (לא) אחז`); 11 ← II 5 (`הגביה את ראש`); 21–22 ← II 117–118 (`השמח` / `השמחה לאידי`); 60 ← II 118 (`נהרה כְּבֵדָהּ` → `את כְּבֵדָם הִנְהַרְתִּי`); 18–20 ← II 114–115 (grave and weeping); 43 ← I 15, 46, III 32 (`אלת־מגן`, `נגשה`); 48 ← I 24 (`חוב … נפטרו`); 42 ← II 50 (`נתיישר`); 51 ← II 95 (`אנחה`); 72 ← I 80, IV c+16 (street); 73–74 ← I 35–36, 38 (`שלא … מי`, `מבלעדי … איזו`, `החיה (מ)מיתה`); 82, 119 ← I 1 (`הלל`); 115–117 ← I 9, 33, III 1 (`כבד`), I 55–58 (the king); 47 ← I 49, 51 (signs); 70 ← I 37, II 7, 110 (`גילה`); 1 ← I 35, III 87 (`הרגיע`); 2 ← I 22, III 44–45 (`תחבושת` → `חבש`); 7 ← I 14 (`אסון`); 89 ← I 14 (`הנופל`); 32 ← II 47 (`אֶרְכַּלָּה`); 36 ← II 44 (`נבלה`); 81 ← II 42 (`פתיחת ירך`); 82 ← II 38 (`הרבים`); 86 ← II 56 (`גאות`).
- **From IV:** *maruštu* `תלאה` (IV d+8 → V 113); *amāru* "see" (IV d+6 `בידי יראה` → V 69, 113); *napardû* `נהר`; *dašāpu* `המתיק` → *duššupu* `ממותק` (58); *ruššû* (65); Esagil, Zarpānītu.
- **Gilgameš:** 9 (*dannatu* + *qāta ṣabātu*, OB Schøyen₂ 22 `בימי מצוקה יאחז בידך`); 37 (*mesû* + *malû*, XI 254 `את פרעותיו במים – כטהור ירחץ`); 57 (*marû* + *ṭabāḫu*, VIII 131); 80 (Aruru + *karāṣu*, I 100). eBL's "cf." links (78, 93, 98, 107) point outside the corpora; 18 = RS 25.460 43′ (no Hebrew translation in the repository).

### Proposals for the shared renderings
1. **§1 names:** the gate names as transliterated and glossed (Decisions); Ḫubur `חֻבּוּר` confirmed; Girra (fire god) `גִּירָה`; Nazi-Maruttaš `נָזִי־מָרוּתַּשׁ`; *angubbû* `אַנְגוּבּוּ`; Sumer `שׁוּמֶר` (always vocalized), Akkad `אַכַּד`; *nannāru* `נַנָּרוּ` (as guidelines §6.7).
2. **§2 ritual / prayer, add:** *utnēnu* (noun/verb) `התפללות` / `התפלל`; *tēmīqu* `עתירה` (open decision 3); *maḫāru* Š (offering) `הקריב` (tagged); *ṣelû* Š `הצית`; *erbu* `הכנסה`; *ṭātu* `שׁוֹחַד` (open decision 4); *igisû* `תשורות`; *šulmānu* `שַׁלְמוֹן` [cognate]; *palāqu* `שחט`; *marû* `מריא` [cognate]; *šapṭu* `משובח`; *rimku* `טבילה`; *tēdištu* `חידוש`; *salāḫu* `הזה`; *tēliltu* `טהרה`; *tarqītu* `מרקחת` [cognate]; *angubbû* `אַנְגוּבּוּ`; *libittu* `לבנה`; *sippu* / *šigaru* / *mēdelu* / *daltu* `מזוזה` / `בריח` / `מנעול` / `דלת`; *qerītu* `משתה`; *bikītu* / *kiḫullû* (V 23, commentary) `בכית`; *dalīlu* `הילולים` (2026-10-23: `תִּפְאֶרֶת`); *tanittu* `תהילה`; *zamāru* "song" `זמר`.
3. **§3 body / society, add:** *ṣamādu* "bandage" `חבש` (with *ṣimdu* `תחבושת`; open decision 2); *qēbiru* `קובר` [cognate]; *bakkû* / *bakkītu* `הַבּוֹכֶה` / `הַבּוֹכָה (עליי)`; *ṣurru* `צור`; *mukaššidu* `מניס`; *māḫiṣu* `מוחץ`; *ākilu* `האוכל`; *eṭemmūtu* `אִטּוּת`; *šalamtu* `נבלה`; *kabātu* D `כיבד` [cognate]; *mānaḫtu* `יגיע`; *maruštu* `תלאה`; *mārū Bābili* `בני בבל`; *ṣalmat qaqqadi* `שחורי הראש`; *mumaʾʾeru* `מצוה`; *baʾūlātu* `נתינים`.
4. **§5 nature / images, add:** *gerru* `כפיר`; *napsamu* `רסן`; *aṣpu* `קלע`, *assukku* `אבן־קלע`; *marru* `מעדר` (flood corpus); *šuršu* `שורש` [cognate] (and III a+1 *erēnu* away from `שורש`, open decision 6); *papallu* `שתיל`; *elēpu* Št `השתרג`; *kerṣu* `קריצה`; *ḫašurru* `חַשׁוּרוּ`.
5. **Formulas (§6):** *lūman lā* `לוּלֵא` (always vocalized); the refrain *anna* `הֵן!` (always vocalized); *ša lā … mannu* / *ela … ayyu* `שלא … מי` / `מבלעדי … איזה` (I 35–36 = V 73–74); *kī uballiṭu mītūta* / *mītūtašu uballiṭ* `החיה (מ)מיתה` (I 38 = V 73); *qāta ṣabātu* `אחז את ידי` (II 112 = V 9).
6. **Always vocalize (§8), add:** the gate names; `לוּלֵא`, `הֵן`, `לְאִטּוּת`, `שׁוּגַּרְתִּי`, `עֲזָבַנִי`, `הַבּוֹכֶה` / `הַבּוֹכָה`, `מְנִיסִי`, `רְאוּיָה`, `גִּירָה`, `הַלְּלוּ` / `הִלְּלוּ` (vs "these"), `יִשְׂבַּע` (vs "will swear"), `שִׁמְשׁוֹ`, `שׁוֹחַד`, `שַׁלְמוֹן`, `שׁוּמֶר`, `מְצַוֵּה`.
7. **verify:** keep the `šulmān` / `ka-silim` allow-list entries and the normalization-token test (see the Progress log); consider the same for other unlemmatized Sumerian names that license a gloss.

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **A pun across languages needs a rendering method fixed before the first instance:** twelve gate names whose Sumerian elements predict the Akkadian gift (in Esagil's gates) could be shown only by a gloss inside the line, worded with the Hebrew of the gift; the method was decided once, written in the file header, and applied to all twelve (same name, same gloss). The reserved-word check then needed the gate name (unlemmatized by eBL) to license `שלום` – check such cases in the tool before drafting.
- **A reversal tablet should be drafted with a table of the earlier tablet's Hebrew beside it**, not only the lemma index: twelve lines of V answer I–II by the same lemma and syntax (the hand, the head, the gloaters, the liver, the street, the god's "who but …?"), and keeping the earlier Hebrew strings (not only the words) made the answers audible.
- **Particles can be cognate even where content words are not:** *lūman lā* and Hebrew `לוּלֵא` are built the same way (*lū*/`לוּ` + negation); rendering the particle by its cognate exposes a structural parallel (Ps 94:17, 124:1–3) that English translations ("had it not been") hide.
- **A stock line shared with an older text (Ugarit RS 25.460 = V 18) belongs in the parallels index even without a biblical counterpart:** it shows that Babylonian thanksgiving had a fixed repertory, which matters for any claim that a biblical psalm "borrowed" from Ludlul.
